> **Implements Pillar**: P1 The World Never Lies (primary — the deck and every position on it are physical facts: the one rectangle set Layout hands out stops the foot box, with no inflated margin and no "perceived" variant, and one integer `pos_curr` is what the Emitter, Suit, camera and every Presenter read); P4 Sanity Rewrites Reality (a constraint, not a feature — Body is `SIM`, never `PERCEPTION_OUT`: where a crewmate's feet are is the one value every player and every hallucination class must agree on, the human channel of truth); P5 Deeper Is Richer, Loss Is Not Total (a restore returns every actor to the same pixel and the same sub-pixel residue; a cut costs a section and a distance, never the truth of a position). **Serves**: P2 Dread Before Chaos (the floor never delivers a tell and never delivers a shock — no push, lurch, tilt, knockback or unannounced displacement of any kind); P3 The Web Watches (supplies the actor positions the stimulus owners read, owns no stimulus and adds no Attention input). Anti-pillars honoured by omission: no combat (nothing in Body can displace another actor), no PvP, no freeform builder (`heading_q` locked 0, weld legality takes no pose input), no authored story, no win state.

## Summary

Ship Body & Interior Physics decides where every crew member is standing on the ship's deck and how a ship that moves through the void can be joined to another or cut in two without the floor ever moving under anyone's feet. It keeps each person's position as a whole-pixel number in the ship's own frame, stops them exactly at the wall footprints the level data gives it, and re-expresses those positions by plain arithmetic when Assembly welds a wreck on, cuts a section off, or the pod hops across. The player never sees it as a mechanic; they feel it as footing, a deck that stays put no matter what the web does outside.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer` (designed), `Ship Section & Layout Data Model` (designed), `Player Controller & Input` (designed), `Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera` (designed); downstream, `Modular Ship Assembly` and `Web Map & Transit` are now designed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was "undesigned (provisional)")*

## Overview

**The framing conflict, stated first.** The systems index describes this system as "the composite ship as one physics body with a ship-local reference frame; characters and props moving inside a moving body (the moving-platform problem); detach spawns a new body" (IDX:29). Read as a mandate to build an engine physics body per ship, that sentence is wrong for this game, and four designed GDDs have already removed engine physics from it: the Controller says "the avatar has no physics body" and moves it by a hand-rolled integer `LocomotionStep` (PCI:103, PC-14 PCI:143); Layout's rectangles are simulation data "never produced by an engine physics shape" and its projector builds no engine tile collision and no navigation mesh in the MVP (LY-7, LY-31); the Emitter's occlusion is an integer segment-versus-rectangle test and "physics-derived observations never enter this block" (EM-30); and the Pipeline draws the interior in the avatar's body frame at the canvas origin, where "ship motion through the void never appears in the interior view" and "ship rotation is never rendered inside" (PIPE C2). The consequence, which this GDD adopts as its first rule (BDY-2, BDY-3): **in the MVP the moving-platform problem is a data problem, not an engine problem.** Every actor's position is an integer in its body's own frame; the composite welded ship is ONE body because Layout holds exactly one `body_id` per welded set (LY-18); a body that moves through the exterior therefore cannot move anything standing inside it, and a weld, a cut or a pod hop is integer arithmetic on stored positions. *(orchestrator ruling R1: option (a), an integer kinematic frame with a pose-invariant interior; options (b) one engine rigid body per ship and (c) an engine-physics exterior hybrid are rejected, the second surviving only as the presentation-only ADR-BODY-05 candidate.)*

**What it is, in one sentence.** Ship Body & Interior Physics is the single owner of *where each actor is inside its body* and *how each body sits in the exterior frame*: a scene-tree-free, integer-only `SIM` block (`body.state`) that holds every actor's body-frame kinematic state and every body's sparse pose and velocity, one per-tick hook (`body.integrate`) that runs the pure `LocomotionStep` sweep against Layout's rectangles, seven mutators that re-home actors when Assembly welds, cuts or Transit hops a pod, or set a body's exterior velocity or pose, a handful of pure reads, and one `PROJECTOR` subscriber that owns the per-body scene root and the camera-limit rectangle. Layout owns the geometry and the `body_id`; the Controller owns intent, octant, gait and the chest point; the Pipeline owns frame composition; Assembly and Transit own the commands that move actors between bodies. Body owns the one thing none of them do: the integer position of a person on a deck, and the arithmetic that keeps it true when the deck is joined to another or cut in two.

**Who owns what (closes the kernel's and the Controller's open boundary).** *Ship Body owns ship-local integer position* — `pos_curr`, `sub`, `last_octant`, `odometer`, `blocked`, the foot box, the sweep, the body transfers and the `LocomotionStep` — *and the Controller owns intent, octant, gait, `v_eff`, committed aim, facing, locomotion state, carry and channel references, the chest point and the hand point.* This closes kernel Open Question 3 (KRN:194, KRN:958) and the Controller's PC-14 recommendation (PCI:143, PCI:1108), which both asked for exactly this split. Body reads `octant` and `v_eff` from `controller.state` and derives nothing of the Controller's (BDY-11). The lamp is **chest**-mounted and hands-free (user decision): if this GDD names a beam origin at all it is the chest point, which the Controller publishes; the foot origin Body owns is the shadow and occluder origin (LO-10); the hand point is a lightless tool grip and nothing in Body touches it.

**How the player meets it.** Never as a menu, a gauge, a velocity readout or a mechanic. The player meets Body as *footing*: a deck that stops exactly where the 24-px wall footprint says it stops (`WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH`), a stop that is pixel-exact with no overshoot, a wall that is slid along rather than stuck to, and — at the two moments the game is most violent on paper — a weld and a cut during which nothing at all happens to the floor under their boots. Ship motion, docking contact and hull separation are events out in the exterior layer and the sound of the hull; they are never forces on the interior (BDY-28). The only things the player *does* that reach Body are walking and the Assembly and Transit verbs that Body's mutators serve, and each lands in `body.state` as an atomic, replayable change.

**What the game loses without it.** Nothing else owns the integer position of a person on a deck. Without Body, `LocomotionStep` has no home and the Controller's 12 sweep tests (PC-18) have no module; Suit's room lookup (SE-3) has no `section_id` to supply; the Emitter's ACTOR anchor (EM-21) and the lamp's shadow origin (LO-10) have no `pos_curr` to read, and `emitter.state`'s `restore_after: [layout.state, graph.state, body.state, controller.state]` (EM-1) names a block that does not exist, which fails boot; Save/Load's mandatory set (SL:288) has no `body.state` and the kernel's restore-idempotence debt (`REBASE_BLOCKS`, SL:577) stays open; a weld or cut would leave every actor standing in a frame that no longer exists; and the weld spike — the riskiest MVP task, the reason this system was added to the index as a `TD-SYSTEM-BOUNDARY` addition (IDX:65, IDX:276) — would have no owner for "the motion". Because the spike is *weld on a moving ship*, the GDD's answer is that welding is safe by construction: weld legality takes no pose input (BDY-27), and the interior never reads the pose.

**Scope guard: what Body does not own.** Geometry, `body_id` allocation, membership, offsets, welds, sockets, seams, hatch states and door leaves are Layout's (LY-18, LY-35); Body owns *pose and actors only*. **No prop is a Body actor in the MVP** (BDY-4): loose items are Interaction records in section-local pixels, so "a weld or a detach moves nothing" (IA-28, LY-22); static props and machinery are Layout rectangles; a thrown glowstick lands by an analytic rule (IA-29); a horror's footprint is a Graph trail, never a Body fact. The index's word "props" is therefore satisfied by a statement, not a mechanism; a Vertical Slice movable prop adds one `kind` here and one bump-list entry in Layout deliberately (LY:229). Intent, gait and the chest point belong to the Controller; camera, zoom, the cut rule and the exterior/transit layers to the Pipeline; weld and cut commands, weld quality `q` and the order of mutators to Assembly and the Graph; the loaded set, the pod leg and the docking script to Web Map & Transit; atmosphere and damage to the Life-Support Graph; sanity and perceived values to Sanity and the Perception Filter. There is no HUD element, no overlay and no number that the player reads from Body. **MVP slice.** In: SEAT actors only (one seat, `MAX_ACTORS` 8); `LocomotionStep` and the sweep; BM1–BM7; weld (BM3) and cut (BM4) on the home-ship 5 / derelict A 4 / derelict B 5 compartment slice; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, X1: the weld, the player seam cut and the install are all MVP, so BM3 and BM4 serving a player-initiated cut carry no cut-first status; co-op stays out of the MVP)* the pod hop (BM5) and a constant-velocity pod leg (BM6); restore idempotence; `body.projector` with rotation 0. Later (Vertical Slice): AGENT rows for horrors (if Horror picks that path), `DETACH_DRIFT_PX_PER_TICK` > 0, sub-pixel exterior velocity, cross-body light, co-op replication (VS-B), presentation-only debris (ADR-BODY-05), actor soft separation.

**Noun guard.** In concept prose "a body they assembled" means the composite ship. In this GDD **`body` / `body_id` is the rigid frame of a welded set** — Layout's id (LY-18), the thing that has a pose — and **"the avatar" or "an actor" is the person standing in it**. Internal nouns (`body`, `body_id`, `pos_curr`, frame, pose, velocity, transfer) are correct in rules and formulas and are never what the player experiences (see Player Fantasy, Language). The word "body" never becomes flesh: flesh tones and violet are reserved for sanity leaks (P1 sane palette), so no Body-originated cue may use them.

**Engine statement (which 2D physics path this relies on).** *None.* The only 2D physics server in Godot 4.7.2 is Godot Physics 2D — **verified-from-docs**: `docs/engine-reference/godot/modules/physics.md` (4.6 changes) records "2D physics UNCHANGED (still Godot Physics 2D)", and `VERSION.md` shows Jolt as the 3D default since 4.6, which does not apply to 2D. Body uses that server for nothing on any simulation-authoritative path (BDY-3). The single candidate use is presentation-only debris at Vertical Slice (ADR-BODY-05), excluded from the simulation hash set (`ALL_SIM`). Because nothing is sampled from a physics step, the kernel's determinism limit G3 ("Godot Physics 2D makes no determinism claim, so G3 either records and replays [physics results] or excludes physics-coupled blocks", KRN:123, CR-13) is met by construction: `body.state` is a pure function of the command stream and Layout, kernel Open Question 5 is moot for the MVP, and the kernel row "hook sampling physics results into its block each tick" (KRN:198) collapses to `body.integrate` with nothing sampled (wording note routed in Cross-References). Everything this GDD says about `_physics_process` ordering, 2D interpolation, `TileMapLayer`, `Node.reparent` and canvas float precision is **[UNV]** (GDScript integer-division truncation and `posmod` were probed 2026-10-28 on the 4.7.2 editor binary, ADR-0010 P11; the release-export repeat is LINT-SPIKE-1) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0010 sync)*: the engine reference has no `scripting.md`, `core.md`, `2d.md` or `tilemap.md` (its `modules/` holds animation, audio, input, navigation, networking, physics, rendering and ui only), and those claims are routed to the weld and motion spikes. Post-cutoff claims used here are tagged verified-from-docs against `breaking-changes.md` (4.6 to 4.7 packed-array element writes no longer call the property setter; typed-return overrides need an explicit `return` on every path).

**Index wording, reconciled.** "The composite ship as one physics body with a ship-local reference frame" = one `body_id` per welded set (Layout) plus one sparse pose row per `body_id` (Body), where the *ship-local frame* is Layout's body frame (LY-3 (b)) and the frame's origin is the point Body locates in the exterior frame. "Characters and props moving inside a moving body (the moving-platform problem)" = BDY-2: positions are body-frame integers and nothing keyed on one reads the pose; props: BDY-4. "Detach spawns a new body" = Layout allocates the new `body_id` and returns `{new_body_id, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px}` (LY-17); Body creates the pose row from the old body's and re-homes the actors on the new side (BDY-23). "Physics PROJECTOR" (KRN:198) = `body.projector`, which builds nothing physical (BDY-33).

*Implementation questions raised while designing and deferred to ADRs (→ `/architecture-decision`): the motion model and sweep formulation, hand-rolled integer kinematics against any engine body (ADR-BODY-01); the `body.state` shape, the persisted-versus-derived split and restore idempotence (ADR-BODY-02); the scene projection of a body, reparent-on-weld against rebuild, shared with ADR-LAY-03, and the camera-limit rectangle (ADR-BODY-03); the tick source and interpolation clock, shared with the kernel's tick ADR (ADR-BODY-04); and whether presentation-only debris may use engine physics at Vertical Slice (ADR-BODY-05).*

## Player Fantasy

**Framing: indirect.** The player never sees a body, a frame, a pose, a velocity or a transfer; they stand on what Body makes true. A direct framing would need a direct interaction, and the only candidates — steering the ship, bracing against it, physically shoving through it — are each forbidden below. *(creative-director consulted; three framings weighed and logged: **A "The Deck Is the Ground"** adopted as the headline; **B "Where Someone Is, Is Never a Lie"** adopted as its Pillar 4 guarantee rather than a competing headline, because the Controller already owns "a body tells the truth about its owner" (PCI:36) and Layout owns "The Wall Is the Last Witness" (LY:41); **C "The Helm"** rejected — nobody steers in the MVP, and a pilot fantasy breeds a helm, a velocity readout, inertia and brace mechanics, every one a violation of BDY-28.)* The structure mirrors Layout's headline-plus-guarantee so the two GDDs read as one promise from two sides: Layout says *the walls never move when you weld*; Body says *you never move when you weld*. Layout's own hand-off is honoured here: "you are standing where you stood" after a weld "is Body's and the Pipeline's to deliver, not a Layout rule" (LY:66).

> Outside, the ship is a vehicle. It drifts along a strand of the web, takes wrecks onto itself and sheds them. Inside, it is the world. The floor does not tilt when the pod leaves, does not shudder when a wreck is welded to the hull, does not slide when half the ship is cut away. Whatever the web does to the ship, it does out there: on the hull, in the air, through the hatch, in the dark beyond a cap plate. It never does it to your footing. *(If the view ever shudders at a docking or a detach, that is the camera's `JOLT_PX`, the Pipeline's knob, default 0, forced to 0 by reduced motion, never the floor.)*
>
> That is why the weld and the cut are the quietest moments in the game. You are standing in a wreck's corridor when the weld finishes, and nothing happens to you. The same floor is under your boots, the same doorway is in front of your light; only now this corridor is part of your ship, and the crewmate on the other side of the seam is aboard with you. When a graft goes bad and the crew cuts it away, the half you are standing on is your ship. The crewmate who was standing past the seam is on the other half. Nobody was thrown, nobody stumbled; the gap between you is a fact you now have to deal with.
>
> And under Pillar 4, where someone is cannot be faked. A crewmate may look like a horror, sound wrong, carry a light you no longer trust; the place their feet are is the place their feet are, for every player, on every frame. When the shape lies, the position does not.

**The emotion named: footing.** Not safety (the ship is never safe) and not control (the crew cannot steer it): the small, durable certainty that the ground under you is real and stays put, so that fear has to come from the world and can be read there. It is what makes the Pandorum ship-as-world credible — the void is unknowable precisely because the deck is not. MDA: Body's felt contribution is *Fantasy* ("we are the only ones awake"; a ship that is a world, not a vehicle) and *Challenge* as spatial certainty under pressure; *Fellowship* gains one concrete footing, the crewmate across a cut seam is truly *over there*. Body has no *Sensation* contribution of its own (see Visual/Audio and Game Feel). Body also deliberately contributes nothing to *Discovery*, the game's first-ranked aesthetic, and protects it only indirectly: a deck that never lies means every unknown the player investigates is in the world and not in their footing. This keeps the MDA mapping complete and stops a later revision from adding exploration mechanics (sea legs, traversal unlocks) to Body to "serve" rank 1; anti-pillar guard rail (6) below already forbids them.

**Anchor moments (MVP slice; durations are illustrative, none is a constant).**

1. **"Aboard" — the weld (primary).** Home-ship and derelict B lie hull to hull at the home-ship's one socket (LY:56). You are inside derelict B by the hatch, sweeping its dead gauges with your chest lamp; your crewmate is on the home side. The weld completes (Assembly's verb). You were on the absorbed body, so your position is re-expressed in the home-ship's frame by one translation, `frame_shift_px` (Layout `fixture_airlock`: `(576, 96)`). You feel none of it. The Pipeline rebases the camera by the same translation and keeps its lag, so you are standing where you stood on screen (PIPE C5); only where the camera bounds clamp does it make a clean cut, never a slide or a shove. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* What changed is out there — the seam bead along the join (Layout's), the sealed hatch you have not opened (Interaction's, the Graph's). What did not change is you.
2. **"The Other Half" — the cut.** The graft's engine room is a fire the crew cannot hold; the cut runs along the seam. Your crewmate is still past it, grabbing one last cell. On the cut tick each actor goes with the half that holds their foot origin (PCI:651); a box straddling the seam is not split. You stay on the home-ship; your crewmate is on the new body. Nobody is pushed. If anyone stands in the hatch passage, a *player-made* cut is refused (Assembly's `validate` through Body's `actors_overlapping`; the reason code is Assembly's): *the ship will not close a seam on a crewmate*, the same promise as the door that will not close on a foot box (LY-11, PCI:650). In the MVP the other half does not drift (`DETACH_DRIFT_PX_PER_TICK` default 0): "adrift" is a fact of membership, not of motion, until Vertical Slice.
3. **"The Hop" — the pod leg (Web Map & Transit is Designed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was "provisional — undesigned")*).** The pod is its own body (PIPE:153 (iv)). Inside the pod nothing moves; the camera is pod-local and the exterior layer slides past at constant velocity. The one MVP moment in which the ship "moves" is presented entirely outside the crew.
4. **"Same Pixel" — the restore.** A death-restart to the last autosave (user decision; Save/Load owns it) returns each actor to the pixel and sub-pixel residue the autosave recorded. The footing is restored exactly. Ship and body are what failure costs; a position is part of what the world remembers honestly. (The claim is block-level: whatever the first tick's gait looks like after a restore is the Controller's.)

The arc: wariness inside a stranger's corridor, the non-event of the weld, belonging (you are aboard), loss that is a distance rather than a blow (the other half), footing restored on failure.

**Pillar alignment (verbatim pillar text).**

| Pillar | Text served | Body's share |
|---|---|---|
| **P1 The World Never Lies (primary)** | "Pipes leak, frost forms, air hisses; the physical world is ground truth. Only instruments and perception can be wrong." Design test: "a hidden stat vs. a visibly leaking pipe -> the pipe." | The deck and the positions on it are physical facts. One rectangle set stops the foot box (Layout's `solid_in`, no inflated margin, no perceived variant, BDY-15); one `pos_curr` is read by the Emitter, Suit, camera and every Presenter. A wall that blocks the foot but not the eye (or the reverse) is a lie. |
| **P2 Dread Before Chaos (serves)** | "Every breach is preceded by a tell a careful player could have perceived ... This pillar owns tempo and presentation." | The floor never delivers the tell and never delivers the shock. No push, lurch, tilt, knockback or docking impulse (BDY-28); nothing moves an actor except `LocomotionStep` and the transfer arithmetic. A shove from the floor would be an untelegraphed event, which P2 forbids; the only relocation Body does is the whole-pixel `frame_shift_px` translation, invisible by construction. |
| **P3 The Web Watches (none)** | "Attention is shown in the world ... never as a hidden meter." | Body owns no stimulus and adds no Attention input. Stated so no later rule invents one (a "noise of a weld lurch"). |
| **P4 Sanity Rewrites Reality (guarantee — a constraint, not a feature)** | "No lie is unfalsifiable ... At least one channel of truth always remains." Human channel: "a crewmate's own light beam (colour and shape) are never faked." | No per-player and no `PERCEPTION_OUT` variant of any Body read (BDY-30). The class-(c) rig is drawn at the true position. Body supplies no lie and reveals none. |
| **P5 Deeper Is Richer, Loss Is Not Total (serves)** | "losing the home-ship or a crewmate costs ship and body, never knowledge." | A restore returns the same pixel and residue (BDY-31). A cut costs a section and a crewmate's proximity, never their position's truth. |

**Design test.** *A floor that moves the crew vs. a floor the crew moves on -> the floor the crew moves on.* It resolves live decisions in this GDD, which is what makes it a test rather than decoration:

| Live decision | Resolution | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| A transfer lands a foot box inside a SOLID rect | No push-out; the actor is never displaced and may only take sub-steps that do not deepen its overlap with any rectangle it already touches, and `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` counts each such tick on which the exemption was evaluated *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: BDY-16 counting rule)* | BDY-16, PCI:650 |
| Ship acceleration, docking contact or detach acting on actors | None; published as events only, feeding `JOLT_PX` (default 0) and Adaptive Audio | BDY-28 |
| Rotation of a body rendered inside | Never; `heading_q` locked 0 | BDY-26, PIPE C2 |
| A perceived or per-player position | None | BDY-30 |
| `sub` zeroed on restore versus on transfer | Transfer and stop only; restore preserves it | BDY-12, BDY-31 |
| Player cut with a crewmate in the hatch passage | Refused by Assembly's `validate` via `actors_overlapping` | BDY-24 |
| System-origin cut (a breach cascade) with a crewmate in the hatch passage | Deferred by Assembly while `actors_overlapping` is non-empty, and preceded by the P2 tell window (a recorded must-repeat, 8.3); on expiry the cut is abandoned, never forced; BM4 refuses as the apply-time net *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)*; Body never displaces | BDY-24 |
| An exempt actor's step would put its foot origin in no section of its body | Refused: the only exits from a hatch cap lead back onto the body's own floor, never out of the hull. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* An actor that cannot leave a cap plate is visibly inside it and reads EXPOSED (Layout answers NONE inside a footprint): the guarantee is visibility, not safety | BDY-16, D.11 |
| Interior reads of world pose or velocity | Forbidden (lint plus an invariance test) | BDY-2 |

*Secondary test (the P4 guarantee):* **a horror drawn where the crewmate is vs. a horror drawn somewhere the crewmate is not -> where the crewmate is.** It resolves BDY-30 and is a must-repeat for the Perception Filter (Designed, and it repeats BDY-30) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *We will know this was right if* playtesters describe a weld as "suddenly we were connected" and a cut as "and then they were on the other side", never "the ship jerked" or "I got knocked"; and a code review finds no write to `pos_curr` outside `LocomotionStep` and the transfer arithmetic of BM1 to BM5.

**How the fantasy deepens under hallucination (not breaks).**

- **Class (a) phantom entities.** A phantom has no Body row (BDY-4: filter-only phantoms have none; a real horror holds its own position). What deepens: the player's own footing is never part of the lie, so "did I just get grabbed?" is answerable by the fact that nothing moved you — Body never moves an actor without a cause the simulation can show.
- **Class (b) false system states.** Geometry is outside the Filter's menu: Layout asks that wall faces, door leaves and seam geometry be tagged truth-channel (LY:41, LY:47; routed to the Perception Filter as a must-repeat). Body's sweep therefore never meets a hallucinated wall, and the player's body cannot be fooled about where it can walk or which side of a cut it is on. The class-(b) lies that remain — machine states, gauges, module sprites — have no Body share.
- **Class (c) crewmates as horrors.** Deepens most. The rig is driven by the crewmate's true `pos_curr` (Body) and true motion and beam (Controller, PCI:36, PCI:216); their beam rides their chest point. A player who watches where the "monster" stands and where its light comes from has a truth channel that costs attention, not certainty — and that is the channel on which system-gated lethal friendly harm is decided, so it must not lie.
- **Class (d) phantom salvage.** No Body share (Interaction and Item records, IA-28). Stated so no later rule makes carried weight a Body velocity effect: carry speed is the Controller's `V_CARRY`, not a Body modifier.

Inherited guard rail from the Controller's secondary test ("-10% move speed vs. a gauge showing a false value -> the false gauge"): sanity never touches `body.state` — it never slows, shifts, drifts or displaces an actor.

**Language (player-facing text and this section).** *Use:* deck, floor, underfoot, footing, aboard, over the seam, across the seam, the other half, adrift, the hop, the pod leg, your ship / their ship, the wreck, standing where you stood. *Never as player-facing nouns:* physics, physics body, inertia, momentum, g-force, gravity, lurch, jolt (as prose), shove, knockback, ragdoll, platform, moving platform, collider, hitbox, rigid body, frame / reference frame / transform / pose / velocity, teleport, snap.

**Reference techniques — adopt and reject, precisely.**

| Reference | Verdict | Exactly what |
|---|---|---|
| Pandorum | ADOPT | Ship-as-world: the interior is the whole world, the outside unknowable. The chest-mounted, hands-free lamp is the user's decision; the foot is the shadow and occluder origin; the hand point is lightless. |
| Beyond the Aquila Rift | ADOPT | A still, intimate interior against an incomprehensible exterior; the horror of distance (the other half, the void past a cap plate), not of violent motion. |
| Project Zomboid | ADOPT | A physics-free, pixel-stepped avatar whose position is a plain, exact fact; "death is a bitch. It happens." (user) without the floor ever being the killer. |
| Barotrauma | ADOPT the hull-is-world framing; REJECT ragdoll, sub-collision knockback and crew thrown by impacts | Its displacement is exactly the unannounced shove P2 forbids and a lethal-friendly-harm vector the anti-PvP anti-pillar forbids. |
| Sea of Thieves (visibly moving deck), FTL-style crew sliding with hull hits | REJECT | Interior motion breaks PIPE C2 and makes the floor an actor. |
| Space Engineers, Starbase (physical ships, per-block physics) | REJECT | The freeform-builder anti-pillar, and a physics body per ship contradicts BDY-3. |
| Dead Space zero-g sections, magnetic-boot gimmicks | REJECT | The interior is inertial and walking is the only locomotion (PC-12 lists what the player cannot do). |
| Stellaris | NOT APPLICABLE | Strategy-layer inspiration belongs to Web Map; nothing to adopt for Body. |

**Anti-pillar guard rails this GDD states.** (1) *Not a combat game:* Body has no push, knockback, shove, impulse, ragdoll or stagger-from-motion, and nothing in Body can be used to displace another actor (PCI:139 PC-12). (2) *No intentional PvP:* Body offers no physical harm channel at all, sane or not, and adds no ungated one — crushing a crewmate in a cut is refused for player-origin cuts (BDY-24 (i)), deferred and never forced for system-origin cuts (BDY-24 (ii)), refused again at BM4's apply-time net (BDY-24 (iii)), and an overlap never displaces or damages (BDY-16) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)*; actors do not body-block one another (BDY-17), so no player can pin a crewmate in a corridor. (3) *Not a freeform builder:* no rotation (`heading_q` 0), no free placement, no dragging a section through space by the player; weld legality takes no pose input (BDY-27). (4) *No authored story, no win state:* no scripted interior motion, no authored "the ship lurches" moment. (5) *No meter:* no HUD element reads Body; no velocity, heading, g-force or docking-alignment readout. (6) *No progression:* no speed buff, traversal unlock or "sea legs".

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

Rule ids are `BDY-n`; formulas are `D.n` (Formulas section); acceptance criteria are `AC-BDY-n`; BM1 to BM7 are Body's mutators; `can_*` are its pure precondition reads. Locked constants and formulas from the kernel, the Graph, the Controller, the Pipeline and Layout are used by name with their registry values and are never restated differently (a needed different value would be a CONFLICT line in Open Questions, never a new number): `TICK_RATE` 60, `TILE_PX` 48, `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` 24 (owner: the art bible), `V_WALK` 2, `V_RUN` 3, `V_CARRY` 1 base px per tick, the 1/256 fixed point with its `UNIT` table (256 cardinal, 181 diagonal), `MAX_BODIES` 8, `MAX_FOOTPRINT_RECTS_PER_BODY` 2,000, `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4, `JOLT_PX` 0. Body-new constants (`FOOT_BOX_W`, `FOOT_BOX_H`, `MAX_ACTORS`, `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT`, `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK`, `DETACH_DRIFT_PX_PER_TICK`, `SWEEP_COST_LOG_MS`) are listed in Tuning Knobs and registered after this GDD is final (Cross-References); any not fixed by a ruling is tagged `[PLACEHOLDER]` (`SWEEP_COST_LOG_MS` is `[PLACEHOLDER]`, suggested 0.05 ms, the per-actor figure of PCI:1078, a logging threshold only). Contracts with undesigned or in-flight systems are marked **(provisional — <system> undesigned)** and name what that GDD must repeat. Conflicts, clarifications and asks are marked **CONFLICT**, **CLARIFY** and **ASK** and are routed in Cross-References and Open Questions, never silently resolved in a neighbour's text. Each rule closes with *Decides:* (the pillar, ruling or sibling rule it serves) and a test tag: (auto) automated, (lint) static check, (review) design review, (spike) engine spike.

#### 1.1 Identity, ownership and the no-physics-server rule

**BDY-1 — One body per welded set; Body adds pose, not geometry.** A body's identity is Layout's `body_id` (LY-18: Layout allocates it from `next_body_id`, first id 1, and owns `bodies[]`, `section_instances[].body_id`, offsets and `welds[]`). Body stores, per `body_id`, only the frame's POSE (`world_x`, `world_y`), its velocity (`vel_x`, `vel_y`) and the reserved `heading_q`, and, per actor, the actor's body-frame state. Body never allocates, retires or edits a `body_id`, section membership, offset, rectangle, socket, seam or hatch state. The composite welded ship is therefore ONE body in exactly the sense the index means: one frame, one pose row, one rectangle set (`solid_in(body_id, aabb)`), however many sections are welded into it. *Decides:* LY-18, LY-35; P1. (review) grep this GDD for `next_body_id`, `welds[]` and `offset_px` as Body-written fields: zero hits.

**BDY-2 — The interior is a pose-invariant integer frame.** Every simulation fact about the interior — a foot box, a `solid_in` rectangle, `pos_curr`, an Emitter anchor, Suit's room lookup, the interior camera pose — is a body-frame integer. **Nothing keyed on a body-frame coordinate reads `world_*` or `vel_*`.** A body that moves in the exterior moves nothing inside it, so the moving-platform problem does not exist as an engine problem; a body in motion and a body at rest produce a byte-identical interior (D.9 states the invariance; the acceptance fixture sets a non-zero `vel` on the avatar's body, runs 600 ticks and compares `canonical_encode(payload["actors"])` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*). Rotation is never a simulation fact (BDY-26). *Decides:* R1, R6, P1, PIPE C2 (PIPE:153). (lint + auto)

**BDY-3 — No Godot physics server on any simulation path.** `RigidBody2D`, `CharacterBody2D`, `StaticBody2D`, `AnimatableBody2D`, `PhysicsBody2D`, `Area2D`, `PhysicsServer2D`, `move_and_slide`, `move_and_collide`, `test_move`, `body_test_motion`, engine tile collision and navigation meshes appear in this GDD only in rejected-alternative text and in the lint criterion, which extends the Controller's lint (PCI:900) from the controller, body and presenter directories to the whole Body module and to Layout's (zero matches for that token set; zero matches in the block and the hook for `randi`, `randf`, `randomize`, `Time.`, `OS.get_ticks`, `Engine.get_physics_frames` (the one forbidden-token list for clock, frame-counter and RNG calls is ADR-0010 SIM-02, section 4.5; ADR-0001 and ADR-0005 point at it), float-typed declarations and non-integer `Vector2(`; no script under `src/sim/body/` names `Node2D`, `Camera2D` or `Viewport`; the PROJECTOR lives in `src/presentation/projector/`, ADR-0010 section 1.2) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0010 sync)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **The token grep is not sufficient on its own, because GDScript type inference can silently produce a float** (`var x := a / b` on ints is integer division, but `var y := a * 0.5` or an untyped `Variant` path is a float that no token list names). The lint therefore also **promotes the GDScript typing warnings to errors for every script under the Body and Layout directories (ADR-0010 section 5 widens this to all of `src/` and `tools/`)** — `UNTYPED_DECLARATION`, `INFERRED_DECLARATION`, `UNSAFE_*`, `NARROWING_CONVERSION`, `INTEGER_DIVISION`, `INFERENCE_ON_VARIANT` — and CI fails on any warning raised by a file in those directories (`debug/gdscript/warnings/directory_rules` exists on 4.7.2 with include/exclude decisions and one global severity per warning (probed 2026-10-28); ADR-0010 section 5 promotes every warning to an error for all of `src/` and `tools/`, which subsumes the Body and Layout directories, and the CI gate is that every script loads) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0010 sync)*. `INTEGER_DIVISION` makes every `/` on integers an error unless annotated, which leaves exactly ONE annotated `/` site in `src/`, inside `IntMath.trunc_div` (`src/shared/int_math.gd`; `floor_div` calls it, BDY-35; ADR-0010 SIM-05, PRJ-04). The 2D server that exists is Godot Physics 2D (**verified-from-docs**: `modules/physics.md`, "2D physics UNCHANGED (still Godot Physics 2D)"; Jolt is the 3D default since 4.6 and does not apply to 2D). Body relies on it for nothing; the one candidate use is presentation-only debris at Vertical Slice, excluded from `ALL_SIM` (ADR-BODY-05). *Rejected alternatives:* one `RigidBody2D` or `CharacterBody2D` per ship with engine collision shapes built from Layout (contradicts four designed GDDs, makes G3 depend on a recorded physics replay, adds an unmeasured shape rebuild per weld and per cut, and lets rotation leak inside, PIPE C2); an engine-physics exterior hybrid (a second authority for pose). *Decides:* R1, R13, KRN:123 (G3), Guard rail 3. (lint) → ADR-BODY-01, ADR-BODY-05.

**BDY-4 — Who is an actor; what is not.** An `actors` row has `kind SEAT | AGENT`. MVP rows are `SEAT` only: one per bound player seat. **A horror holds its own position**: of the two paths (keep a position in its own state block and call the pure `LocomotionStep` of BDY-10, or register `AGENT` rows here), Horror Entities chose path 1 (HE-23: position, residue and `last_octant` in `horror.state`, movement only through `LocomotionStep` with Body's foot box; consistency pass 2026-10-27), so MVP `AGENT` rows stay unused, and a horror's footprint is a Graph trail, never a Body fact. **No prop is an actor**: loose items are Interaction records in section-local pixels (IA-28, LY-22: "a weld or a detach moves nothing"); static props and machinery are Layout rectangles; a thrown glowstick lands analytically (IA-29). A Vertical Slice movable prop adds one `kind` here and one bump-list entry in Layout (LY:229) deliberately. **Orphaned bodies persist**: a body left with no actor keeps its pose row; membership and discard are Layout's, Assembly's and Web Map's (`discard_section_geometry`); Body offers `actors_on(body_id)` and `body_count()` for their decisions. Filter-only phantoms (hallucination class (a)) have no Body row. *Decides:* R9, R18, R19; P4. (review)

#### 1.2 The block

**BDY-5 — `body.state` is one SHARED `SIM` block.** Registration (CR-8, with `supported_versions` from SL-15 item 2, SL:224): `block_id body.state`; owner Ship Body; `class SIM`; `persist true`; `persist_scope WORLD`; `scope SHARED`; `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`; `schema_version 1`; `supported_versions [1]`; `restore_after: [layout.state]`; exactly one hook, `body.integrate`; exactly one `PROJECTOR` subscriber, `body.projector`. Two sparse tables: `bodies` keyed by `body_id` (Layout's id) and `actors` keyed by `actor_id`, plus the block-local counter `next_actor_id` (CR-8: entity ids are allocated by the owning block from a counter stored inside it). Body repeats what Layout asked it to repeat (LY-18): it owns `body.state` (pose, velocity, actor positions); its `restore_after` is `[layout.state]`; and it owns the physics `PROJECTOR` slot (BDY-33). *Rejected:* `PER_PLAYER` scope (bodies and pods have no PlayerId); two blocks (`body.state` and `body.frames`) — a second restore node and a second owner registration where EM-1 names one. *Decides:* R2, EM-1, SL:288; CR-8. (auto: registration lint) → ADR-BODY-02.

**BDY-6 — One position name.** The persisted canonical field is **`pos_curr`** (the name EM:426, LO:217 and SL:474 already read). `pos_prev` is DERIVED: set `:= pos_curr` in Body's own `post_restore`, at placement and at every transfer, never persisted *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix: the `post_restore` reset is a Body-internal derivation of a non-persisted field, not the camera-continuity mechanism, which is solely the kernel `snap_baseline` plus the camera CUT on a `load_seq` change, ADR-0008 § 6.1; see BDY-31)*. The Controller's `pos` (PCI:447, "`body.state.pos`") is an alias for `pos_curr`; this sentence is where the alias is declared, once (a wording note is routed to Save/Load, whose SL:474 and SL:849 list both `pos` and `pos_curr`, and to the Controller). `blocked` and every `diag` value are derived and outside the hashed set. *Decides:* R2, R3, PIPE C5, SL-15 item 8 (SL:230). (auto)

**BDY-7 — Persisted fields (schema v1).** Per actor: `{actor_id, kind, seat, body_id, pos_curr (x, y), sub (x, y), last_octant, odometer}`. Per body, sparse (a row absent means all zeros): `{body_id, world_x, world_y, vel_x, vel_y, heading_q}`. Block scalar: `next_actor_id`. `last_octant` (0-7, 8 = NONE) is **Body's own** record of the octant its last step integrated; it is a different field from `controller.state`'s decel-ladder memory of the same name (PCI:311), and this sentence is the disambiguation. `seat` is the PlayerId whose `controller.state` row feeds this actor's intent; **seat 0 means "no seat" (`AGENT` rows; any number of `AGENT` rows may carry it) and PlayerIds start at 1; two `SEAT` rows may never share a seat, and a `SEAT` row with `seat` 0 or a negative seat is `BDY_PLACE_BAD_KIND` at placement and `BDY_RESTORE_KIND` on restore** (the kernel defines a PlayerId as "a stable seat identity (1 in single-player)", KRN:52, and Suit keys by an integer PlayerId, SE-2; no designed GDD assigns PlayerId 0, and the kernel states the reservation once, KRN:52, answered *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*); it is persisted because the kernel's binding table "is not saved, since sessions rebuild it" (KRN:100), so after a restore nothing else says which `actor_id` is seat 1; Body matches an actor to its Controller row on its own `seat` field, so no kernel reverse lookup is needed. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Who rebuilds the kernel's `PlayerId → {controlled entity IDs}` binding after a load (the step KRN:100 left unowned until 2026-10-28; ANSWERED, below).** KRN:100 says the table "may be rebuilt from Body's `seat` field" and SL-20 says "a session rebuilds it", but at the design review no GDD assigned the step, and the MVP death-restart path (restore the last autosave, then play) needs it: without it a seat-1 command fails the authority stage (`ACTOR_NOT_OWNED`, KRN CR-5) after every restore. Body's half of the contract is stated here and is closed: Body publishes the pure read `seat_bindings() -> [(seat, actor_id)]` (ascending `seat`, `SEAT` rows only, derived from the actors table on each call, no persisted field) and nothing else. The other half is the kernel's *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)*: kernel CR-7 rebuilds `PlayerId -> {controlled entity IDs}` as the last step of every `LOADING` (after the last `post_restore()`, before `RUNNING`), calling Body's registered binding source `seat_bindings()` and binding each pair (KRN-BIND-01). Body registers `seat_bindings` as a binding source at BOOTING and does nothing else. **ANSWERED 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling G6): kernel CR-7 rebuild, KRN-BIND-01 to 04; Save/Load SL-20, AC-SL-149.** AC-BDY-6 and AC-BDY-138 are Body's explicit INTEGRATION CLOSURE GATE: they run against the real kernel and the real Save/Load registrations in the composition-root story (the first end-to-end tick story), and Body's epic does not close until both pass. A `SEAT` row with no Controller row (a Vertical Slice disconnect, a corrupt save) is **not stepped**, is treated as `v_eff` 0 (`sub` and `last_octant` reset) and is counted `BDY_SEAT_UNBOUND`. `odometer` is 64-bit and travels in the binary payload (SL-2) as a native int64 (exact); the halves-or-strings convention remains legal, and is mandatory only where a value can reach JSON (header, dump-save), never as a JSON number *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0006 sync)*. *(orchestrator ruling: `seat` is an additive field beyond R3's list, adopted from the technical-director and engine-programmer proposals, which independently found the gap; the engine-programmer's name `owner_ref` is dropped in favour of the kernel's vocabulary, CR-7.)* *Decides:* R2, R3, CR-7, CR-8. (auto)

#### 1.3 The tick

**BDY-8 — `body.integrate` registration and order.** `hook_id body.integrate`, owner Ship Body, divisor 1, offset 0, **`after: [controller.step, layout.doors]`**. This resolves Layout Open Question 6 and LY-10 (LY:217-218) inside Body's own registration: an opening `area` or `door_state` changes only in P2; `layout.doors` runs in P3 and writes the leaf rectangle set; `body.integrate` reads that set in the same tick. The order is a PARTIAL order, not a chain: `body.integrate` runs after both `controller.step` and `layout.doors`, which are mutually unordered (`layout.doors` is `after: []` and must not depend on `body.integrate`); `controller.step` follows `suit.vitals` (SE-1, SE:146; PCI:133, PCI:145); `emitter.place` is `after: [body.integrate, graph.propagate.failure]` (EM:104, transitive, no Emitter edit). Every hook id this GDD writes is one its owner registers; the ids Body names are `suit.vitals`, `controller.step`, `layout.doors`, `body.integrate`, `emitter.place`, `graph.propagate.power`, `graph.propagate.atmo` and `graph.propagate.failure` (others, such as Interaction's `interaction.step`, IA:428, are registered and are simply not named here), written exactly because an unknown id fails boot (CR-3). The Controller's PC-14 and PC-15 text "`after: controller.step`" is a wording refresh (routed), not a contract change. *Decides:* R4, LY-10, CR-3, reflexion pattern 6. (auto: topological-order test)

**BDY-9 — Per-tick order inside `body.integrate`; the hook is total.** (i) *Pose.* For each `bodies` row in ascending `body_id`, `world += vel` (BDY-26), before any actor steps. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Immediately before the add the derived, non-persisted `world_prev := world` is set (BDY-26 states its reset rules on every discontinuous write). (ii) *Actors.* In ascending `actor_id`: read inputs (BDY-11), resolve the move octant and the residue reset (BDY-12), advance the residue of both axes (a refused step stores no energy and loses none, PCI:289, PCI:908), issue ONE `solid_in` query for the swept box (BDY-15), sweep x then y, and commit `pos_prev := pos_curr(old)`, `pos_curr := new`, `odometer`, `blocked`. (iii) *Derive.* Foot box and diagnostics. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **One `actor_section` rule (BDY-29): it is derived ON DEMAND at call time and is never computed per tick by the hook for every actor.** The only `section_at` calls the hook itself makes are the exit-containment tests of an EXEMPT actor's candidate sub-steps (BDY-16); an ordinary tick makes none. The earlier reading "(iii) derives `actor_section` per tick" is struck, so BDY-9 and BDY-29 no longer disagree and a tick with no exempt actor pays nothing for sections. **Allocation-free hook (budgeted whole, D.12):** the hook allocates no `Array`, `Dictionary`, `RefCounted` or `Vector2i` per tick: the actors table is held as id-sorted struct-of-arrays, `PackedInt32Array` for the s32 columns and `PackedInt64Array` for the two s64 columns (`actor_id` and `odometer`: a 2^53 + 7 odometer must not truncate, AC-BDY-137), kept sorted by the mutators (insertion by binary search in BM1, deletion in BM2, never a sort at tick time; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* the struct-of-arrays layout is the working assumption, not a contract, and spike S1 records it against an array-of-records form because copy-on-write and lookup cost are unmeasured, Open Question 46; the snapshot shape of BDY-31 is independent of it), `LocomotionStep` writes into a caller-owned scratch record (BDY-10), and the `solid_in` result lands in a caller-owned `PackedInt32Array` (BDY-15). A **stationary actor** (move octant NONE, or both requested steps zero this tick) issues no query and no sweep: it costs only the residue update and the commit, which covers every idle tick and the ~29 % zero-motion ticks of a carry diagonal (PCI:157 amendment 3). No iteration over an unordered container is ever hashed; no RNG, no wall clock, no float state. **The hook never FAULTs on a data defect** (a crash on a save or authoring defect would be the world simply stopping, a P1 violation of its own kind); defects are absorbed deterministically, counted and visible only in diagnostics:

| Condition found in the hook | Deterministic outcome | Counter |
|---|---|---|
| `actor.body_id` not live in Layout | actor skipped (not stepped, not moved), row kept | `BDY_ORPHAN_ACTOR` |
| `octant` not in 0..8 | treated as NONE | `BDY_OCTANT_INVALID` |
| `v_eff` not in 0..3 (the ceiling is the Controller's `v_max = v_run`, PCI:99, 3 base px per tick) | clamped into `[0, 3]` for this tick only | `BDY_VEFF_INVALID` |
| `SEAT` row with no Controller row | not stepped, residue reset | `BDY_SEAT_UNBOUND` |
| `world + vel` would leave ±`WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` | that body's `vel` set to (0, 0) in the same tick, `world` unchanged | `BDY_WORLD_LIMIT` |

Only an invariant of the block's own tables (a duplicate `actor_id`, a negative counter) is a `FAULTED`-class defect, and `restore` rejects those before they can exist (BDY-31). *(orchestrator ruling: where the engine-programmer proposed saturating `world` at the bound and the technical-director proposed freezing `vel`, the technical-director's form is adopted, which never clamps a coordinate in the hook; the one stated exception is the guarded, defect-only saturation of a BM4 new pose row, BDY-23 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Hook budget, stated once (D.12):** `body.integrate` is budgeted as ONE hook (pose step, every actor step including the exempt worst case, commit, dirty marking, diagnostics), not per actor in isolation; the catch-up worst frame of `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` ticks is four such hooks back to back (D.12 states both lines). *Decides:* KRN CR-9, CR-13, KRN D.12 `state_hash`; P1. (auto: PC-18 test 9)

**BDY-10 — `LocomotionStep` is a pure function keyed by `actor_id`.** `step(state, intent, collide) -> state'`, with `state = {pos, sub, last_octant, odometer}`, `intent = {octant, v_eff}` and `collide` bound to Layout's `solid_in`; it returns the new state plus `moved` and `blocked`. It is a plain `RefCounted`, scene-tree-free (kernel H10), headless-testable, and used by Body for `SEAT` rows. The equivalence between the specified behaviour (1-px sub-steps) and any faster formulation is a test, not an assumption (BDY-15). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Allocation-free form (the hot-path signature).** The value-returning form above is the specification and the unit-test surface; the hot path calls `step_into(state_idx, intent, collide, scratch)`, which reads and writes the id-sorted struct-of-arrays row in place (BDY-9) and writes `moved_x`, `moved_y`, `blocked` and the exempt flag into a **caller-owned scratch record** that `body.integrate` allocates once at boot and reuses for every actor and every tick; `collide` is bound once at boot to Layout's `solid_in_into` (BDY-15), not re-created per call. Both forms must return identical results (AC-BDY-91); neither constructs a `Dictionary`, `Array`, `RefCounted` or `Vector2i` per call. *Decides:* R9, PC-3, H10. (auto) → ADR-BODY-01.

**BDY-11 — Inputs Body reads, and nothing else of the Controller's.** From `controller.state[seat]`: `octant` (0-7 or NONE) and `v_eff` (the Controller's `gait_effective`, written by `controller.step`, PCI D.4). Body derives no gait, speed, facing, carry, channel or locomotion-state fact (reflexion pattern 3) and never reads `loco_state`: a STAGGERED, DOWNED, DEAD, `IN_POD` or CHANNELLING seat arrives with `v_eff = 0` and Body's own rules do the rest (BDY-12). The Controller is the sole writer of the speed; Body is the sole writer of the position. *Decides:* R21, PCI:143. (auto)

**BDY-12 — The sub-pixel residue and its single reset predicate.** The two-axis residue `sub` is Body's. Define the **move octant**: `NONE` when `v_eff == 0`; else `octant` when `octant != NONE`; else — only in the deceleration ladder (`STOP_DECEL_TICKS` > 0, PCI D.5, dormant at the MVP default 0) and only while `last_octant != NONE` — Body's own persisted `last_octant`; otherwise NONE, which is IDLE and counts `BDY_OCTANT_INVALID` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. `sub` resets to `(0, 0)` **before** the step on any tick where the move octant differs from Body's persisted `last_octant`; after the step `last_octant := move octant`. So `sub` resets on a stop, on any `v_eff == 0` tick (STAGGERED, DOWNED, DEAD, `IN_POD`, CHANNELLING, the end of a ladder) and on any change of octant; it does **not** reset on a gait change (walk to run in one octant keeps the residue, PCI D.3, PC-18 test 12), on a refused step, or in `post_restore`. Comparing against Body's OWN `last_octant` (not the Controller's restore-normalised `octant`) is what makes BDY-31 work. Consequence for the Controller's text: the STAGGERED line "`sub = (0, 0)`" (PCI:690, PCI:990) writes a Body-owned field and is reworded "`v_eff = 0`; Body resets `sub`" (**CONFLICT**, routed; behaviour is identical). *(orchestrator ruling: the gameplay-programmer's "reset whenever `v_eff == 0`" is the rule; the technical-director's and engine-programmer's decel-ladder direction is kept as the provisional sub-case above, so the ladder is not silently broken if `STOP_DECEL_TICKS` is ever raised.)* *Decides:* R2, R3, PCI amendments 2 and 4, R21. (auto: PC-18 tests 4, 12; D.14)

**BDY-13 — Axis step.** Per tick, per axis, x then y: `sub_a += UNIT[o].a * v_eff; step_a = trunc(sub_a / 256); sub_a -= step_a * 256; moved_a = sweep_a(step_a)`; the position advances by `moved_a`. The expression, the `UNIT` table (256 cardinal, 181 diagonal), `trunc` rounding (so west mirrors east), `V_WALK` 2, `V_RUN` 3, `V_CARRY` 1 and the diagonal cadences (walk 1,1,2,1,2,1,1,2; run eight 2s then a 3) are CITED from PCI D.3 (PCI:273-300) and the registry entries `V_WALK`, `V_RUN`, `V_CARRY` and `locomotion_axis_step`, and are never restated here as Body values. The registry entry `locomotion_axis_step` was re-sourced to this GDD with PCI:275's expression on 2026-10-27; the former extra `*256` factor (a transcription defect) was removed at re-source (Done; 8.2). *Decides:* T4; Guard rail 2. (auto: PC-18 tests 1-3)

**BDY-14 — Foot box, foot origin, first legal row.** `foot_box(actor) = [pos.x - 8, pos.x + 8) x [pos.y, pos.y + 8)` in body-frame cells, half-open (LY-7 convention), with `FOOT_BOX_W` 16 and `FOOT_BOX_H` 8 (Body-registered, confirming PCI:103). **The foot origin is the box's top-centre**: horizontally centred, and the origin row is the box's top row. `y_foot` is the Pipeline's floored presentation foot row `p.y` (PIPE D.9, `p = floor(prev + (curr - prev) * tick_alpha)`, PIPE:535); the foot origin is the box's top row, so at rest (`pos_prev == pos_curr`: a stop, `post_restore`, a transfer) `y_foot = pos_curr.y` and the first-legal-row corollary (`y_0 + 24`) is literal. That corollary makes both designed examples literal: a body walking north into a wall whose footprint rows are `[y_0, y_0 + 24)` rests with `pos.y = y_foot = y_0 + 24`, "the first legal row south of the wall" (PIPE:543, PIPE:727, PCI:909 "the foot box's top edge rests at 124"); a body walking south into the same wall rests with `pos.y = y_0 - 8` (box bottom flush). A committed `pos` that places a foot-box cell inside a SOLID rectangle is a collision defect (`diag.actor_in_footprint`, `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP`) unless BDY-16 applies. `foot_box` is registered as a formula (D.2), not as two loose constants. Layout's passage extents (16 across an east-west doorway, 8 across a north-south one, LY D.5) are unchanged. **CONFLICT (a moved anchor, swept now rather than later):** every designed line that says the foot origin is the box CENTRE must read "horizontally centred; the foot origin is the box's top row": PCI:103 ("foot-centred"), PCI:447 ("`body.state.pos`, foot-box centre"), PCI:651 ("foot origin (foot-box centre)") and PCI:908 ("foot-centred"); PCI:1001 says only "foot origin" and needs no edit; the other "foot origin" hits in Layout, Emitter, Lighting, Interaction and the Controller name the origin, not the centre, and stand. The origin is 4 px north of the old centre reading; Edge Cases states the consequence for the detach side rule once. *(orchestrator ruling R5: the Stage-1 brief's `y_foot := pos.y - 4` is overruled — it rewrites the Pipeline's PIPE D.12 variable table inside a consumer GDD and needs an art offset the Presenter does not have; top-centre sits inside the authority LY-11 and PCI:1087 already grant Body.)* *Decides:* R5; PIPE D.12; LY-11. (auto: D.2 and D.3 fixtures; spike: both rest poses captured at zoom steps 1 and `k_auto + 2` for art sign-off, because top-centre puts the sprite's contact row on the box's north edge)

**BDY-15 — Collision is `solid_in`, exactly, with no margin.** `collide` is Layout's `solid_in(body_id, aabb)` (body frame, key order `(section_id, idx)`, `SOLID` filter, LY-12 (c)), called ONCE per moving actor per tick with the swept bounding box of the whole move — the Minkowski cover of the box over the x-then-y path, at most 19 x 11 cells at `|step| = 3` (`16 + |step_x|` by `8 + |step_y|`, D.12) — and the sweep tests only the returned set; a rectangle not returned cannot be hit by construction. The sweep is 1-px sub-steps, x axis then y axis: before each pixel the destination box is tested against every candidate rectangle and the first refusal stops that axis for the tick. Any faster formulation (an analytic clamp against each rectangle's near edge) is permitted if and only if it is result-identical on every input; an equivalence property test carries the proof (the choice is → ADR-BODY-01). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Baseline and oracle, decided.** The **analytic clamp is the shipped baseline** (Layout already assumes it: "Callers issue ONE `solid_in` per actor per tick with the swept bounding box and sweep analytically against the result", LY-12 (c)); the **literal 1-px stepper above is the specification and the TEST ORACLE only** and is never the production path. The cost model of D.12 prices the clamp, not the stepper. **Allocation-free query:** Body calls `solid_in_into(body_id, aabb, out: PackedInt32Array) -> int` (fills a caller-owned buffer with rectangle indices in key order and returns the count) rather than `solid_in(...)`, which returns a fresh array each call; `solid_in_into` is an additive ASK to Layout (8.3, Open Question 32), and until it exists Body calls `solid_in` and the hot-path allocation criterion (AC-BDY-125) is BLOCKED on it. The buffer is sized once at boot to `MAX_FOOTPRINT_RECTS_PER_BODY`. There is no inflated margin, no "perceived" variant and no second rectangle set: a wall that blocks the foot but not the eye, or the reverse, is a lie (P1). Collision uses the 24-px footprint, never the 72-px face (`WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH`, art-bible-owned; Layout is not its source). Doors are rectangles Layout has already rewritten (BDY-18). *Decides:* P1, LY-34, T5. (auto: PC-18 tests 5-6; property tests)

**BDY-16 — Overlap exemption: the body is never displaced, and an exempt actor can only leave.** Let `E` be the set of SOLID rectangles the actor's foot box intersects **at the start of the actor's tick** (fixed for that tick). Members of `E` arise from a door leaf that closed under the actor, a restore of an older save against a changed template, or a template defect *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)* (a cut can no longer cap a hatch under an actor: BM4 refuses it, BDY-24 (iii)). For an actor with a non-empty `E`, a 1-px sub-step on either axis is admitted iff **for every `r` in `E`, `|box_after ∩ r| <= |box_before ∩ r|`** (overlap measured in cells; D.11 states `ov(r)`) **and the destination foot origin lies in a section of the actor's body (exit containment, below)**; every rectangle not in `E` stays fully solid, so an exempt actor cannot walk into a wall it was not already inside. The test is per rectangle, not total: on the `fixture_airlock` hull-line geometry a box astride two abutting exempt rectangles `[672, 696)` and `[696, 720)` keeps its TOTAL overlap at 128 cells while stepping east (one rectangle loses a column, the other gains one), so a total-area test would let it cross the hull line, while the per-rectangle test refuses that step because the east rectangle's own overlap grows; every move along the shared edge (both overlaps unchanged) and every move away (both shrink) stays admitted. `E` is computed afresh each tick from the current box (no stored flag; nothing persisted or hashed). Each exempt actor-tick increments `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` (read 0 in a clean build) and, in debug, fires `diag.actor_in_footprint` (PIPE:727). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **What "exempt actor-tick" counts (option (a), adopted).** `E` is computed from a `solid_in` query, and a stationary actor issues none (BDY-9), so the counter increments **once per actor-tick in which `E` was evaluated and found non-empty** (a tick on which the actor requested a non-zero step, so the query ran), **plus once at each detection event whose start-of-tick box test finds `E` non-empty** (placement, a transfer, a weld or cut for every actor on the touched bodies, `post_restore`): a detection event on an actor whose box intersects nothing adds nothing, so a clean weld, cut or restore leaves the counter at 0 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier wording read as an unconditional increment per event)*. An idle exempt actor therefore adds nothing on its idle ticks, and a trapped actor that stops pressing a key stops counting; the counter is a lower bound on exempt time, and its only contract is the QA gate "0 in a clean build" (CI, not a playtest alarm; Tuning Knobs). Stationary overlaps are made visible by the detection events, by the `BDY_TRAPPED_ACTOR` probe below and by the debug tree-walk (PIPE:727), never by a per-tick query Body does not otherwise issue. A rectangle in `E` is fully solid for the actor again on the first tick whose start box no longer intersects it. **Nothing shoves**: `pos` is unchanged on every tick the actor does not step, and a push-out, shove or snap is struck (an unannounced displacement the player did not cause, P2; it contradicts PCI:650 "the body is never displaced" and LY-11). The rule is strictly tighter than PCI:650's literal "non-solid for that actor until the overlap clears" (PCI:650, PCI:1002); the additive wording row is routed to the Controller in 8.3. Detection happens at placement, at every transfer, at `post_restore` and on every stepping tick; a stationary actor issues no query, so a stationary overlap is caught at those events and by the debug tree-walk. **Exit containment (the second clause of the predicate; P1).** A sub-step that passes the overlap test is ALSO refused if its destination foot ORIGIN (the box top-centre, D.2) lies in no section of the actor's own body (D.10: `actor_section` would answer NONE for that origin). The test reads the sections of the actor's current `body_id` only, so a plate whose section has left the body (the B plate after a cut) is no longer floor for this purpose. The only exits from a hatch cap therefore lead back onto the body's own floor: no exempt sub-step can carry an actor out of the hull, and an exempt step never produces a NONE section. An actor whose START origin is already in no section (a defect, Edge Cases) may step only toward an origin that lies in a section. The reason is that a crew member standing outside the hull while their air, temperature and pressure still came from a room they had left would be a hidden stat contradicting what the world shows, which is the failure the Pillar 1 design test forbids. **What the predicate guarantees, and what it does not.** Any sub-step with a component away from a rectangle in `E` shrinks that rectangle's overlap, so a PARTIALLY overlapping box whose shrinking direction leads onto the body's own floor leaves in a bounded number of ticks and can never deepen. Three residuals are accepted and are defect-only paths: (i) a box wholly inside one rectangle (the common case, because `FOOT_BOX_H` 8 is less than the 24-px strip) has constant overlap until an edge crosses out, so it may exit on either side, including through a closed leaf, provided the destination origin lies in a section of the body (never out of the hull); (ii) a box astride two abutting exempt rectangles can only slide along their shared edge or back out, and if neither reaches open floor it stays exempt in place; (iii) **the cap trap:** an actor in a hatch cap whose only shrinking direction would put its origin outside the body cannot leave. The concrete case: after a restore of a save recorded in that pose against the Layout state holding plate A SOLID, an actor at `pos = (690, 282)` has the box `[682, 698) x [282, 290)` astride the east edge of the cap plate `[672, 696) x [240, 336)` (plate B, `[696, 720)`, belongs to a section that is not part of this body); stepping west deepens the overlap (refused), stepping north or south keeps it (admitted by the overlap test) until the box meets the rectangles that are not in `E` and stay solid (D.11 gives the extent), and stepping east shrinks it and is admitted only while the destination origin is inside plate A: at `V_WALK` 2 the actor walks `pos.x` 690, 692, 694, 695 and the sub-step to 696 is refused (the origin would lie in no section of the body), so it rests inside the cap at `pos.x = 695` with `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` counting every tick on which it presses a key *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the counting rule above; the trap is a strip, not a point: box `[x - 8, x + 8)` straddles the plate's east edge for `pos.x` 689 to 695, where west deepens the overlap (120 to 128 at 689) and refused, so an east input converges to `pos.x = 695` from any start in the strip; from `pos.x` 680 to 688 the box lies wholly inside the plate, west keeps the overlap at 128 and is admitted, and the actor walks back onto the floor; in y the strip spans `pos.y` 240 to 328, the rows for which the box lies wholly inside the plate's rows `[240, 336)`, and at the margins `pos.y` 233 to 239 and 329 to 335 the neighbouring hull-wall rectangle joins `E` and the outcome depends on Layout's neighbour geometry, which the fixture does not fix — D.11 states the full admitted-move set)*. That is a stuck state, and it is not a safe one. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **The exposure rule for a footprint-resident exempt actor, stated once (BDY-16, BDY-29, D.11 repeat it).** The first draft added that the trapped actor's "every air, heat and pressure reading ... is the reading of a section it is still in"; that sentence was false and is struck. Body guarantees only that `actor_section` answers a section of the actor's own body (the origin `(695, 282)` lies in plate A). Suit does not read the section: it reads Layout `room_at(section_id, actor_local_px)`, and Layout answers NONE for any point inside a wall footprint (LY-5, LY:905; a `CAPPED` or `CUT_OPEN` hatch is a bare SOLID `HULL` plate with no room, LY-15), and Suit maps a NONE that is not `IN_POD` to EXPOSED (SE-3, SE:342; the Graph answers the exterior's atmosphere, LSG:206). **Rule (option (c), adopted): an exempt actor whose foot origin lies inside a footprint rectangle of its own body (the cap trap, and residual (i) while it stays inside) is EXPOSED: it takes the exterior's values (vacuum, anoxic, exterior temperature) on a pressurised ship until its origin leaves the footprint or the game restarts from the last autosave.** Options (a) and (b) were examined and rejected. (a) Layout owns the answer for a plate cell and it is NONE; Body cannot write "the adjoining room" over a designed neighbour, so that is an ASK to Layout (Open Question 31) that would supersede this paragraph if adopted. (b) "origin in a section AND `room_at != NONE`" cannot help here: the trapped actor's START origin is already inside the plate, so `room_at` already answers NONE; the one shrinking direction is refused by the same containment and every other direction is refused by the overlap rule, so (b) freezes the actor where it stands, still EXPOSED. **A door-leaf exemption is not exposed:** a closed leaf sits on the door's sill and `room_at` answers the door's `sill_room` there (LY-5), so only wall and hull-plate (cap) footprints read NONE. **How this meets Pillar 1:** the actor is exactly where its feet are, visibly inside the plate, and Suit's own instruments report the exterior band it is standing in (the bands and cues are Suit's); the world does not lie, but the guarantee is **visibility, not safety**, and no sentence of this GDD claims otherwise. Body offers no rescue (a push-out is struck); the primary guard is that the state is unreachable (Assembly's refusal, deferral and the P2 tell window, and BM4's apply-time refusal, BDY-24); recovery belongs to Assembly (a re-weld), to Save/Load (the death-restart) or to the content fix (Open Question 27). This is why **Assembly's refusal (player cut) and deferral plus the P2 tell window (system cut) are the primary guard, a named must-repeat** (BDY-24, 8.3), BM4's apply-time refusal stands behind them (BDY-24 (iii)), and this rule is only the residual net for a restore or a template defect; `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` in the debug overlay makes every such tick visible. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Trapped-actor probe and the restored-while-trapped case (diagnostic only, never a displacement; Body will never rescue).** At each detection event (placement, a transfer, `post_restore`, **and a weld or cut: at BM3 and BM4 the event covers EVERY actor on the bodies the mutator touched, moved or not, because the SOLID rectangle set changed under them — a box left in a SOLID rectangle by a template defect would otherwise first be seen at the next `post_restore`; a cut itself cannot create the D.11 cap trap, because BM4 refuses it first (BDY-24 (iii))**) Body runs, for every actor whose start box gives a non-empty `E`, a bounded read-only probe: for each of the four axis directions it repeats the admitted 1-px sub-step of this paragraph (overlap test plus exit containment) up to `TILE_PX` (48) times from the actor's pose, writing nothing, and asks whether the box reaches a pose that intersects no SOLID rectangle. If none of the four directions does, the actor is **TRAPPED**: `BDY_TRAPPED_ACTOR` counts, the debug overlay shows it and the pure read `trapped_actors() -> actor_id[]` (ascending; recomputed on every read from current state and never cached, so a stale entry cannot suppress an autosave indefinitely; not persisted, not hashed) lists it. The probe costs at most 4 x 48 box-versus-`E` tests per flagged actor, at those events and on each `trapped_actors()` read (one `solid_in` query per actor to find `E`, at most `MAX_ACTORS` actors per read), never inside `body.integrate` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. It exists for one case the restore path creates: **an autosave written while an actor was trapped restores the trap** (positions restore exactly, BDY-31), and the MVP death policy (restart from the last autosave) can then reload into it; the probe makes that visible on the restore, and Body's answer is none beyond that: the remedy is content and authoring validation (Assembly's refusal and deferral keep the state unreachable, BDY-24; Open Question 27) and Save/Load declining to write an autosave while `trapped_actors()` is non-empty, a MUST-REPEAT owned by Save/Load whose acceptance criterion lives in Save/Load's GDD (Open Question 33, 8.3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was an optional ASK)*. The QA gate is `BDY_TRAPPED_ACTOR` = 0 across every clean-build scenario (AC-BDY-29c). *Decides:* R7, CP-1, PCI:650, LY-11, P2. (auto: D.11 hatch fixture; bounded exit from a partial overlap)

**BDY-17 — Actors do not block one another.** In the MVP an actor's foot box collides with Layout rectangles only: two crewmates (and, later, a horror and a crewmate) pass through each other, and two foot boxes overlapping is legal and silent — no diagnostic. Reasons: no player may body-block a crewmate's escape down a corridor in a breach (an ungated friendly-harm vector against the anti-PvP anti-pillar); a doorway jam in the breach rhythm would be chaos from the controls, not from the world (P2); and actor-pair sweeps sit outside the 0.05 ms per actor budget model (PCI:1078). Cites PCI:105 ("no actor pushes another at MVP") and PCI:139. Doors still respect every actor: `leaf_would_overlap` takes every seat's foot box (LY-11, BDY-18), so "a door will not close on any crewmate" still holds. Crowding in a one-tile corridor at four seats is a Vertical Slice question (Open Questions). *Decides:* PCI:105, PCI:139; P2; anti-pillar "no intentional PvP". (auto)

**BDY-18 — Door leaves are read in the tick they change; Body supplies the boxes.** Body reads the leaf rectangle set after `layout.doors` (BDY-8). Body publishes `foot_boxes(body_id)` (opaque half-open rectangles, ascending `actor_id`) that Interaction's `validate` gathers for Layout's `leaf_would_overlap(door_ref, area, foot_boxes[])` (IA-24; Layout never reads `body.state`, LY-11). Body does not decide `BLOCKED` or `OBSTRUCTED` (LY-11 notes the Controller spells the reason `BLOCKED` and Interaction `OBSTRUCTED`; neither is Body's) and obeys BDY-16 for the residual door-leaf, restore and template-defect cases *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)*. *Decides:* LY-9, LY-11, IA-24. (auto: PC-18 test 7)

**BDY-19 — `odometer` and `blocked`.** `odometer += (|moved_x| + |moved_y|) * ODO_W[move octant]` post-collision (`ODO_W` 256 cardinal, 181 diagonal; 64-bit; PCI:157 amendment 5, PCI:491, PCI:502); walking into a wall adds 0. `blocked` is derived (true on a tick where a non-zero step was requested and the committed `moved` is (0, 0) on both axes), not persisted, not hashed and not replicated, and is **readable by PRESENTATION and DIAGNOSTIC subscribers only; never by a SIM hook** (lint): a SIM hook that read it would make it sim state that must then be persisted and hashed (a schema bump, deliberately). Its SIM-readable sibling is the derived read `moved(actor_id) := pos_curr - pos_prev` (CP-3; States 3.6): it adds no persisted field and is readable by a SIM hook only if that hook is ordered `after: [body.integrate]` (lint). `PC_BLOCKED_TICKS` is the Controller's counter and is cited, not re-registered. *Decides:* R16, PCI:491, PCI:502. (auto: PC-18 test 8; lint)

#### 1.4 Actor lifecycle, mutators and transfers

**BDY-20 — Actor lifecycle: place, remove, capacity.** BM1 `place_actor(body_id, pos, kind, seat) -> actor_id` **allocates** the id from `next_actor_id` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: BM1 returns that `actor_id` (>= 1) or a negative `FAULT_CODE`, and a fault writes nothing and leaves `next_actor_id` unchanged, BDY-21)* (first id 1, never reused after a removal, int64) and writes the row with `pos_prev := pos_curr`, `sub := (0, 0)`, `last_octant := 8`, `odometer := 0`. BM2 `remove_actor(actor_id)` deletes the row. The preconditions are pure reads the caller's `validate` invokes (BDY-21): `BDY_PLACE_UNKNOWN_BODY`, `BDY_PLACE_INSIDE_FOOTPRINT` (the foot box at `pos` intersects a SOLID rectangle), `BDY_PLACE_CAPACITY` (`actor_count >= MAX_ACTORS`), `BDY_PLACE_BAD_KIND`, `BDY_PLACE_DUPLICATE_SEAT`. `MAX_ACTORS` 8 (range 4-16) `[PLACEHOLDER]`, Body-registered: four seats at Vertical Slice plus horrors. Spawn tables are Derelict Authoring's; `init_world` converts a section-local spawn to the body frame through `section_offset` and calls BM1; the cryo-bay start is a template field, not a Body fact (Derelict Authoring DA-3 repeats this: the spawn table is section-local data converted by `init_world`; DA is now Designed and repeats BDY-20, so the contract is confirmed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was "In Design, so the contract stays provisional")*). *(orchestrator ruling: R8 lists `actor_id` as a BM1 argument, but locked fact CR-8 and R2's own text say the owning block allocates; the signature is corrected and a wording fix to R8 is routed.)* *Decides:* R8, R11, CR-8. (auto)

**BDY-21 — Mutator surface BM1-BM7, all Class A; every mutator returns OK or a FAULT_CODE; validation is a pure read.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the contract of this rule is rewritten; the earlier assumption that an apply cannot fail is struck, because it contradicted MSA-14, MSA-31 and AC-MSA-142)* Body publishes seven mutators — BM1 `place_actor`, BM2 `remove_actor`, BM3 `rebase_on_attach`, BM4 `rebase_on_detach` (five arguments, the fifth `cap_rects[]`, BDY-24 (iii) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)*), BM5 `transfer_actor`, BM6 `set_body_velocity`, BM7 `set_body_pose` (added at the consistency pass 2026-10-27, routed from `web-map-and-transit.md` ASK-B1, WM-6) — each called only from Assembly's or Transit's single `apply`, AFTER the Layout mutator in the same `WriteScope` (KRN:197 names the three mutator owners; the order inside one scope, Graph then Layout then Body, is Assembly's contract (Modular Ship Assembly is Designed and repeats it: MSA-14, table T3 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*), implied by Layout returning the records Body consumes, LY-16, LY-17). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Every mutator returns a status; none is assumed to succeed.** GDScript has no throw and no catch, and `assert()` is compiled out of a release export, so a mutator that cannot honour its apply-time predicate has to say so in its return value (the caller's half is MSA-31, MSA-14 and AC-MSA-142). BM2 to BM7 return an `int`: `OK` (0) or a NEGATIVE `FAULT_CODE`, which is the negated Body reason constant of the `can_*` read that would have rejected the call (reason constants are positive ints, so `-BDY_PLACE_CAPACITY` is a `FAULT_CODE`). BM1 returns the allocated `actor_id` (an int >= 1) or a negative `FAULT_CODE`: one return channel, no out-parameter and no per-call allocation. `rc < 0` is a fault for every mutator. The caller's `apply` (Assembly's or Transit's) checks the status after EVERY Body call and, on the first negative value, calls the kernel's fault entry (MSA-31, ASK K2) and makes no further mutator call, so a failure inside `apply` moves the kernel to `FAULTED` (KRN:104). Body's mutators are not kernel-registered owner callables (they run inside the caller's `apply`); Body's kernel-registered callables (`body.integrate`, `snapshot`, `restore`, `post_restore`) take the kernel's `ctx`, return their results through it and end `return ctx.complete()` (delegated ruling R3). What R8 calls "BM1 validates ..." is **published as pure `can_*` precondition reads** — `can_place`, `can_remove`, `can_rebase_attach`, `can_rebase_detach`, `can_transfer`, `can_set_velocity`, `can_set_pose` — that the caller's `validate` invokes before any mutator runs, exactly as the Graph's pure `can_join` serves weld validation; inside `apply` the same checks are re-evaluated as the apply-time predicate below, and every mutator is **check-then-write** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: it evaluates its WHOLE predicate before its first row write, so a call that returns a `FAULT_CODE` has written nothing (the tables, `next_actor_id`, the dirty set and the event queue are exactly as before the call, and no half-applied weld or cut exists). Every apply-time defect calls the injectable **`plan_mismatch_sink`**, a module-level `Callable` taking `(mutator_id, code)`: its default increments the counter `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH`, which is ACTIVE in every build, and in a debug build also calls `push_error`. No mutator path uses `assert()`, because a GDScript `assert` is compiled out of release and cannot be intercepted by a test; a test injects a recording sink and exercises every defect in the ordinary debug gdUnit4 runner (harness rule 1).
