The apply-time predicate per mutator is a mismatch with the pre-state its `can_*` read would have proven: BM1 any `can_place` reason; BM2 an unknown actor; BM3 the two ids equal or the surviving body not live in Layout; BM4 the old body not live, `new_body_id` not live in Layout, or a `moved_sections[]` member not owned by `new_body_id` (the call-order trap of BDY-23), an actor of the old body overlapping `cap_rects[]` (`actors_overlapping(old_body_id, cap_rects)` non-empty, BDY-24 (iii)) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)*, or *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* a new pose row out of range *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: this one is NOT a failing predicate; it is the saturate-and-continue branch of the split below)*; BM5 an unknown actor, an unknown destination or an arrival box inside a SOLID rectangle; BM6 an unknown body or a velocity out of range; BM7 an unknown body or a coordinate out of range. **The split per defect class *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.** (i) *Return-the-code class* (the call writes nothing and returns the `FAULT_CODE`; the caller faults the kernel): an unknown, equal or dead id (BM2 `BDY_UNKNOWN_ACTOR`; BM3 and BM4 `BDY_REBASE_SAME_BODY` or `BDY_REBASE_UNKNOWN_BODY`; BM5 `BDY_UNKNOWN_ACTOR` or `BDY_PLACE_UNKNOWN_BODY`; BM6 `BDY_VEL_UNKNOWN_BODY`; BM7 `BDY_POSE_UNKNOWN_BODY`); a BM4 `moved_sections[]` member not owned by `new_body_id` (`BDY_REBASE_SECTION_NOT_OWNED`, the call-order trap of BDY-23); *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)* a BM4 call for which `actors_overlapping(old_body_id, cap_rects)` is non-empty (`BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP`, BDY-24 (iii); evaluated before the first write, so the call writes nothing); a BM3 or BM4 `frame_shift_px` off Layout's 24-px grid (`BDY_REBASE_SHIFT_OFF_GRID`); any rejected BM1, including an over-cap one (`BDY_PLACE_CAPACITY` and the other `can_place` reasons); a BM5 arrival box inside a SOLID rectangle (`BDY_PLACE_INSIDE_FOOTPRINT`); a BM6 velocity or BM7 coordinate out of range (`BDY_VEL_OUT_OF_RANGE`, `BDY_POSE_OUT_OF_RANGE`; the call writes nothing, so no unloadable row can result). (ii) *Saturate-and-continue class* (the call completes and returns `OK`): **only BM4's new pose row**, where `world_old + frame_shift_px` leaves `±WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` or `vel_old` plus drift leaves `±VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK`; it saturates per BDY-23, the sink counts `BDY_WORLD_LIMIT` (where applicable) and `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH`, and BM4 returns `OK`. The reason is the user's ruling that a death restarts from the last autosave: by the time BM4 runs the Graph and Layout have already applied the cut, and a row `restore` would reject (`BDY_RESTORE_POSE_RANGE`) would make the next autosave unloadable, so this branch is load-bearing and must not become a fault. Class (i) is checked first: a BM4 call that has both an unknown new body and a range overflow returns the code. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)* Within class (i) the id, ownership and grid defects are checked before the cap overlap (the overlap read needs a live old body), and `can_rebase_detach(old_body_id, frame_shift_px)` is unchanged by the cap guard (it takes no `cap_rects`), so Assembly's V4 and the MSA-7 reason order are untouched. *Consistency* after any call, including every call that returned a `FAULT_CODE`: `restore(snapshot())` accepts the block, actor ids are unique and below `next_actor_id`, and every `body_id` in either table is live in Layout; there is no exception (an over-cap BM1 is a returned code and writes no row, so no over-cap row can exist and Open Question 13 is closed). This *is* validate-then-apply (CR-9) with the two halves held by different owners. None of the `can_*` reads needs Layout's apply-time return record *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: except that `can_rebase_detach` takes the seam's `frame_shift_px` as a caller-supplied argument, below, exactly as `docked` does)*: each proves only that the bodies it names are live in Layout's pre-state, that ids differ and, for BM4, that the new pose row is in range (`can_rebase_detach(old_body_id, frame_shift_px)` proves that the old body is live in Layout *and, (Amended 2026-10-27 — design review), that the cut cannot produce a pose row `restore` would reject*: it answers `BDY_REBASE_POSE_OUT_OF_RANGE` when `world_old + frame_shift_px` leaves `±WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` on either axis and `BDY_VEL_OUT_OF_RANGE` when `vel_old` plus the drift (BDY-28) leaves `±VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK`; `frame_shift_px` is the value Layout's D.12 fixes for the seam (Assembly passes its own V3 `frame_shift_px`, the value its `detach_preview` produced, to this read, so the range guard runs on the real validate path; the Layout preview itself is optional, Open Question 34; Assembly's V4 row and AC-MSA-77 still show the one-argument form, which Assembly conforms to this signature because the registry's `body_mutators` is sourced here (G1); at apply time Layout's REAL record is authoritative as for every other mutator) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; a fresh Layout `body_id` cannot already own a Body row, because `BDY_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_BODY` rejects any Body id Layout does not hold, and an apply-time disagreement between a returned record and the pre-state returns its `FAULT_CODE` and is reported to the sink as `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH`); at `apply` time Assembly passes Layout's REAL record, which Body treats as authoritative. Body registers no player command type and subscribes to no Layout dirty keys. *Rejected:* Body reconciling from Layout's dirty keys inside its hook (a write outside a mutator; CR-9), and Layout calling Body (breaks the acyclic LY-18). Dirty keys use Layout's encoding `(KIND << 32) | id`, `BODY` 2 (the shared family) and a new `ACT` 4, with **explicit dirty marking in every mutator** (4.7 packed-array element writes no longer call the property setter — verified-from-docs, `breaking-changes.md` 4.6 to 4.7, "Packed array element writes"). The full interface is in States and Transitions. *Decides:* R8, CR-9, LY-18; P1 (no half-applied weld leaves an actor in a frame that does not exist). (auto) → ADR-BODY-02.

**BDY-22 — Weld transfer (BM3).** On attach, only actors on the ABSORBED body are touched: `pos_curr += frame_shift_px`, `body_id := surviving_body_id`, `pos_prev := pos_curr`, `sub` and `last_octant` untouched (a whole-pixel shift cannot disturb a residue), and `BODY_TRANSFER{weld}` is emitted for those actors only. **Host-body actors are untouched** ("a weld moves nothing already built", LY:250). BM3 deletes the absorbed body's pose row. The mutator runs in P2 and `body.integrate` in P3 of the same tick, so the shifted actor is stepped this tick from the shifted position with a zero interpolation delta; the one-tick transfer artefact is removed by the Pipeline's C5 REBASE, not by Body: `BODY_TRANSFER{weld}` carries `frame_delta_px = +frame_shift_px` (3.7), and the Pipeline shifts its camera solver's `f` and history by it into the surviving body's frame and keeps the lag, so the avatar's screen position does not move (registry `camera_cut_predicates`: a weld or detach `BODY_TRANSFER` that carries `frame_delta_px` is a rebase, not a cut); a hard cut, never a pan, happens only where the new body's camera bounds clamp the rebased target *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. `frame_shift_px` is Layout's return value (the absorbed frame's origin expressed in the host frame, LY D.6; airlock fixture `(576, 96)`); Assembly passes the home-ship socket first, so the home-ship's body survives (LY-16, LY OQ-21). The invariant that makes this safe, stated as a test: for every actor moved, `(actor_section, actor_local_px)` is identical before and after (BDY-29). *Decides:* R7, LY-16; T3. (auto: D.4 fixture)

**BDY-23 — Detach transfer (BM4): the foot origin picks the side.** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)* Before any row is written BM4 evaluates its whole apply-time predicate, including the cap guard of BDY-24 (iii): if `actors_overlapping(old_body_id, cap_rects)` is non-empty it writes nothing and returns `-BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP`. A box that straddles the seam overlaps the seam's hatch rectangles, so in play it is refused rather than split; the side rule below is the specification for every origin that passes the guard and the unit-test surface with `cap_rects[]` empty. For each actor on the old body the side is the component whose section contains the foot ORIGIN on the detach tick (PCI:651, PCI:1001); a foot box straddling the seam is not split. Because BM4 runs after Layout's mutator, Layout has already re-based the moved sections, so Body evaluates containment against the **reconstructed pre-cut geometry**: for `s` in `moved_sections[]`, `offset_pre(s) = section_offset(s) + frame_shift_px`; for every other section of the old body `offset_pre(s) = section_offset(s)` (D.5; stated once as the call-order trap). Containment is by the section's FLOOR area (its rooms and the rectangles it owns, including its own 24-px hull footprint and hatch rectangles), never by bounding box, because hull-abutting sections have overlapping bounding boxes. Actors on `C_new`: `pos_curr -= frame_shift_px`, `body_id := new_body_id`, `pos_prev := pos_curr`, `sub := (0, 0)`, `last_octant` kept (harmless: `sub` is already zero, so a held key reads "no change"); emit `BODY_TRANSFER{detach}` with `frame_delta_px = -frame_shift_px` (3.7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Actors on `C_keep` are untouched. An origin lying in no section (an exterior gap, a defect) stays on `C_keep` and counts `BDY_TRANSFER_NO_SECTION`; this is a defect-only state, CI asserts it never occurs in a clean build (AC-BDY-29a), and its consequence is exterior exposure, never the last room's atmosphere (BDY-29, 8.3). BM4 creates the new body's pose row from the old body's (`world := world_old + frame_shift_px`, `vel := vel_old`, plus the default-zero detach drift of BDY-28), so a cut leaves both halves co-moving; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **the new row is always restorable.** The pose range is a restore rule (`BDY_RESTORE_POSE_RANGE`), so a BM4 that wrote an out-of-range row would make the next autosave unloadable and break BDY-21's `restore(snapshot())` guarantee and the death-restart path. The normal defence is `can_rebase_detach`'s `BDY_REBASE_POSE_OUT_OF_RANGE` and `BDY_VEL_OUT_OF_RANGE` (BDY-21) and Transit's re-origin discipline (WM-6 keeps every pose within `WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX`, 1/32 of the limit). The apply-time net for a caller that ignored the read is the D.7 freeze rule applied to the new row: if `world_old + frame_shift_px` leaves the range the new `world` is saturated per axis to `±WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` and `vel` is set to `(0, 0)`; if `vel_old + drift` leaves `±VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` it is saturated per axis; `BDY_WORLD_LIMIT` (where applicable) and `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH` count through the sink and BM4 returns `OK`: this is the saturate-and-continue class of BDY-21, and every other BM4 defect returns its `FAULT_CODE` and writes nothing *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. This is the one place a stored coordinate is saturated, it is defect-only, it touches the exterior pose alone (the interior bytes are unchanged, BDY-2), and the exterior `abs` conservation below is waived on that branch; the Controller clears `channel_ref` and keeps `octant`, `carry_ref` and `facing` (its rule, cited). The `sub` zeroing is a TRANSFER rule and does not conflict with BDY-31 (a restore is not a transfer). **Conservation law (the spike's falsifiable invariant):** with `abs(actor) = world(body_of(actor)) + pos_curr(actor)`, detach conserves `abs` exactly for every actor on either side; a weld conserves `abs` iff the docking invariant of BDY-27 holds; and **a weld followed by a cut of the same seam is not in general an identity on `pos_curr`** (detach re-bases to the section `m` with minimum id in `C_new`, LY D.12, while a weld shifts by the absorbed offset; the two coincide only when the absorbed body's section sat at its own frame origin), so any round-trip criterion asserts `abs` and the section-local position, never raw `pos_curr`. Fixture: Layout `fixture_chain` (chain 1 `(0, 0)`, 3 `(576, 96)`, 4 `(1056, 96)` cut at the (1, 3) seam: `frame_shift_px (576, 96)`, new offsets 3 `(0, 0)`, 4 `(480, 0)`). *Decides:* R7, LY-17, PCI:651; T3. (auto: D.5 fixture)

**BDY-24 — The hatch-cap hazard: refuse on player origin, never displace.** After a cut the socket's hatch is a SOLID `HULL` rectangle (`CAPPED` or `CUT_OPEN`, LY-15), so an actor standing in the hatch passage ends inside a footprint — a real hazard no designed GDD names. (i) Body publishes the pure read `actors_overlapping(body_id, rects[]) -> actor_id[]` (ascending); Assembly's `validate` of a PLAYER-origin cut rejects while it is non-empty for the seam's two hatch rectangles (the reason code is Assembly's: `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED`, adopted in MSA-21). **Origin is read as Assembly's `cause`** (consistency pass 2026-10-27, routed from `modular-ship-assembly.md` C7): hooks submit SYSTEM-origin commands only (KRN CR-3), so every channel-completed cut reaches P2 as SYSTEM-origin; throughout this GDD "PLAYER-origin cut" means `seam_cut {cause CHANNEL}` (rejects, at begin, handoff and completion) and "SYSTEM-origin cut" means `seam_cut {cause CASCADE}` (defers, MSA-22). (ii) If any transfer nonetheless lands an actor's foot box inside a SOLID rectangle — a restore of an older save, a template defect (a cut can no longer be the cause, clause (iii)) — BDY-16 applies: the body is never displaced. PCI:650 already rules the door analogue (a system-origin close enters `CLOSING_BLOCKED` and retries); this GDD turns the mirror into a **named must-repeat for Modular Ship Assembly (now Designed and repeating it, MSA-21, MSA-22; recorded in 8.3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was "provisional, undesigned")***: a SYSTEM-origin cut DEFERS while `actors_overlapping` is non-empty for the seam's two hatch rectangles, and the cut is preceded by the Pillar 2 tell window that Assembly and Adaptive Audio own (the 10 to 30 s escalating tell; the Graph's `W_MIN_WARNING_S` fairness gate), so a breach-driven cut can never separate a crewmate or seal one into a cap without a telegraphed window. How long a deferred cut may wait is Assembly's (Open Question 10); on expiry the cut is abandoned, never forced (MSA-22); Body supplies the pure read and, behind Assembly's V5, the apply-time net of clause (iii). The no-displacement rule is unchanged and correct: the deferral prevents the overlap, it does not resolve one. (iii) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)* **Crisis-cut guard — Resolved 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6 (user delegated all remaining calls): there is no cut path around Assembly's completion validate V5** — every cut is `seam_cut {cause CHANNEL|CASCADE}`, CHANNEL rejects (`MSA.CUT_BLOCKED`), CASCADE defers and on expiry is abandoned, never forced (MSA-21, MSA-22), and Layout's `detach_section` has no other caller; Layout's "crisis cut" is the CASCADE cut. Body rules REFUSE, not EJECT (ejection is the displacement that P2 and PCI:650 struck). Body adds the apply-time net behind V5: BM4 becomes `rebase_on_detach(old_body_id, new_body_id, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px, cap_rects[])`, where `cap_rects[]` is the `rects[]` of Layout's `detach_section` record (the seam's hatch rectangles, pre-cut body frame, equal to `detach_preview().rects[]`). Its apply-time predicate, evaluated before the first write (check-then-write), gains a class-(i) member: if `actors_overlapping(old_body_id, cap_rects)` is non-empty BM4 writes nothing and returns `-BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP`; the caller faults the kernel (MSA-31) and `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH` counts through the sink. `can_rebase_detach(old_body_id, frame_shift_px)` is unchanged, so Assembly's V4 and the MSA-7 reason order are untouched. `BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP` reads 0 in every clean build (a CI gate alongside `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` and `BDY_TRAPPED_ACTOR`). Consequence: a cut can no longer create the cap trap; its only sources are a restore of an older save and a template defect (BDY-16). **Struck:** a defensive deterministic push-out and its diagnostic, which contradict PCI:650 and P2. *Decides:* R7, LY-11, PCI:650, P2. (auto: hatch-cap fixture)

**BDY-25 — The pod hop is a body transfer.** BM5 `transfer_actor(actor_id, to_body_id, pos, cause POD)`: `pos_prev := pos_curr`, `sub := (0, 0)`, `last_octant` kept, `carry_ref` kept (the Controller's, PCI:694); emit `BODY_TRANSFER{pod}`. Body does not search for a free arrival cell (that would be a displacement): `can_transfer` answers `BDY_PLACE_INSIDE_FOOTPRINT` to the caller and Transit owns the alternative cell; two crewmates arriving at one cell is legal (BDY-17). **The pod is a Layout body** (Web Map & Transit is Designed and repeats it, WM-5 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was "provisional — Web Map & Transit undesigned")*; Derelict Authoring DA-3 authors the `pod` template, one room `pod_cabin`, seat cell (72, 48)): a section built by Layout `build_section` from a pod template, with its own Layout `body_id`, counted against `MAX_BODIES`; Body places the actor on it. Uniform rectangle and occluder paths and the Emitter's `body_id` partition (EM-30) and Lighting's mask bits then work unchanged. Web Map & Transit must repeat (DA-3 already does): the pod template exists and the pod is a body. **Web Map's answer to SE-35 (consistency pass 2026-10-27; WM-5, WM-17):** `room_at` answers `pod_cabin`, never NONE, on the pod (consistent with BDY-29: NONE is never a place on a Layout body); the pod's air is Suit's `HOLD_ROOM` keyed on `loco_state == IN_POD` (WM-17, Web Map ASK-S1), not a NONE answer. *Decides:* R15, PCI:173, PCI:192, PCI:694, EM-30. (auto)

#### 1.5 Pose in the exterior frame, the inertial interior

**BDY-26 — World pose, velocity and range.** `world_x`, `world_y` are integer base px that locate **the body-frame origin** (Layout's frame origin for that `body_id`, LY-3 (b)) in the exterior frame; `vel_x`, `vel_y` are whole base px per tick (no sub-pixel exterior residue in schema v1: the exterior layer is presentation and may interpolate by `tick_alpha`; a slower drift would be a schema bump made deliberately). `body.integrate` adds `vel` to `world` once per tick before stepping actors (BDY-9). Ranges: `|world_*| <= WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` 2^22 and `|vel_*| <= VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` 16, both Body-registered `[PLACEHOLDER]`; `can_set_velocity` rejects `BDY_VEL_UNKNOWN_BODY` and `BDY_VEL_OUT_OF_RANGE`; the defence against a leg that would run past the world range is the `BDY_WORLD_LIMIT` freeze of BDY-9 (a mis-scripted leg is Transit's lint, not a Body projection); the 2^22 bound exists so a canvas offset (a difference of two world coordinates, within ±2^23) stays inside the range a float32 `Node2D.position` represents exactly (2^24) **[UNV: build precision]** (→ ADR-BODY-03). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Lowering either bound is a schema bump.** `BDY_RESTORE_POSE_RANGE` rejects any saved row beyond the CURRENT `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` or `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK`, so lowering either constant after saves exist makes those saves unloadable; a lowering therefore ships with `schema_version` 2 and a migration that saturates the stored rows (Save/Load's migration, SL), never as a silent tuning change. The defaults stay 2^22 and 16: Web Map & Transit's own budget is stated against them (`WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX` 2^17 `<= WORLD_COORD_LIMIT / 32`, WM L741/L953), so a 2^21 default would break a designed neighbour's lint (declined, Open Question 35). **Derived exterior interpolation state (not persisted, not hashed, not replicated).** The exterior layer interpolates by `tick_alpha` (the one interpolator), so the read view carries `world_prev(body_id)`: the pose one tick ago. It is set `:= world` immediately before `body.integrate` adds `vel` (BDY-9), and **reset to the new `world` on every discontinuous write**: BM7 `set_body_pose` (including a Transit re-origin), BM4 for the new body's row, `post_restore` for every row, a BM6 that deletes a sparse row, and the BDY-9 freeze. BM3 deletes the absorbed row and writes none. A reset gives a stall of at most one tick of `vel` (at most 16 px, once) in place of a smear across the discontinuity. The renderer reads `canvas_offset_render(b, tick_alpha) = floor(off_prev + (off_curr - off_prev) * tick_alpha)` per axis, with `off_curr = world(b) - world(avatar_body)` and `off_prev = world_prev(b) - world_prev(avatar_body)` (D.7); the CURRENT avatar body is used for both, so a BM5 origin switch (the avatar's body changes on a pod hop, a weld or a cut) never interpolates across two reference frames — the Pipeline's C5 rebase (weld, cut) or cut (pod hop) fires on the same `BODY_TRANSFER` and the first post-switch frame has `off_prev` relative to the new origin *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **`heading_q` is reserved and MUST be 0**, and is rejected on restore with `BDY_RESTORE_HEADING_UNSUPPORTED` (the mirror of Layout's `rot_q` and `LAYOUT_ROT_UNSUPPORTED`, LY:154); a tumbling hulk is an exterior-layer sprite, never a simulation fact. MVP writers of `vel`: Web Map & Transit (the pod leg at constant velocity, PIPE:153 (iv)) and detach (default 0). **World-pose setter (BM7; consistency pass 2026-10-27, routed from `web-map-and-transit.md` ASK-B1 / WM-6; answers Open Question 23):** `set_body_pose(body_id, world_x, world_y)` overwrites `world_x`, `world_y` of one pose row and leaves `vel` unchanged; `can_set_pose` (Web Map's `can_set`) rejects `BDY_POSE_UNKNOWN_BODY` and `BDY_POSE_OUT_OF_RANGE` (any `|world_*|` above `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT`). It writes the exterior pose only: no actor row, `pos_curr`, `sub` or interior read changes (BDY-2 and D.9 hold unchanged), and it raises no event (a pose set is not a dock contact). It subsumes re-origin: Transit re-origins by translating every live-content body by one common vector inside one apply (WM-6), so no separate `rebase_world` mutator exists. Its only caller is Web Map & Transit, inside the applies WM-6 lists (`init_world`, departure, `pod_arrive`, `pod_settle`, `berth`, `unberth`, `berth_step`). *(orchestrator ruling: the world bound is named `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` with the technical-director's float32 rationale; the engine-programmer's `WORLD_ABS_MAX_PX` and `VEL_ABS_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` are dropped.)* *Decides:* R6, PIPE C2 (i) option (a). (auto + lint)

**BDY-27 — Canvas placement, the docking invariant, weld legality.** The canvas placement of a non-avatar body root is `world_pos(b) - world_pos(avatar_body)` in base px: already an integer (BDY-26), **one floored offset per body** (PIPE PR-4 rule 2, LO:420), never snapped at render time *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the per-frame value the renderer uses is the interpolated `canvas_offset_render` of BDY-26 / D.7, still one floored integer per body per frame)*. **Weld legality takes no pose input**: it is Layout `can_attach` plus the Graph's `can_join` and nothing else, so a weld on a "moving" ship is trivially safe — the pose is not an input, and the interior never reads it (BDY-2). On attach the absorbed pose row is deleted (BM3). The **docking invariant** (Transit's to establish, Body's to test): before a weld, `world_b - world_a == frame_shift_px`, which lies on the 24-px grid by Layout D.6; when it holds the visual jump at weld is zero. `docked(a, b, frame_shift_px) -> bool` is a pure read (equal `vel` and the invariant holds) offered to Assembly and Transit and **gating nothing inside Body** (Body's legality is unchanged: Layout `can_attach` plus the Graph's `can_join`); the shift is an argument because only Layout can compute it for a candidate seam. When the invariant does not hold a weld still succeeds in the simulation and the absorbed hull would visibly jump by `delta_abs` in the exterior layer, which a player would read as the ship jerking: the failure Player Fantasy anchor 1 and AC-BDY-74b forbid. **Named must-repeat for Web Map & Transit and Modular Ship Assembly (8.3; both designed and repeating it):** a PLAYER-origin weld is only OFFERED once `docked(a, b, frame_shift_px)` holds (this gates the offer, never Body's `validate`: MSA-12, AC-MSA-16; Transit establishes `docked`, WM-21). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **The offer gate is the ONLY guard of the exterior hull jump.** The earlier alternative — "where Transit cannot guarantee it, the Pipeline hides every non-zero `delta_abs` behind its C5 camera cut" — is struck: C5 resets the CAMERA and hides camera motion (the avatar's screen position), it does not hide the absorbed hull's jump in the exterior layer, so it cannot stand in for the gate. The gate is tested automatically (AC-BDY-74a), the interior half (the avatar's screen position does not move when `docked` is false) is tested by AC-BDY-118, and the playtest (AC-BDY-74b) runs only on the shipped, gated build. A weld that skips the gate exists only in a test build, as a non-gating diagnostic (AC-BDY-74c). Cross-body light is off (EM-30), which mitigates the rest. *(orchestrator ruling R12: the earlier proposal to snap the canvas offset to the 24-px grid is dropped; R12 wrote `docked(a, b)` and the shift argument is the one extension.)* *Decides:* R12, PR-4, LO:420; P1. (auto: D.8 fixture)

**BDY-28 — The interior is an inertial frame; Body events.** Ship motion applies **no force, push, tilt, drift or knockback** to any actor; nothing moves an actor except `LocomotionStep` and the transfer arithmetic of BDY-22, BDY-23 and BDY-25. There is no velocity readout, g-force, stagger, speed buff or traversal unlock. Docking contact, hull separation and detach are published as Body EVENTS in the P5 notification batch: `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT{tick, surviving_body, absorbed_body}` (raised by BM3), and `BODY_HULL_SEPARATION{tick, old_body, new_body}` (raised by BM4; the one event for the Pipeline's "hull section separation" and "detach" triggers, PIPE:163). They feed the camera's `JOLT_PX` trigger list (default 0, max 2, never Attention, Sanity, Tell or breach; reduced motion forces 0; registry `JOLT_PX`) and Adaptive Audio, whose job is the felt result Body asks of them: the weld and the cut are heard on the hull, through the structure, not felt in the floor. Optional at Vertical Slice and exterior-only: `DETACH_DRIFT_PX_PER_TICK` (default 0, Body-owned) added to the new body's `vel` along the separation axis away from the old body (the axis and sign are defined by the Vertical Slice stage that raises the knob; the MVP criteria do not exercise a non-zero value). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **The knob is LOCKED at 0 until that definition exists**: with no axis and no sign rule `vel_new = vel_old + drift` is undefined for a non-zero value, so a non-zero setting is rejected at boot (lint) and the 0-2 range quoted by the registry is the range the Vertical Slice definition may open, not a range available now (Tuning Knobs; Open Question 2); the interior never sees it (BDY-2). Whole-pixel velocity means 1 px/tick is already 60 px/s against a 640-px field, so "slow drift" is inexpressible in schema v1 (Open Questions). *(orchestrator ruling: event names follow the engine-programmer's wording, which echoes PIPE:163; the technical-director's `BODY_DOCKED` and `BODY_SEPARATED` are dropped.)* *Decides:* R14, P2, anti-pillar "not a combat game", PIPE:163. (auto + review)

#### 1.6 Reads, persistence, determinism

**BDY-29 — Section lookup for Suit; derived on demand.** Body's read view publishes `actor_section(actor_id) -> section_id | NONE` (the foot ORIGIN decides; derived from the current `pos_curr`, `body_id` and Layout offsets at call time, never stored) and `actor_local_px(actor_id) = pos_curr - section_offset(section_id)`. Suit then calls Layout's `room_at(section_id, actor_local_px)`, which takes SECTION-LOCAL px (LY-3 (c)), never body-frame `pos_curr` (the subtraction is plain integer arithmetic; offsets may be negative after a detach re-base, LY-3 (f), and Layout floors). A foot origin in no section of its body answers NONE, and **NONE on a Layout body is a defect-only state, not a place**: no Body rule produces it (an exempt step is refused before it can, BDY-16; ordinary sweeps are bounded by the hull footprints), CI asserts it never occurs in a clean build (AC-BDY-29a), and its consequence is stated once here as a **must-repeat for Suit & Equipment and the Life-Support Graph (routed, 8.3)**: an actor whose origin is in no section is exposed to the EXTERIOR (vacuum, cold), never to the atmosphere, temperature or pressure of the last room it occupied; a body standing outside the hull must not breathe the hull's air (P1). Suit's hold-the-last-room rule (SE-3) therefore stays for the pod abstraction (`loco_state` IN_POD, SE-35) and is not Body's to extend to a NONE on a Layout body. **On demand, because `suit.vitals` is the first hook of tick T+1 and reads Body after P2 of T+1 may have run a transfer:** an answer cached across a mutator would be stale; any cache is invalidated by every mutator and every restore (→ ADR-BODY-02). **Containment, not precedence.** A point is in a section iff it lies in that section's own half-open cell set (its rooms and the rectangles it owns, LY-7); LY-14's `GEOM_OVERLAP` test on every rect pair of a mate and the LY:207 `RECT_OVERLAP` lint make those sets disjoint across sections, so a point lies in at most one section and **no tie rule exists**: that a foot origin at `x = 696` in the `fixture_airlock` weld belongs to derelict B is a consequence of half-open rectangles (`[672, 696)` is A's, `[696, 720)` is B's), not a rule. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **`section_at` is Layout's, ANSWERED, and Body has no fallback.** Layout publishes `section_at(body_id, px) -> section_id | NONE` (LY-28, `ship-section-and-layout-data-model.md` L312: containment in each section's own half-open cell set translated by `section_offset`, disjoint across a body, FLOOR division, restore-safe), so `actor_section(actor_id)` is exactly `section_at(body_of(actor), pos_curr)`. The earlier "until answered Body derives it by scanning `sections_of(body_id)`" fallback is deleted: Body implements no section scan, so there is no second implementation to keep equivalent. **Cost.** One call is one containment query on Layout's side (its complexity is Layout's to state; Layout has not stated it, which is an additive ask, Open Question 36); Body's call count is bounded by construction: at most one per seat per `suit.vitals` run (the consumer) plus, only on a tick with an exempt actor, one per candidate sub-step of that actor (at most 6 per actor-tick, D.12), so an ordinary tick makes none; the trapped-actor probe (BDY-16) adds at most 4 x 48 `section_at` calls per flagged actor, per detection event and never per tick, so the per-tick bound is unchanged. **The footprint-resident exempt actor (the exposure rule of BDY-16, repeated here for Suit and the Graph):** an exempt actor whose origin lies inside a wall or hull-plate footprint of its own body has `actor_section != NONE` (the plate belongs to a section) but `room_at(section_id, actor_local_px)` answers NONE (LY-5), and Suit maps that to EXPOSED (SE-3, SE:342; LSG:206). Body therefore makes no "readings of a section it is still in" claim for it; a door-leaf exemption answers `sill_room` and is not exposed. The integration criterion on the D.11 fixture at `pos.x = 695` is AC-BDY-29b. Routed to the producer: the index edge Suit → Ship Body (Hard), which SE:985 (the index corrections) already asks for. *Decides:* R10, SE-3. (auto: D.10)

**BDY-30 — Read-view class, no variants, co-op.** `body.state` is a `SIM` block; its read view (pose, `pos_curr`, `pos_prev`, `actor_section`, foot boxes) may be read by PRESENTATION subscribers (CR-12) — this is the sentence LO:420 asks Body to repeat. There is no `PERCEPTION_OUT` variant and no per-player variant of any Body read; the Perception Filter draws its class-(c) rig at the true position (PCI:216, "never moves the chest or hand point"), and sanity never touches `body.state` (Sanity and Perception Filter are Designed and both repeat it: the Filter cites BDY-30, Sanity cites BDY-29 and BDY-30 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*). The read view's fields: `pos_curr`, `pos_prev`, `body_id`, `kind`, `seat`, `foot_box`, `bodies[*].{world, vel}` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: plus the derived, non-persisted `bodies[*].world_prev`, BDY-26)* and the pure reads of States and Transitions; `blocked` appears in the view marked presentation- and diagnostic-only (BDY-19). Vertical Slice co-op (VS-B): `SHARED_SUMMARY` replicates `pos_curr`, `body_id`, `seat`, `kind`, `odometer`, `last_octant`, `sub` (`seat` and `kind` so that a `MIRROR` can attach a rig to a seat); `blocked` and every `diag` value are outside the hashed and replicated sets; remote Presenters read `MIRROR` views; there is no client-side prediction in the MVP (KRN:745); a constant-`vel` body replicates on change. Cross-body light stays OFF in the MVP (EM-30) and is carried as Open Question "cross-body light, VS-A" (owner Ship Body + Emitter). *Decides:* R22, CR-12, LO:420, PCI:216; P4. (auto + review)

**BDY-31 — Persistence and restore idempotence.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Snapshot and restore shape, stated once.** `snapshot()` returns plain data (kernel CR-8) and `restore(data, stored_version, ctx)` takes that same shape, decoded by Save/Load, so a restore input is a Variant-shaped tree in which the type check can meet a float or a string: `{next_actor_id: int, n_bodies: int, n_actors: int, bodies: <n_bodies * 6 ints>, actors: <n_actors * 10 ints>}`, flat row-major, rows ascending by id, the body columns in the order `body_id, world_x, world_y, vel_x, vel_y, heading_q` and the actor columns in the order `actor_id, kind, seat, body_id, pos_x, pos_y, sub_x, sub_y, last_octant, odometer`. The canonical BYTES of BDY-32 (3) are the kernel's TLV encoding of exactly this payload (`canonical_encode(snapshot())`, kernel D.12) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; they are what the hash, the golden file (AC-BDY-110) and the byte-identity claim use, and they are never a restore input. `restore` validates in this order and rejects the WHOLE block at the first failure, leaving the live state untouched: type (every element a plain integer), count, per-row fields, order and canonicity, then Layout liveness. `snapshot(restore(snapshot()))` is byte-identical for `body.state` alone (equal `canonical_encode` output; SL-15 item 10), so `body.state` leaves `REBASE_BLOCKS` (SL:577, "emptied when the Controller/Body owners answer"). `post_restore` sets `pos_prev := pos_curr` (and resets `world_prev := world`, BDY-26) because `pos_prev` is a non-persisted derived field that Body itself must re-derive after a restore (BDY-6; SL-15 item 8 (SL:230)); that write is **Body-internal derivation and is NOT the camera-continuity mechanism**: camera and presentation restore continuity is solely the kernel's `snap_baseline` on entry to `RUNNING` plus the camera CUT on a `PresentationClock.load_seq` change (ADR-0008 § 6.1; ADR-0001 § 4), and Pipeline C5 no longer asks Body for a `post_restore` `previous := current` *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix: ADR-0008 § 6.1, TD-22)*. It preserves `sub` (it is persisted and is not touched), and verifies the table below. Because Body compares the move octant with its OWN persisted `last_octant` (BDY-12), a key still held across a restore reads N versus N, no change, and the residue continues; a key released during the save/load window reads NONE versus N and resets `sub` exactly as a stop does in uninterrupted play. The claim is **block-level G4-exactness only**: this GDD makes no claim about pair-level G4, because whatever PC-17's normalisation does to Controller-owned state (`octant := NONE`, the run ramp, `loco_state`) is the Controller's. **Assumption (GAP, stated as the test's GIVEN):** the "key held" case requires the Adapter's resync (PC-2 trigger iii) to land in P2 of the first post-restore tick so that `controller.step` writes N before `body.integrate`; if the resync lands one tick later, the first tick reads NONE, resets `sub` (at most 255/256 px) and the cadence restarts — not a correctness defect, but it changes the fixture's GIVEN. **CONFLICT (wording, one additive amendment to a designed GDD):** PCI:103 ("zeroes `sub`" at restore), PCI:202, PCI:285 (the PCI D.3 variable table), PCI:855, PC-17 (PCI:149), PC-18 test 10 (PCI:151), PCI:913 (fixture) and criterion PCI:1043 (`sub == (0, 0)`), and PCI:1108 Open Question 1 ("residue reset") each become "`pos_prev == pos_curr`; `sub` is preserved and is reset on the first octant change"; PC-18 test 10 is re-fixtured as: snapshot mid-move with `sub != 0`, restore with the same key held, assert `pos_prev == pos_curr` and that the per-axis cadence continues from the persisted residue (PCI:1043 is additionally inconsistent with PC-7's CHANNELLING-with-`octant == N` and is corrected in the same amendment). **Restore validation — rejection is a reported failure, never a silent drop or clamp** (a clamp would make `snapshot(restore(x)) != x`): `restore` rejects the WHOLE block on the first failure with a named reason, **reported through `ctx.fault(BDY_RESTORE_*)`** (Body's domain code plus the named detail), never by a return value (no `(ok, reason, detail)` tuple) and never by clamping: the kernel fires `load_failed` and Save/Load maps it to the `RESTORE_FAILED` class of SL-11 *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0006 sync: section 2.2; ADR-0003)*. The named reasons are `BDY_RESTORE_VERSION` (version not in `[1]`), *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `BDY_RESTORE_TYPE` (any field of any row that is not a plain integer when decoded — a float `pos_x`, a bool, a string, a null, a missing field: the type check runs before every range check, so a float cannot be silently truncated into a range-valid integer and break `snapshot(restore(x)) == x`), `BDY_RESTORE_ACTOR_ID` (duplicate, below 1 or not below `next_actor_id`), `BDY_RESTORE_KIND` (kind not `SEAT` or `AGENT`, negative `seat`, an `AGENT` with a seat, a `SEAT` with `seat` 0, two `SEAT` rows sharing a seat), `BDY_RESTORE_OCTANT` (`last_octant` outside 0..8), `BDY_RESTORE_SUB_RANGE` (`|sub|` above 255) and `BDY_RESTORE_SUB_INVARIANT` (a non-zero `sub` with `last_octant` NONE, a sign opposing `UNIT[last_octant]`, a non-zero off-axis residue for a cardinal octant, or, *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* on a diagonal octant, `|sub.x| != |sub.y|`), `BDY_RESTORE_ODOMETER` (negative), `BDY_RESTORE_HEADING_UNSUPPORTED`, `BDY_RESTORE_POSE_RANGE`, `BDY_RESTORE_CAPACITY` (actors above `MAX_ACTORS`), and *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* `BDY_RESTORE_COUNT` (`n_bodies` or `n_actors` negative, or a column length other than `n_bodies * 6` or `n_actors * 10`), `BDY_RESTORE_ORDER` (body rows not strictly ascending by `body_id`, which includes a duplicate `body_id`, or distinct actor rows not ascending by `actor_id`; a duplicate `actor_id` stays `BDY_RESTORE_ACTOR_ID`, checked first) and `BDY_RESTORE_NONCANONICAL` (a body row with `world` and `vel` both `(0, 0)`: the sparse form deletes such a row, so storing it would break `snapshot(restore(x)) == x`); and `BDY_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_BODY` (any `body_id` in either table not live in Layout), which is checked in `restore()` itself against Layout's already-restored state (`restore_after: [layout.state]` guarantees Layout restored first), so it rejects the whole block with the live state intact; `post_restore` repeats the check as a diagnostic only, because once every block has restored the kernel holds no intact prior state to fall back to. A foot box found overlapping a SOLID rectangle after restore is **not** a failure — a Layout template that changed between builds must not make a save unloadable; BDY-16 applies and `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` counts. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `post_restore` also runs the trapped-actor probe of BDY-16 for every exempt actor (diagnostic only: `BDY_TRAPPED_ACTOR` counts, nothing is displaced and the restore still succeeds), and **a restore never rebuilds the kernel's seat binding itself**: the kernel's `LOADING` rebuild (CR-7) does, from Body's registered binding source `seat_bindings()` (BDY-7, KRN-BIND-01; Open Question 30, closed) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)*. *Decides:* R3, SL-15 items 8-10, SL OQ-9; P5. (auto: restore round trip at every tick of a 600-tick run in three phases — cardinal, diagonal with `sub != 0`, stopped; synthetic odometer above 2^53)

**BDY-32 — Determinism contract (G3 and G4).** (1) *Order:* actors in ascending `actor_id`, bodies in ascending `body_id`, pose before actors; actors cannot affect one another (BDY-17), so actor order cannot change a result, yet it is fixed so that diagnostics and dirty-key insertion are reproducible. (2) *Sweep input:* Layout's result arrives in key order `(section_id, idx)`; the sweep takes a minimum over candidates and is order-independent by construction, and the order is asserted in debug so a Layout reorder is caught. (3) *Canonical bytes* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: canonical bytes are the kernel's (D.12 TLV), not Body's. Body defines only the SHAPE of the `snapshot()` payload: a Dictionary with exactly five keys `next_actor_id` (int), `n_bodies` (int), `n_actors` (int), `bodies` (Array of ints, `n_bodies * 6`, rows ascending by `body_id`: `body_id, world_x, world_y, vel_x, vel_y, heading_q`) and `actors` (Array of ints, `n_actors * 10`, rows ascending by `actor_id`: `actor_id, kind, seat, body_id, pos_x, pos_y, sub_x, sub_y, last_octant, odometer`); plain `Array`, never Packed, never floats, no `pos_prev`. The in-memory s64 columns (`PackedInt64Array`, BDY-9) are copied into the Arrays at snapshot time, and ids are sorted at snapshot time, never taken in `Dictionary` order (`snapshot()` never iterates a `Dictionary` for hashing; insertion order is **[UNV]**, not in the engine reference). The kernel encodes it: DICT keys sorted by code point of `String(key)` *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0005 sync)* (`actors`, `bodies`, `n_actors`, `n_bodies`, `next_actor_id`), every int an 8-byte little-endian INT, so s32 and s64 widths no longer exist in the bytes and an odometer of 2^53 + 7 is exact. Body ships no byte writer and does not use `var_to_bytes` for any hashed byte (its layout is an engine serialisation detail, **[UNV]**, and not a stable cross-version canonical format). The tables are kept id-sorted by the mutators (BDY-9), so `snapshot()` verifies sortedness in debug and sorts only a table a defective caller left unsorted. (4) *No float state, no wall clock, no RNG* in the block or the hook; `Vector2i` may exist in memory, but the snapshot stores plain int arrays and `odometer` is never placed in a `Vector2i` (Body's own lint; SE-34's "no `Vector2i` in `snapshot()`" is the sibling precedent). (5) *Tick-source independence:* Body is correct for any number of `body.integrate` runs per rendered frame up to `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` 4; Presenters see only `pos_prev` and `pos_curr` of the last tick of a frame, and four catch-up ticks move at most 12 px, under `JUMP_CUT` (PIPE:501) at the default field. Body does not choose the tick source (kernel Open Question 2, KRN:957). (6) *Proofs:* PC-18 test 9 (replay identity) and the invariance fixture of BDY-2. *Decides:* KRN CR-13, D.12; R13. (auto + lint)

#### 1.7 Projection, diagnostics, units

**BDY-33 — The PROJECTOR slot.** Body registers exactly one `PROJECTOR` subscriber, `body.projector`, which owns (a) one per-body root `Node2D` per live `body_id` and its canvas TRANSLATION (rotation 0 by BDY-26; the avatar's body at the origin, others at BDY-27's offset); (b) the loaded body's bounding rectangle, published as the camera-limits rectangle to the Pipeline's camera solver on weld, detach and load (PIPE:165 C8) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0009 sync: OQ-C; `body.projector` never writes `Camera2D.limit_*`; the solver is the only clamp and the primary variant has no `Camera2D`, ADR-0008 section 6.2; confirmed with ADR-0034)*; and (c) nothing physical — no shape, no collision, no `PhysicsServer2D` call in the MVP. A PROJECTOR "writes engine-side derived objects ... never writes blocks" (CR-10). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Y-sort ownership and Presenter parenting.** (d) Each body root owns exactly one child layer node with `y_sort_enabled = true` — the **actor/props layer** — created, owned and freed by `body.projector`; Layout's section nodes (floor, wall faces) are siblings of that layer under the same root, never inside it, so the Pipeline's tie rule (PIPE D.12: layer order decides equal keys, the actor layer drawing after the wall layer) is a property of two named nodes and not of insertion order. (e) **Sort-key convention:** a Presenter's node `position` is set by the Presenter (the Controller's) to the integer `(p.x, p.y)` of PIPE D.9 in its body's frame, so the Y-sort key of an actor is its `y_foot = p.y` (PIPE D.12), and a prop's key is the Pipeline's `sort_y`; nothing in Body writes a sort key or a `z_index`. (f) **Reparent on `BODY_TRANSFER`:** when the P5 batch of tick `T` carries `BODY_TRANSFER{weld | detach | pod}` for an actor, `body.projector` moves that actor's Presenter node into the destination body's actor/props layer in the same tick and before the frame that follows it renders, then the Presenter writes its position from `pos_curr` in the destination frame (the engine's global-transform-preserving reparent is NOT used; whether `Node.reparent` keeps local or global position is **[UNV]**, ADR-BODY-03); Presenters register their node with the projector (`attach_presenter(actor_id, node)`) so Body, not the Controller, owns the move — a must-repeat for the Controller's Presenter story (8.3). The draw-order criteria of AC-BDY-69 and AC-BDY-73 depend on (d) to (f); AC-BDY-92b tests the parent after a weld, a cut and a pod hop. Layout's PROJECTOR parents its section nodes under Body's root for that `body_id` (**ASK Layout**, additive: LY:354 names "a per-body `Node2D`" without an owner). Whether a weld reparents section nodes or rebuilds the body's scene is the weld-spike-measured choice shared with ADR-LAY-03; it lives in ADR-BODY-03, not in behaviour text, and Y-sort under reparent and the tie rule are **[UNV]** (a HIGH gap Layout already carries). The Camera is a top-level node, never a child of a body; `physics_interpolation` is OFF for all presentation and `tick_alpha` is the one interpolator (PIPE:119 rule 8; ADR-BODY-04). *Decides:* R20; KRN:198; PIPE:165. (auto: projector contains no physical call; spike: reparent versus rebuild)

**BDY-34 — Diagnostics.** Body owns `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` (PCI:650 attributes the assert to "a Layout projection"; LY:222 routed it here, and it can run only where the rectangle and the foot box are both visible, which is Body's sweep), `diag.actor_in_footprint` (PIPE:727), the event `BODY_TRANSFER{weld|detach|pod}` with old and new body ids and the frame delta (the Pipeline rebases by it on a weld or detach, PIPE C5, and records `diag.cut_reason = BODY_TRANSFER` only when it cuts *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; `PC_BODY_TRANSFER{detach|pod}` is the Controller-spelled counter of PCI:651, fed by this event), the defect counters of BDY-9, BDY-23, BDY-21 and BDY-16 (`BDY_ORPHAN_ACTOR`, `BDY_OCTANT_INVALID`, `BDY_VEFF_INVALID`, `BDY_SEAT_UNBOUND`, `BDY_WORLD_LIMIT`, `BDY_TRANSFER_NO_SECTION`, `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH`, and *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `BDY_TRAPPED_ACTOR`, and *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)* `BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP`) and per-actor sweep cost (microseconds per `body.integrate` actor step, logged above `SWEEP_COST_LOG_MS`). `BDY_BLOCKED_TICKS` is NOT created: the Controller's `PC_BLOCKED_TICKS` is cited. Diagnostics live in the hook's diagnostics channel, never in the block, never in the hashed set. *Decides:* R16, D17. (auto)

**BDY-35 — Units, rounding and zoom invariance, stated once.** Every Body length is base px and identical at every ladder step; no `k`, `s`, `field_h` or zoom value appears in this GDD outside this sentence (modelled on LY-3 (e)). Two rounding rules are kept apart and never mixed: **`trunc` toward zero** is the SUB-PIXEL rule (PCI:157 amendment 2, PCI:904), applied only to `sub_a / 256` in BDY-13; **FLOOR division** is the px-to-tile rule (LY-3 (f)), applied only where a pixel becomes a tile or a local coordinate (`section_at`, the broadphase, `room_at` inputs). Each is a single named static helper in `IntMath` (`src/shared/int_math.gd`), shared by Body, Layout and Emitter, so they cannot be confused; the function list and divisor guard are owned by ADR-0005 section 1.4 and 1.7, and ADR-0010 section 5.4 fixes only the path and the single annotated site *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0010 sync)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Float-free definitions.** `trunc_div(a, b)` is the ONE annotated (`@warning_ignore("integer_division")`) `/` site in `src/` (ADR-0010 SIM-05, PRJ-04): with both operands typed `int` and `b != 0` it is integer division truncating toward zero (truncation toward zero and signed `%` probed 2026-10-28 on the 4.7.2 editor binary, ADR-0010 P11; the release-export repeat is LINT-SPIKE-1), and it contains no float, no `int()`/`floor()` call and no `Variant`. `floor_div(a, b)` for `b > 0` is `trunc_div(a - posmod(a, b), b)`: the numerator is an exact multiple of `b` (`posmod` is non-negative for `b > 0`), so the division is exact and the truncating/flooring distinction cannot arise; for `b <= 0` it is a defect (assert in debug). The fixtures below (`floor_div(-1, 48) == -1`) and the lint of BDY-3 prove both; no other `/` on integers is allowed in `src/`. GDScript's integer `/` truncating toward zero was probed on the 4.7.2 editor binary (ADR-0010 P11; the release-export repeat is LINT-SPIKE-1), and the unit-test root still carries engine-semantics fixtures — `trunc(-181 / 256) == 0`, `trunc(-362 / 256) == -1`, `trunc(-768 / 256) == -3`, `floor_div(-1, 48) == -1` — evaluated through the same expression the step uses; if the engine disagrees, the failure is a spike finding and the step uses an explicit sign-split expression. Body-frame coordinates and `offset_px` may be negative after a detach re-base (a negative-coordinate test is in Acceptance). *Decides:* R24; reflexion pattern 5. (auto + lint)

### States and Transitions

Body has three state machines, none of them player-visible: the persisted **actor body-state** (where an actor row is in its life), the per-tick **actor step-state** (derived, never persisted), and the **body pose-state**. Every transition is caused by exactly one writer; the table names it. Transfers are atomic inside one P2 `apply` (CR-9), so between ticks only `ON_BODY` is observable in the persisted table.

#### 3.1 Actor body-state (persisted row life)

| State | Entry | Exit | Transitions | Who may cause them | Observable between ticks |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PLACED` | BM1 creates the row: `pos_prev == pos_curr`, `sub (0, 0)`, `last_octant` 8, `odometer` 0; the spawn box was cleared by `can_place` | in the same `apply` | → `ON_BODY` | BM1 only (`init_world`, respawn) | No (logical) |
| `ON_BODY` | end of BM1; end of any transfer | BM2; a transfer mutator | → `TRANSFERRING{weld \| detach \| pod}`, → `REMOVED` | the hook steps it each tick (it changes `pos_curr`, `sub`, `last_octant`, `odometer` only); mutators change `body_id` | **Yes** (the only persisted state) |
| `TRANSFERRING{weld}` | BM3: the actor's `body_id` is the absorbed body | same `apply` | → `ON_BODY` on the surviving body: `pos_curr += frame_shift_px`, `pos_prev := pos_curr`, `sub` and `last_octant` kept | Assembly's `apply` (after Layout `attach_section`) | No |
| `TRANSFERRING{detach}` | BM4: the actor's foot origin lies in a `moved_sections[]` extent (pre-cut geometry, BDY-23) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)* and `actors_overlapping(old_body_id, cap_rects)` is empty (otherwise the call returns `-BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP` before any row is written and no actor transfers, BDY-24 (iii)) | same `apply` | → `ON_BODY` on `new_body_id`: `pos_curr -= frame_shift_px`, `pos_prev := pos_curr`, `sub := (0, 0)`, `last_octant` kept; an actor on `C_keep` is not transferred | Assembly's `apply` (after Layout `detach_section`) | No |
| `TRANSFERRING{pod}` | BM5 | same `apply` | → `ON_BODY` on the pod body (or back): `pos_prev := pos_curr`, `sub := (0, 0)`, `last_octant` kept, `carry_ref` kept (Controller's) | Transit's `apply` (Web Map & Transit, Designed) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | No |
| `REMOVED` | BM2 | terminal | row deleted; `actor_id` is never reused within a world | Transit (despawn; Assembly calls only BM3 and BM4, MSA-17); never the hook | Row absent |

A transfer that leaves the foot box inside a SOLID rectangle keeps the actor `ON_BODY` under BDY-16: the exemption is recomputed each tick from the current box and is not a separate state.

#### 3.2 Actor step-state (derived each tick inside `body.integrate`; not persisted, not hashed)

| Step-state | Condition at the start of the actor's step | What the hook does | Counters |
|---|---|---|---|
| `IDLE` | move octant NONE (a stop, `v_eff == 0` for any reason, or, *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* `v_eff > 0` with no usable octant while the ladder is off or `last_octant` is NONE, which also counts `BDY_OCTANT_INVALID`) | resets `sub` if `last_octant` was not NONE, sets `last_octant := NONE`, `pos_prev := pos_curr`, `moved (0, 0)`, `blocked false`; **no Layout query** | — |
| `STEPPING` | move octant 0-7 and the foot box overlaps nothing SOLID | advances `sub` (both axes), one `solid_in` query, x sweep then y sweep, commits | — |
| `BLOCKED` | a non-zero step was requested and the committed `moved` is (0, 0) | as `STEPPING`; `blocked := true` (readable by PRESENTATION and DIAGNOSTIC subscribers only) | the Controller's `PC_BLOCKED_TICKS` (cited) |
| `EXEMPT` | the foot box intersects one or more SOLID rectangles at the start (BDY-16) **and the move octant is 0-7 and a non-zero step is requested this tick, so the `solid_in` query ran** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: an idle overlapping actor is `IDLE`, issues no query and adds no count; BDY-16 counting rule)*; those rectangles form `E` for this tick | as `STEPPING`, but a 1-px sub-step is admitted only if, for every `r` in `E`, `|box_after ∩ r| <= |box_before ∩ r|` AND the destination foot origin lies in a section of the actor's body (exit containment, BDY-16); rectangles not in `E` stay solid; no shove | `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` +1; `diag.actor_in_footprint` in debug |
| `STATIONARY` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | the foot box intersects SOLID rectangles at the start and the move octant is 0-7, but both requested steps are zero this tick (a carry-diagonal zero-motion tick, about 29 % of that cadence) | as `IDLE` for queries: no `solid_in` query, `E` not evaluated, residue update and commit only; not `EXEMPT` | none (the overlap was counted at its detection event, and counts again on the next tick with a non-zero step) |
| `SKIPPED` | `body_id` not live in Layout, or a `SEAT` row with no Controller row | not stepped, not moved, row kept; for `BDY_SEAT_UNBOUND` `sub` is reset to `(0, 0)` and `last_octant := NONE` as `IDLE` does; for `BDY_ORPHAN_ACTOR` the row is left untouched | `BDY_ORPHAN_ACTOR`, `BDY_SEAT_UNBOUND` |

#### 3.3 Body pose-state (per `body_id`, derived from the sparse row)

| State | Meaning | Entered by | Exits to | Who may cause it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `STATIC` | row absent, or `vel == (0, 0)`; `body.integrate` leaves `world` unchanged | default; BM6 with zero velocity; BM7 (a pose set on a row with `vel == (0, 0)`); BDY-9's limit freeze | `MOVING` | BM6, BM7; the hook (limit freeze only) |
| `MOVING` | `vel != (0, 0)`; `world += vel` each tick; the interior is unaffected (BDY-2) | BM6 (pod leg, detach drift); BM4 copying a moving body's `vel` | `STATIC`, `DISSOLVED` | Transit (BM6); Assembly (BM4) |
| `DISSOLVED` | the body was absorbed by a weld: BM3 deletes the pose row; the `body_id` is retired in Layout | BM3 | terminal (Layout owns the id) | Assembly (BM3) |

A zero velocity on a zero-world row deletes the row (sparse form), so a body with no actor and no motion costs nothing; `body_count()` counts bodies that have at least one actor or a pose row.

#### 3.4 What changes in which tick phase

| Event | Phase | Writer | Block fields written | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Walking, sliding, stopping | P3 | `body.integrate` | `pos_prev`, `pos_curr`, `sub`, `last_octant`, `odometer` (`blocked` derived) | the only writer of these during play |
| Pose integrates | P3 | `body.integrate` | `world_x`, `world_y` (and `vel` only at the limit freeze) | first action of the hook (BDY-9) |
| Weld | P2 | BM3 (after Layout `attach_section`) | actors on the absorbed body; the absorbed pose row is deleted | `BODY_TRANSFER{weld}` per moved actor; `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT` |
| Cut | P2 | BM4 (after Layout `detach_section`; the cap guard of BDY-24 (iii) is evaluated first, before any write) | actors on `C_new`; a new pose row | `BODY_TRANSFER{detach}`; `BODY_HULL_SEPARATION`; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* the BDY-16 detection event (box test and trapped-actor probe) for EVERY actor on the touched bodies, moved or not |
| Pod hop | P2 | BM5 | one actor | `BODY_TRANSFER{pod}` |
| Place, remove | P2 | BM1, BM2 | one `actors` row, `next_actor_id` | |
| Velocity set | P2 | BM6 | one pose row | |
| Pose set (placement, re-origin) | P2 | BM7 | `world_x`, `world_y` of one pose row | exterior only; no event, no actor field (BDY-26) |
| Restore | LOADING | `restore`, then `post_restore` | `pos_prev := pos_curr` and `world_prev := world` (Body-internal derivation of non-persisted fields, BDY-6, BDY-31; not the camera-continuity mechanism, which is the kernel `snap_baseline` plus the camera CUT on `load_seq`, ADR-0008 § 6.1); verification only *(Amended 2026-10-29 — sync knock-on fix)* | after `layout.state` (`restore_after`) |
| Events and counters | P5 | the kernel's notification batch | none | payload carries ids and counts only, no state |

#### 3.5 Mutator interface (all Class A; each returns OK or a FAULT_CODE and is check-then-write; explicit dirty marking)

| Id | Mutator | Caller | Precondition read (caller's `validate`) and Body-spelled reasons | Writes | Dirty keys |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BM1 | `place_actor(body_id, pos, kind, seat) -> actor_id` (an int >= 1, or a negative `FAULT_CODE`; BM2 to BM7 return `OK` or a negative `FAULT_CODE`, BDY-21) | `init_world` (Transit, Authoring), respawn | `can_place`: `BDY_PLACE_UNKNOWN_BODY`, `BDY_PLACE_INSIDE_FOOTPRINT`, `BDY_PLACE_CAPACITY`, `BDY_PLACE_BAD_KIND`, `BDY_PLACE_DUPLICATE_SEAT` | new `actors` row; `next_actor_id += 1` | `ACT/<actor_id>` |
| BM2 | `remove_actor(actor_id)` | Transit (Assembly calls only BM3 and BM4, MSA-17) | `can_remove`: `BDY_UNKNOWN_ACTOR` | delete row | `ACT/<actor_id>` |
| BM3 | `rebase_on_attach(surviving_body_id, absorbed_body_id, frame_shift_px)` | Assembly `apply`, after Layout `attach_section` | `can_rebase_attach(surviving_body_id, absorbed_body_id, frame_shift_px)`: `BDY_REBASE_SAME_BODY`, `BDY_REBASE_UNKNOWN_BODY`, and *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* `BDY_REBASE_SHIFT_OFF_GRID` (`frame_shift_px` not on Layout's 24-px grid, a Layout defect; also the apply-time `FAULT_CODE`; Assembly passes the shift it holds, MSA V5) | shift and re-home absorbed actors; delete the absorbed pose row | `ACT/<id>` per moved actor, `BODY/<absorbed>` |
| BM4 | `rebase_on_detach(old_body_id, new_body_id, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px, cap_rects[])` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)* | Assembly `apply`, after Layout `detach_section` (`cap_rects[]` is the `rects[]` of Layout's `detach_section` record) | `can_rebase_detach(old_body_id, frame_shift_px)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `frame_shift_px` added as a caller-supplied argument; two reasons added)*: `BDY_REBASE_UNKNOWN_BODY` (the old body is not live in Layout), `BDY_REBASE_POSE_OUT_OF_RANGE` (`world_old + frame_shift_px` beyond `±WORLD_COORD_LIMIT`), `BDY_VEL_OUT_OF_RANGE` (`vel_old` + drift beyond `±VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK`); apply-time *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: a returned `FAULT_CODE` for an equal-id, not-live, not-owned or off-grid defect (`BDY_REBASE_SAME_BODY`, `BDY_REBASE_UNKNOWN_BODY`, `BDY_REBASE_SECTION_NOT_OWNED`, `BDY_REBASE_SHIFT_OFF_GRID`) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)* or an actor overlapping `cap_rects[]` (`BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP`, BDY-24 (iii)), while a pose or velocity range overflow SATURATES the new row through the D.7 freeze and returns `OK` (BDY-23) | shift and re-home `C_new` actors; create the new pose row (`world := world_old + frame_shift_px`, `vel := vel_old`, plus drift; always within range, BDY-23) | `ACT/<id>` per moved actor, `BODY/<new>` |
| BM5 | `transfer_actor(actor_id, to_body_id, pos, cause POD)` | Transit `apply` (Web Map & Transit is Designed, WM-5) | `can_transfer`: `BDY_UNKNOWN_ACTOR`, `BDY_PLACE_UNKNOWN_BODY`, `BDY_PLACE_INSIDE_FOOTPRINT` | one row | `ACT/<actor_id>` |
| BM6 | `set_body_velocity(body_id, vx, vy)` | Transit (pod leg), detach default 0 | `can_set_velocity`: `BDY_VEL_UNKNOWN_BODY`, `BDY_VEL_OUT_OF_RANGE` | creates or updates the pose row (a zero velocity on a zero-world row deletes it) | `BODY/<body_id>` |
| BM7 | `set_body_pose(body_id, world_x, world_y)` (consistency pass 2026-10-27; Web Map ASK-B1, WM-6) | Transit `apply` only (`init_world`, departure re-origin, `pod_arrive`, `pod_settle`, `berth`, `unberth`, `berth_step`) | `can_set_pose` (Web Map's `can_set`): `BDY_POSE_UNKNOWN_BODY`, `BDY_POSE_OUT_OF_RANGE` (any `\|world_*\|` above `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT`) | creates or updates the pose row's `world_x`, `world_y`; `vel` unchanged (a zero world on a zero-velocity row deletes it); no actor row is touched | `BODY/<body_id>` |

Dirty keys use Layout's encoding `(KIND << 32) | id` (registry `layout_dirty_key_encoding`), keys being namespaced by block, so reusing `BODY` 2 inside `body.state` is legal and `ACT` 4 is unused by Layout (D.13). `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256 per block per tick, above which the kernel reports `ALL`. A negative return from any mutator makes the caller's `apply` fault the kernel (`FAULTED`, CR-9, MSA-31); the `plan_mismatch_sink` and its `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH` counter record it (BDY-21), and because every mutator is check-then-write the fault leaves the Body tables untouched *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

#### 3.6 Pure reads and the read view (SIM; PRESENTATION subscribers may read, CR-12)

| Read | Returns | Used by |
|---|---|---|
| `pos_curr(actor_id)`, `pos_prev(actor_id)` | int pair | Presenter; Emitter ACTOR anchor (EM-21); Lighting shadow origin (LO-10) |
| `body_of_actor(actor_id)` | `body_id` | Emitter partition (EM-30); Lighting mask bits |
| `foot_box(actor_id)`, `foot_boxes(body_id)` | half-open rectangle(s), ascending `actor_id` | Interaction `validate` (IA-24); Assembly |
| `actor_section(actor_id)`, `actor_local_px(actor_id)` | `section_id \| NONE`; int pair (section-local) | Suit (SE-3) |
| `actors_overlapping(body_id, rects[])` | `actor_id[]` ascending | Assembly `validate` of a PLAYER-origin cut, i.e. `cause CHANNEL` (BDY-24, MSA-21) |
| `actors_on(body_id)` | `actor_id[]` ascending | Assembly, Transit (discard decisions) |
| `world_pose(body_id)` | `(world_x, world_y, vel_x, vel_y)`; an absent row reads zeros *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the read view also carries the derived `world_prev`, and `canvas_offset_render(b, tick_alpha)` of D.7 is computed from it by the renderer; neither is persisted or hashed)* | `body.projector`, Pipeline canvas offsets, Transit |
| `seat_bindings()` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | `[(seat, actor_id)]`, ascending `seat`, `SEAT` rows only; derived on each call, no persisted field | consumer: the kernel's `LOADING` binding rebuild (CR-7, KRN-BIND-01; BDY-7) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)* |
| `trapped_actors()` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | `actor_id[]` ascending; **recomputed on every read** from current state (the bounded probe of BDY-16 over every actor whose current start box has `E` non-empty), never cached, so an actor that leaves the trap leaves the list on the next read and a stale entry cannot suppress an autosave; derived, not persisted, not hashed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | Debug Overlay; Save/Load's autosave guard (a must-repeat, Open Question 33) |
| `docked(a, b, frame_shift_px)` | bool | Assembly (gates the PLAYER weld offer, MSA-12), Transit; gates nothing inside Body (registry `docking_invariant`) |
| `body_count()` | int | Assembly (a future `CAPACITY` reason code) |
| `odometer(actor_id)` | int64 | Presenter (PCI D.11 frame index, PCI:487); Debug |
| `can_*` (seven reads, 3.5) | OK or a Body-spelled reason | the callers' `validate` |
| `moved(actor_id)` := `pos_curr - pos_prev` | int pair (derived on each call; no persisted field, not hashed) | SIM-readable **only from a hook ordered `after: [body.integrate]`** (lint): within such a hook it is a difference of two tick-boundary persisted values and therefore G4-exact; a SIM hook ordered before `body.integrate` would read last tick's value in play and 0 on the first post-restore tick. Reader: the Controller's `controller.noise` hook, ordered `after: [body.integrate]` (PCI PC-15, D.13; CP-3 (b) adopted, 4.1) |
| `blocked(actor_id)` | bool, **presentation and diagnostic only** | Presenter; Debug Overlay (lint forbids any SIM read) |

#### 3.7 Events and diagnostics

| Name | Kind | Payload | Consumers |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BODY_TRANSFER{weld \| detach \| pod}` | P5 event | `{tick, actor_id, seat, old_body, new_body, frame_delta_px}` | Pipeline (REBASE by `frame_delta_px` on a weld or detach, PIPE C5; a cut with `diag.cut_reason = BODY_TRANSFER` on a pod hop or where the camera bounds clamp *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*), Controller diagnostics (`PC_BODY_TRANSFER`) |
| `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT` | P5 event | `{tick, surviving_body, absorbed_body}` (raised by BM3) | Pipeline `JOLT_PX` trigger list, Adaptive Audio & Tells (Designed, `adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`) |
| `BODY_HULL_SEPARATION` | P5 event | `{tick, old_body, new_body}` (raised by BM4) | Pipeline `JOLT_PX`, Adaptive Audio & Tells (Designed) |
| `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP`, `diag.actor_in_footprint` | counter; debug assert | actor-ticks on which the exemption was evaluated non-empty, plus one per detection event whose box test found `E` non-empty (BDY-16; exact deltas in AC-BDY-27, 29c, 104 and 130; counting rule amended 2026-10-27 — design review and re-review) | Debug Overlay; QA gate (0 in a clean build) |
| `BDY_ORPHAN_ACTOR`, `BDY_OCTANT_INVALID`, `BDY_VEFF_INVALID`, `BDY_SEAT_UNBOUND`, `BDY_WORLD_LIMIT`, `BDY_TRANSFER_NO_SECTION`, `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH`, `BDY_TRAPPED_ACTOR` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `BDY_TRAPPED_ACTOR` added)*, `BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)* | defect counters | counts only | Debug Overlay |
| per-actor sweep cost | log | microseconds per actor step; logged above `SWEEP_COST_LOG_MS` | Debug Overlay, perf gate |

Nothing in this table is in the block or in the hashed set, and every event is a notification (it carries no state a subscriber could use to desynchronise the simulation). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **`frame_delta_px`, defined once.** It is the displacement the transfer applies to the actor's `pos_curr`, signed NEW minus OLD (the same convention as D.8's `delta_abs`): `+frame_shift_px` on a weld (`BODY_TRANSFER{weld}`), `-frame_shift_px` on a cut (`BODY_TRANSFER{detach}`), and `to_pos - pos_curr(old)` on a pod hop (`BODY_TRANSFER{pod}`). The Pipeline REBASES by it on a weld and a detach (C5: the camera solver shifts `f` and its history into the new body's frame and keeps the lag, so the avatar's screen position does not move; registry `camera_cut_predicates`), and cuts on a pod hop and wherever the new body's camera bounds clamp the rebased target. Body only supplies the value; AC-BDY-76 asserts the payload and AC-BDY-118 the Pipeline's result.

### Interactions with Other Systems

*Disk state used throughout (Glob of `design/gdd/`, 2026-10-26, at the moment this section was written):* designed — Command & Simulation Layer, Life-Support Graph, Item & Module Database, Player Controller & Input, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera, Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query, 2D Lighting & Occlusion, Failure & Trail, Save/Load & Versioned Format, Suit & Equipment, Interaction System, Ship Section & Layout Data Model (the last designed earlier in this same run). Derelict Authoring (`derelict-authoring-handmade-kits.md`) and Attention (`attention-system.md`) are on disk with status In Design (siblings in flight); Modular Ship Assembly, Web Map & Transit, Horror Entities, Boarding & Salvage, Sanity, Perception Filter and Debug Overlay have no file (Modular Ship Assembly and Web Map & Transit are being designed concurrently in this run), so every contract with a neighbour that is undesigned or still In Design is **provisional** and names what that GDD must repeat (the LSG:1140 / LY:405 pattern). *Refreshed at the consistency pass 2026-10-27: Modular Ship Assembly, Web Map & Transit, Horror Entities, Debug Overlay, Adaptive Audio & Tells and HUD & Suit Layer are on disk with status Designed; the rows that their routed asks name are updated below, and the remaining status words above are as of 2026-10-26.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **status as on disk at the review, Glob of `design/gdd/` 2026-10-27 — every neighbour this GDD names has a file with `Status: Designed`**: Command & Simulation Layer, Life-Support Graph, Item & Module Database, Player Controller & Input, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera, Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query, 2D Lighting & Occlusion, Failure & Trail, Save/Load & Versioned Format, Suit & Equipment, Interaction System, Ship Section & Layout Data Model, Modular Ship Assembly, Web Map & Transit, Horror Entities, Boarding & Salvage, Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Perception Filter, Attention, Derelict Authoring, Debug Overlay, Adaptive Audio & Tells, HUD & Suit Layer. The words "undesigned", "no file" and "In Design" in the rows below are the authoring-time state and are superseded by this sentence; "provisional" now means only that the neighbour's own text has not yet repeated a Body contract, which each row's status says.)* Each row quotes the designed neighbour's own sentence; "Body repeats" lists what the neighbour asked Body to repeat. Interface owners are named so that none of a neighbour's rows is read as Body's.

#### 4.1 Upstream (Body depends on these)

| System (status) | Data in | Data out | Interface owner and exact names | Neighbour's sentence (quoted) and Body's answer |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Command & Simulation Layer** (designed) | the 60 Hz tick (P3 hook slot), `WriteScope` for mutators, the block registry, dirty-key rules, the P5 notification batch | block registration (`body.state`), hook (`body.integrate`), `PROJECTOR` registration (`body.projector`), events | Kernel owns CR-3, CR-8, CR-9, CR-10, CR-12, CR-13; Body registers the three | KRN:198 (Interactions row 6): "Hook sampling physics results into its block each tick; `PROJECTOR` for layout -> physics; ship-local frame ... The determinism limit (G3) bites here; the tick-to-physics-step relationship and the `PROJECTOR` class need a TD ruling -> ADR". KRN:194 (row 2): "**Open boundary**: who owns character position. Proposal — Ship Body owns ship-local kinematic position, Controller owns intent and facing". **Body's answer:** position ownership is closed (Overview, BDY-11); with no physics results sampled, "hook sampling physics results" collapses to `body.integrate` and the `PROJECTOR` builds nothing physical (BDY-33); `body.state` is not physics-coupled, so kernel Open Question 5 (G3 scope) is moot for the MVP and a CR-13 wording note plus the KRN:198 row wording are routed to the kernel owner. **Body repeats:** it does not choose the tick source (Open Question 2); it is correct under either (BDY-32). KRN:197 (row 5): Assembly's `apply` "calls published mutators on Graph, Layout and Body" — Body publishes BM1-BM7 (BDY-21) |
| **Ship Section & Layout Data Model** (designed) | `solid_in(body_id, aabb)` and `rects_in` (BODY frame); `body_of(section_id)`; `section_offset(section_id)`; `sections_of(body_id)`; section template extents; `room_at` (passes through to Suit); the return records of `attach_section` and `detach_section` | `foot_boxes` as caller arguments only; nothing else (Layout reads nothing from `body.state`) | Layout owns geometry, `body_id`, membership, offsets, welds, hatches, leaves; Body owns pose and actors | LY-18 (LY:254): "Ship Body owns pose, velocity, actor positions, kinematics and any engine-side shapes built from Layout data. Body must repeat *(provisional — Ship Body & Interior Physics undesigned)*: its ownership of `body.state` (pose, velocity, actor positions); `restore_after: [layout.state]`; and the physics PROJECTOR (KRN:197, KRN:198). Layout reads nothing from `body.state` (acyclic)." **Body repeats all three** (BDY-5, BDY-33). Layout's Interactions row (LY:439) takes from Body "nothing from `body.state` (acyclic: Body is `restore_after` Layout; Layout never reads Body); foot boxes as arguments only". LY-16: Layout returns `{surviving_body_id, absorbed_body_id, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px}` "so Body can emit `BODY_TRANSFER` (PIPE:694) and keep the avatar's screen position where the layout allows — Body's concern" (BDY-22). LY-17: detach returns `{new_body_id, moved_sections[], frame_shift_px}` (BDY-23).
