LY-10 (LY:217-218): `body.integrate` after `layout.doors` — **registered by Body** (BDY-8, answers Layout Open Question 6). LY-12 (c): one `solid_in` query per actor per tick with the swept box (BDY-15). **ASKs (additive, provisional):** `section_at(body_id, px) -> section_id \| NONE` by containment over each section's own half-open cell set, disjoint by LY-14 and LY:207, so no tie rule exists (BDY-29); Layout's PROJECTOR parents its section nodes under Body's per-body root (BDY-33); the foot-origin wording of Layout's gap notes at LY:1447 and LY:1478 reads stale after BDY-14 (optional refresh). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **all three ANSWERED**, confirmed on disk: `section_at` is LY-28's read (L312); Layout's Dependencies row lists "Body's per-body root `Node2D` parents Layout's section nodes (BDY-33)" (L996); the foot-origin gap notes now read "RESOLVED by Ship Body BDY-14" (L1459, OQ-7 L1490). New additive asks raised by this review: `solid_in_into` (BDY-15), a stated cost for `section_at` (BDY-29), a pure detach preview returning `frame_shift_px` (BDY-21); Open Questions 32, 34 and 36.)* The `MAX_SECTIONS_PER_BODY` cap and the `CAPACITY` reason of `can_detach` are Layout's and Assembly's (Layout Open Question 8); Body offers `body_count()` |
| **Player Controller & Input** (designed) | `octant` (0-7 or NONE) and `v_eff` (`gait_effective`) from `controller.state[seat]`; the ordering `controller.step` before `body.integrate` | `pos_curr`, `pos_prev`, `odometer` (read view), `BODY_TRANSFER` records; `blocked` goes to PRESENTATION and DIAGNOSTIC subscribers only (BDY-19); the derived `moved` is offered to a Controller hook ordered after `body.integrate` (CP-3) | Controller owns intent, octant, gait, `v_eff`, facing, locomotion state, carry and channel references, chest point, hand point; Body owns ship-local integer position | PC-14 (PCI:143): "**Adopt: Ship Body owns ship-local integer position (`pos`, `sub`, `odometer`, `LocomotionStep`); the Controller Sim owns intent, gait, committed aim, facing, locomotion state, carry and channel references, chest point and hand point.**" ... "the foot box (16 x 8) and the 1/256 fixed point are Body's to confirm." PCI:103: "The avatar has no physics body." PCI:202 (Interactions row 2): the 12 tests of PC-18 "are Body's to implement". PCI:650: "the body is never displaced". PCI:651: "Body assigns the actor to the body whose section contains the foot origin on the detach tick — a foot box straddling the seam is not split." **Body confirms** the foot box (16 x 8, with a top-centre origin, BDY-14), the 1/256 fixed point, `trunc` rounding and the `tests/unit/body/` location (PCI:1087), and takes the 12 PC-18 tests as its own (test 10 re-fixtured, BDY-31). **Routed CONFLICTS (wording, additive):** the foot-origin lines (BDY-14), the restore lines (BDY-31), the STAGGERED `sub` line PCI:690 and PCI:990 (BDY-12), `after: controller.step` refreshed to `[controller.step, layout.doors]` in PC-14, PC-15 and PCI:145 (BDY-8). **CONFLICT (resolved 2026-10-27) — noise ordering:** PCI:648 and PCI:926 / PCI D.13 have `controller.step` push running noise as zero when `moved == (0, 0)` against a wall, but `controller.step` runs BEFORE `body.integrate` in P3, so this tick's `moved` does not exist yet. The gameplay-programmer proposes the Controller read the previous tick's `moved` (a one-tick lag, invisible at the Attention intake's divisor 6); the technical-director holds that anything a SIM hook reads is sim state that must be persisted and hashed, so `blocked` stays presentation- and diagnostic-only (BDY-19). **Resolution (CP-3; ANSWERED — the Controller adopted option (b), hook `controller.noise`, PCI PC-15, D.13):** Body publishes `moved(actor_id) := pos_curr - pos_prev` as a DERIVED read (no persisted field, no schema change), SIM-readable only from a hook ordered `after: [body.integrate]`, where it is a difference of two tick-boundary persisted values and therefore G4-exact; `blocked` stays presentation- and diagnostic-only, and the "requested" half is the Controller's own `octant` and `v_eff`, so a Controller hook can derive "requested but not moved" from `moved`. The Controller owner adopted (b): a second divisor-1 Controller hook, the registered `controller.noise` (`after: [body.integrate]`; divisor 1 because PCI:926 needs one stimulus per moving tick and Attention's divisor-6 intake cannot carry it), which reads `moved(actor_id)` and pushes the WALK / RUN / CARRY class to Attention. Option (a), persisting `moved` in `body.state`, is NOT adopted. **Dropped (CP-4):** the proposed read-view stamp for a same-tick clear of `channel_ref` on a P2 transfer is not adopted; that clear is reachable by CR-9's cross-block pattern (Assembly's `apply` may call a Controller-published mutator in the same `WriteScope`) and is Controller and Assembly business, so Body needs no stamp |
| **Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera** (designed) | `tick_alpha`; the PR-4 canvas rules; the C2 hard constraints; the C5 camera-reset rule; the definition of `y_foot` | `world_pose`; the per-body root and canvas offset (via `body.projector`); the loaded body's bounding rectangle as the camera-limits rectangle published to the Pipeline solver (never written to `Camera2D.limit_*`, ADR-0009 OQ-C); `BODY_TRANSFER`, `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT`, `BODY_HULL_SEPARATION` | Pipeline owns frame composition and placement; Body owns the transforms and the frame | PIPE row 8 (PIPE:297): "the interior scene sits at the world-canvas origin in the ship-local frame; ship motion appears only on exterior/transit layers; **ship rotation is never rendered inside**; bodies are axis-aligned; the one-interpolator rule (engine physics interpolation off for sim-owned transforms; whether debris may use it is Body's)". PIPE:153 C2 (i): "the render rotation of every human-built body is 0". PIPE:727: "If physics ever places `y_foot` inside `[y_0, y_0 + 24)` the actor is behind the wall by D.12 and visibly wrong — a Ship Body collision defect, caught by the debug tree-walk's 'actor inside footprint' check." PIPE:694: "the new body's frame is placed so the avatar's screen position is unchanged where the layout allows, making the cut invisible; where it does not, it is a cut, never a pan." **Body's answers:** rotation is locked (`heading_q` 0, BDY-26); `y_foot` is the Pipeline's floored presentation foot row `p.y` (PIPE D.9, PIPE:535), equal to `pos_curr.y` at rest, with the first-legal-row corollary (BDY-14); the one-interpolator rule is honoured and debris is the ADR-BODY-05 candidate; Body supplies `frame_delta_px` in `BODY_TRANSFER` and the Pipeline decides whether the screen position moved *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: Pipeline C5 and the registry's `camera_cut_predicates` make a weld or detach transfer that carries `frame_delta_px` a REBASE, not a cut; Body defines the sign in 3.7)*. Pipeline Open Question 3 (position ownership) is closed here. **Body relies on** the kernel's `snap_baseline` on entry to `RUNNING` and the camera CUT on a `load_seq` change for restore continuity; there is no Pipeline-driven or camera-driven `post_restore` `previous := current` (C5). Body's own derived `pos_prev := pos_curr` (and `world_prev := world`) reset in `post_restore` is block-internal and is not that mechanism (BDY-6, BDY-31) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync; sync knock-on fix)* |

## Formulas

**Conventions for every formula below.** All hashed or replicated quantities are integers (kernel determinism rule; BDY-32); lengths are base px and identical at every ladder step (BDY-35); rectangles are half-open `[x0, x1) x [y0, y1)` (LY-7); `trunc` toward zero is the sub-pixel rule and FLOOR division is the px-to-tile rule, never mixed (BDY-35). The only float in this section is `tick_alpha` inside the presentation-only D.3, and nothing in D.3 is hashed. Every sibling formula is cited with its owner's prefix (`PCI D.3`, `PCI D.4`, `PCI D.5`, `PIPE D.9`, `PIPE D.12`, `LY D.5`, `LY D.6`, `LY D.12`) and is never restated with a different value; Body's own formulas are bare `D.n`. Registry names are used verbatim (`V_WALK`, `V_RUN`, `V_CARRY`, `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH`, `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS`, `MAX_BODIES`, `MAX_FOOTPRINT_RECTS_PER_BODY`, `STEP_BUDGET_MS`, `layout_dirty_key_encoding`, `weld_offset`, `detach_rebase`, `tick_alpha`); registry line numbers are never printed. The three transfer formulas D.4 to D.6 are registered together as `body_transfer`; D.9 as `interior_invariance`; D.2 as `foot_box`; D.1 re-sources `locomotion_axis_step` (Cross-References 8.2). Fixtures reused from the neighbours: the Layout airlock (`LY D.6`: home socket E-facing at `(696, 240)`, derelict socket W-facing at `(120, 144)`, `frame_shift_px (576, 96)`), the Layout chain (`LY D.12`: sections 1 `(0, 0)`, 3 `(576, 96)`, 4 `(1056, 96)`), the Controller's wall fixtures (PCI:908, PCI:909) and the Pipeline's wall row (PIPE:543).

### D.1 Axis step

The axis step formula is defined as:

`sub_a += UNIT[o].a * v_eff;  step_a = trunc(sub_a / 256);  sub_a -= step_a * 256;  moved_a = sweep_a(step_a)`   (axis `a` in {x, y}, x first then y; the position advances by `moved_a`)

This is PCI D.3's expression verbatim (PCI:275), re-sourced to this GDD. Both axes' residues advance BEFORE either sweep (BDY-9), so a refused x step never changes `sub_y`, and a refused step stores no energy and loses none.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `o` | int | 0 to 7 (the move octant of D.14; NONE never reaches this step) | `controller.state.octant`, or Body's own `last_octant` in the provisional decel ladder (D.14) | movement direction |
| `v_eff` | int | 0 to 3 (clamped into `[0, 3]` for the tick, BDY-9) | `controller.state` `gait_effective` (PCI D.4) | whole base px per tick requested this tick: `V_WALK`, `V_RUN` or `V_CARRY` as the Controller resolves it |
| `UNIT[o].a` | int | {-256, -181, 0, 181, 256} | the `UNIT` table of PCI D.3 (256 cardinal, 181 diagonal; cited, not restated) | per-axis velocity scale in 1/256 px |
| `sub_a` | int | -255 to 255 | `body.state` `actors[].sub` (persisted) | sub-pixel accumulator for axis `a`, 1/256 px |
| `step_a` | int | -3 to 3 | calculated | whole px requested on axis `a` before collision |
| `moved_a` | int | -3 to 3, `abs(moved_a) <= abs(step_a)` | calculated by the sweep (BDY-15) | whole px actually moved after the 1-px sweep against Layout's `solid_in` set |

**Output Range:** `abs(step_a) <= 3` per tick. Cardinal motion leaves `sub` at 0 on every tick (`step = v_eff`). A diagonal tick moves `(k, k)` in magnitude, so a diagonal is always exactly 45 degrees; over 256 consecutive ticks in one octant the per-axis displacement is exactly `181 * v_eff` px for a diagonal and exactly `v_eff` px per tick for a cardinal, with `sub` returning to 0. `trunc` (not `floor`) is mandatory so that west mirrors east. `odometer += (abs(moved_x) + abs(moved_y)) * ODO_W[move octant]` after collision (`ODO_W` 256 cardinal, 181 diagonal; PCI:491), so a wall adds 0 and a diagonal slide `moved = (0, -1)` adds 181.

**Example:** NE walk at `v_eff` 2 from `sub = (0, 0)`: the increment is `181 * 2 = 362` per axis per tick. `sub.x` runs 362 (step 1, residue 106), 468 (step 1, residue 212), 574 (step 2, residue 62), 424 (step 1, residue 168), 530 (step 2, residue 18), 380 (step 1, residue 124), 486 (step 1, residue 230), 592 (step 2, residue 80): steps **1, 1, 2, 1, 2, 1, 1, 2**, the cadence printed in PCI D.3. West mirror: NW at `v_eff` 1 from rest: tick 1 `sub.x = -181`, `step = trunc(-181 / 256) = 0`; tick 2 `sub.x = -362`, `step = -1`, residue `-362 + 256 = -106`; the east twin reads `(0, 181)` then `(1, 106)`, an exact mirror (a `floor` step would read `-1` and `+75` on tick 1).

### D.2 Foot box

The foot box formula is defined as:

`foot_box(pos) = [pos.x - FOOT_BOX_W/2, pos.x + FOOT_BOX_W/2) x [pos.y, pos.y + FOOT_BOX_H)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `pos` | int pair | body frame, may be negative after a detach re-base | `body.state` `pos_curr` | the foot origin: horizontally centred, and the box's TOP row (BDY-14) |
| `FOOT_BOX_W` | int | 16 (even; safe range 8 to 16 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the upper bound was 24; it is Layout's 16-px east-west passage extent, LY D.5, unless LY D.5 changes in the same edit; and `BROADPHASE_CELL_PX >= FOOT_BOX_W + 3`, Tuning Knobs)*) | Body-registered, confirming PCI:103 | box width, even so that `pos.x` is a cell boundary and west mirrors east |
| `FOOT_BOX_H` | int | 8 (safe range 4 to 8 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the upper bound was 16; it is Layout's 8-px north-south passage extent, LY D.5, unless LY D.5 changes in the same edit)*) | Body-registered, confirming PCI:103 | box height, hanging south from the origin row |

**Output Range:** a half-open rectangle of exactly `FOOT_BOX_W * FOOT_BOX_H` = 128 cells; its lowest-indexed cell is `(pos.x - 8, pos.y)` and its highest `(pos.x + 7, pos.y + 7)`. A committed `pos` whose box intersects a SOLID rectangle is a collision defect unless BDY-16 applies.

**Example:** `pos = (304, 384)` gives `[296, 312) x [384, 392)`, the close-safety box of the Layout door fixture (`LY D.5`). PCI:908 under the top-centre origin: `pos = (100, 200)` gives `[92, 108) x [200, 208)`; against the wall footprint `[108, 132) x [150, 250)` the rightmost box column is 107, so `pos.x` can never exceed 100 and the x axis is refused every tick while y keeps sliding. PCI:909: wall `[0, 400) x [100, 124)`, `pos = (200, 140)` walking north at `v_eff` 3: `pos.y` runs 137, 134, 131, 128, 125, then the sixth tick requests 3 and the contact at box top 124 admits 1, so `pos.y = 124` and holds.

### D.3 Foot row and the first legal row

The foot row formula is defined as:

`y_foot = p.y,  p = floor(pos_prev + (pos_curr - pos_prev) * tick_alpha)  (PIPE D.9);  at rest y_foot = pos_curr.y;  legal rest rows:  y >= y_0 + WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH  (wall to the north)  and  y <= y_1 - FOOT_BOX_H  (wall to the south)`

`y_foot` is the Pipeline's identity (PIPE:535, PIPE D.12), cited and not registered by Body. It is a floored presentation value `p.y`; it equals `pos_curr.y` only when `pos_prev.y == pos_curr.y` (a stop, `post_restore`, a transfer) or at `tick_alpha` 1. The foot origin is the box's top row, which is what makes the first-legal-row corollary literal at rest.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `pos_prev`, `pos_curr` | int pair | body px | `body.state` read view (PRESENTATION subscribers may read, CR-12) | committed positions of the previous and current tick |
| `tick_alpha` | float | 0.0 to 1.0 | registry `tick_alpha` (kernel) | interpolation fraction; presentation only, never hashed |
| `p.y` | int | body px | PIPE D.9 | the floored integer row the Presenter writes; the Y-sort key of PIPE D.12 |
| `y_0` | int | multiple of 24 | Layout rectangles | north edge of a wall's floor footprint (rows `[y_0, y_0 + 24)`) lying NORTH of the actor |
| `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` | int | 24 (set once) | registry; owner: the art bible | footprint depth, the collision rows of a wall |
| `y_1` | int | multiple of 24 | Layout rectangles | north edge of a footprint lying SOUTH of the actor |
| `FOOT_BOX_H` | int | 8 | D.2 | box height |

**Output Range:** integer rows. An actor south of a wall rests with `pos.y = y_foot = y_0 + 24`, the first legal row south of the wall (PIPE:543, PIPE:727); an actor north of a wall rests with its box bottom flush, `pos.y = y_1 - 8`. The asymmetry (24 rows of stand-off on one side, 8 on the other) follows from the origin being the box's top row and is the known art risk (Visual/Audio Requirements).

**Example:** wall footprint rows `[240, 264)` (PIPE:543). South of the wall: `y_foot = 264`, which sorts equal to the wall (`sort_y = 264`), so the layer-order tie-break draws the actor over the face. North of the wall: `pos.y = 240 - 8 = 232`, box rows `[232, 240)`, `y_foot = 232 < 264`, so the actor is hidden behind the face by PIPE D.12, correct. Mid-stride, `pos_prev.y = 130`, `pos_curr.y = 128`, `tick_alpha` 0.25: `p.y = floor(130 - 0.5) = floor(129.5) = 129` (`round` would give 130). **Wording note (routed to the Pipeline owner, low severity):** PIPE:543's illustrative "actor at `y_foot = 239`, just north" is not a reachable rest row under any foot-box placement, because a box starting at row 239 covers rows 239 to 246 and so intersects the footprint rows 240 to 246 (`diag.actor_in_footprint`); the predicate it illustrates still holds at 232.

### D.4 Weld transfer

The weld transfer formula (`body_transfer`, weld case, BM3) is defined as:

`for each actor a with body_id == absorbed:  pos_curr' = pos_curr + frame_shift_px;  body_id' = surviving_body_id;  pos_prev' = pos_curr';  sub' = sub;  last_octant' = last_octant;   host-body actors unchanged;   the absorbed pose row is deleted`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `frame_shift_px` | int pair | on Layout's 24-px grid (`LY D.6`), may be negative | Layout's `attach_section` return record (LY-16): `offset_b - offset_b_old` | the absorbed body's frame origin expressed in the host's frame; every absorbed section gains it |
| `pos_curr` | int pair | absorbed body frame | `body.state` | the actor's foot origin before the weld |
| `surviving_body_id`, `absorbed_body_id` | int | live Layout ids, distinct | Layout return record | the host keeps its id (the home-ship socket is passed first, LY-16) |
| `n_moved` | int | 0 to `MAX_ACTORS` | calculated | actors on the absorbed body |

**Output Range:** host-frame coordinates; `sub` and `last_octant` untouched because a whole-pixel shift cannot disturb a residue. **Preservation property P-W:** for every moved actor `actor_section` and `actor_local_px` (D.10) are identical before and after, because the absorbed sections' offsets gain exactly `frame_shift_px` and so do the positions. Dirty keys: one `ACT` per moved actor plus one `BODY` for the deleted pose row (D.13). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The event payload's `frame_delta_px` is `+frame_shift_px` (3.7), the value the Pipeline rebases by.

**Example (`LY D.6` airlock, `frame_shift_px = (576, 96)`):** an actor on the derelict body at `(100, 50)` in section 3 (pre-weld offset `(0, 0)`) becomes `(676, 146)` on the home-ship body; `actor_local_px` before `(100, 50) - (0, 0) = (100, 50)`, after `(676, 146) - (576, 96) = (100, 50)`. A host actor at `(300, 100)` is untouched and raises no `BODY_TRANSFER`. If the absorbed body had been welded before (`offset_b_old != (0, 0)`), `frame_shift_px` is the difference, not the new offset.

### D.5 Detach transfer

The detach transfer formula (`body_transfer`, detach case, BM4) is defined as:

`offset_pre(s) = section_offset(s) + frame_shift_px  if s in moved_sections[]  else  section_offset(s);   side(a) = NEW  iff  pos_curr(a) in cells(s) + offset_pre(s)  for some s in moved_sections[];   NEW actors:  pos_curr' = pos_curr - frame_shift_px,  body_id' = new_body_id,  pos_prev' = pos_curr',  sub' = (0, 0),  last_octant' = last_octant;   KEEP actors unchanged;   world_new = world_old + frame_shift_px,  vel_new = vel_old + drift`

BM4 runs AFTER Layout's `detach_section` (Assembly's call order; LY-16, LY-17), so Layout has already re-based the moved sections; Body recovers the pre-cut geometry by adding `frame_shift_px` back for `moved_sections[]` only (the call-order trap, BDY-23). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)* BM4 first evaluates the cap guard: an actor on the old body whose box overlaps `cap_rects[]` makes it return `-BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP` with nothing written (BDY-24 (iii)). The foot ORIGIN picks the side; a box straddling the seam is not split.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `moved_sections[]` | list of section ids | non-empty | Layout's `detach_section` return record (LY-17) | the sections now in `C_new` |
| `frame_shift_px` | int pair | on the 24-px grid | the same record; `offset_old(m)` for `m = min(section_id)` over `C_new` (`LY D.12`) | the new frame origin expressed in the old frame; moved sections LOSE it |
| `cells(s)` | half-open cell set, section-local | the section's rooms and the rectangles it owns, incl. its own 24-px hull footprint and hatch rectangles | Layout template | containment is by floor area, never by bounding box |
| `pos_curr(a)` | int pair | old body frame | `body.state` | the foot origin on the detach tick |
| `world_old`, `vel_old` | int pair | D.7 ranges | `body.state` pose row | the old body's pose |
| `drift` | int pair | magnitude `DETACH_DRIFT_PX_PER_TICK` (default 0, exterior only) | Body-owned | added to `vel_new` along the separation axis, away from the old body (BDY-28) |

**Output Range:** new-body coordinates for NEW actors; KEEP actors untouched. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The event payload's `frame_delta_px` is `-frame_shift_px` (3.7), the value the Pipeline rebases by. **Preservation property P-D:** moved actors keep `(actor_section, actor_local_px)` exactly. **Conservation:** with `abs(a) = world(body_of(a)) + pos_curr(a)`, detach conserves `abs` exactly for every actor on both sides (at default drift 0, and drift touches `vel` only). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Range guard.** The formula's `world_new = world_old + frame_shift_px` and `vel_new = vel_old + drift` must stay inside the D.7 ranges or the next autosave is rejected (`BDY_RESTORE_POSE_RANGE`): `can_rebase_detach(old_body_id, frame_shift_px)` answers `BDY_REBASE_POSE_OUT_OF_RANGE` / `BDY_VEL_OUT_OF_RANGE` before the cut, and an `apply` that was not guarded applies BDY-23's two-case net and returns `OK` (world overflow: `world` saturated per axis and `vel` set to `(0, 0)`; velocity overflow: `vel` saturated per axis; counted `BDY_WORLD_LIMIT` where applicable and `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH`), waiving the `abs` conservation of the exterior pose on that defect branch only. **Example:** `world_old = (4,194,304, 0)` and `frame_shift_px = (576, 96)` gives `world_new.x = 4,194,880`, out of range: validate rejects the cut; an unguarded apply stores `(4,194,304, 96)` with `vel (0, 0)`, and `restore(snapshot())` accepts it. An origin lying in no section (an exterior gap, a defect) stays on `C_keep` and counts `BDY_TRANSFER_NO_SECTION`. A weld followed by a cut of the same seam is not in general an identity on `pos_curr` (BDY-23), so round-trip criteria assert `abs` and the section-local position, never raw `pos_curr`.

**Example (`LY D.12` chain: sections 1 `(0, 0)`, 3 `(576, 96)`, 4 `(1056, 96)` on body 1, cut at seam (1, 3)):** `frame_shift_px = (576, 96)`, new offsets 3 `(0, 0)`, 4 `(480, 0)`. Reconstruction: `offset_pre(3) = (0, 0) + (576, 96) = (576, 96)`, `offset_pre(4) = (480, 0) + (576, 96) = (1056, 96)`, `offset_pre(1) = (0, 0)`. An actor at old-body `(600, 200)` (section-3 local `(24, 104)`, assuming that cell lies in a section-3 room: the fixture fixes offsets, not extents) moves to `(24, 104)`; an actor in section 4 at `(1100, 150)` moves to `(524, 54)` (local `(44, 54)` before and after); an actor in section 1 at `(300, 100)` stays. An actor with origin `(690, 282)` lies in section 1's east wall footprint `[672, 696)` and its box reaches column 697 into section 3's footprint `[696, 720)`: the seam's hatch rectangles overlap it, so BM4 called with `cap_rects[]` = those two plates returns `-BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP` and writes nothing (BDY-24 (iii)); the side rule alone (BM4 with `cap_rects[]` empty, the unit-test surface) would keep it on `C_keep` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)*. With `world_old = (1000, 500)` the new pose row is `(1576, 596)`; the section-3 actor's `abs` is `(1000, 500) + (600, 200) = (1600, 700)` before and `(1576, 596) + (24, 104) = (1600, 700)` after.

### D.6 Pod transfer

The pod transfer formula (`body_transfer`, pod case, BM5; Web Map & Transit is Designed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was "provisional — undesigned")*) is defined as:

`pos_curr' = to_pos;  body_id' = to_body_id;  pos_prev' = pos_curr';  sub' = (0, 0);  last_octant' = last_octant;  carry_ref untouched (the Controller's)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `to_pos` | int pair | a cell whose foot box (D.2) is clear of every SOLID rectangle of `to_body_id` | Transit's `apply` argument (the arrival cell is Transit's; Body never searches) | destination foot origin in the destination body's frame; for a single-section pod body this is `spawn_local + section_offset(pod_section)` with offset `(0, 0)` |
| `to_body_id` | int | a live Layout id (the pod is a Layout body, BDY-25) | Transit | destination body |

**Output Range:** a legal standing cell, validated by `can_transfer` (`BDY_PLACE_INSIDE_FOOTPRINT` otherwise); `sub` is zeroed because a transfer is a re-placement, which does not conflict with BDY-31 (a restore is not a transfer).

**Example (numbers illustrative; the pod template's spawn cell is Authoring's, DA-3 `(72, 48)`, and Transit's *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*):** a pod spawn local `(72, 48)`, offset `(0, 0)`: `pos_curr = (72, 48)`, `pos_prev = (72, 48)`, `sub = (0, 0)`; two crewmates arriving at one cell is legal (BDY-17); an arrival cell inside a footprint is rejected, not nudged.

### D.7 World pose integration and canvas offset

The world pose integration formula is defined as:

`for b in ascending body_id (rows present):  if  abs(world_x + vel_x) <= WORLD_COORD_LIMIT  and  abs(world_y + vel_y) <= WORLD_COORD_LIMIT  then  world' = world + vel  else  vel' = (0, 0), world' = world, BDY_WORLD_LIMIT += 1;     canvas_offset(b) = world(b) - world(avatar_body)`

One add per tick, before any actor steps (BDY-9). A row absent reads `(0, 0, 0, 0)` and is not touched; a zero velocity on a zero-world row deletes the row (sparse form). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Interpolated render offset (presentation only; the only floats here are `tick_alpha`; nothing below is hashed or persisted):** `world_prev(b) := world(b)` immediately before the add above, and reset to the new `world(b)` on every discontinuous write (BM7, BM4's new row, `post_restore`, a deleted sparse row, the freeze; BDY-26); `off_curr(b) = world(b) - world(avatar_body)`, `off_prev(b) = world_prev(b) - world_prev(avatar_body)` (the CURRENT avatar body for both terms, so a BM5 origin switch never interpolates across two frames of reference); `canvas_offset_render(b, tick_alpha) = floor(off_prev + (off_curr - off_prev) * tick_alpha)` per axis (PIPE D.9 floor, one floored integer per body per frame, PIPE PR-4 rule 2). **Worked cases.** (1) *Mid-leg re-origin (BM7 on every live body in one apply, WM-6):* pod B moves at `vel (3, 0)` with `world (900, 240)`, avatar body A at `world (0, 0)`; Transit translates both by `V = (-600, 0)`: A to `(-600, 0)`, B to `(300, 240)`, both `world_prev` reset to those values. The frame after the apply has `off_prev = off_curr = (900, 240)` for B (the difference of the two new poses), so `canvas_offset_render` is `(900, 240)` at every `tick_alpha`: it does not jump by `|V| = 600`, and the next tick adds `(3, 0)` to B only, so the offset continues 900 to 903 smoothly (a common translation leaves differences unchanged; the reset costs B one tick of its 3-px motion, once). (2) *A single-body pose set (a `berth` or `pod_settle` snap):* B at `(900, 240)` is set to `(912, 240)` with A untouched at `(0, 0)`. With the reset `off_prev = off_curr = (912, 240)` and the offset steps once; without it `off_prev` would still be `(900, 240)` and the hull would slide 12 px across the following frame, a smear across a discontinuity the exterior layer was never meant to animate. The freeze sentence of D.7's output range ("never clamps a coordinate") has one stated exception, the guarded BM4 row of BDY-23.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `world` | int pair, base px | `abs(world_*) <= WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` 2^22 = 4,194,304 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | `body.state`; set directly only by BM7 `set_body_pose` (Transit) and BM4 (`world_old + frame_shift_px`) | locates the body-frame origin in the exterior frame |
| `vel` | int pair, base px per tick | `abs(vel_*) <= VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` 16 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | BM6, or BM4 copying the old body's `vel` | whole px per tick; no sub-pixel exterior residue in schema v1 |
| `canvas_offset(b)` | int pair, base px | `abs <= 2^23` | calculated; PIPE PR-4 rule 2 | where the per-body root sits on the world canvas relative to the avatar's body at the origin; one floored offset per body |
| `avatar_body` | int | a live body id | the local seat's actor row | the body whose frame is the canvas origin |

**Output Range:** integers only. The 2^22 bound exists so a canvas offset (a difference of two world coordinates, within +/-2^23) stays inside the range a float32 `Node2D.position` represents exactly (2^24) **[UNV: build precision]** (ADR-BODY-03). The freeze is the deterministic defence: it never clamps a coordinate *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: except the guarded BM4 row of BDY-23)*, and a mis-scripted leg is Transit's lint, not a Body projection.

**Example:** avatar body A `world (0, 0)`; pod B `world (600, 240)`, `vel (3, 0)`: after 100 ticks B is at `(900, 240)` and `canvas_offset(B) = (900, 240)`; after 3,600 ticks (60 s) `world.x` has moved 10,800 px. Limit: a body at `world_x = 4,194,000` with `vel_x = 16` reaches 4,194,304 after 19 ticks (exactly the bound, admitted); on tick 20 the sum would be 4,194,320, so `vel` becomes `(0, 0)`, `world` stays 4,194,304 and `BDY_WORLD_LIMIT` increments exactly once. The interior hash contribution is unchanged throughout (D.9).

### D.8 Docking invariant

The docking invariant formula is defined as:

`docked(a, b, frame_shift_px) = ( vel(a) == vel(b) )  and  ( world(b) - world(a) == frame_shift_px );     delta_abs = frame_shift_px - ( world(b) - world(a) )`   (`a` = host, `b` = absorbed)

`delta_abs` is the change in an absorbed actor's `abs` position at the weld (`abs_after - abs_before`) and equally the jump the absorbed hull makes in the exterior layer; its sign is defined once, here: **new minus old**. A weld never reads `docked` inside Body (BDY-27); it is a pure read that gates nothing in Body, and the OFFER of a player-origin weld is gated on it by Transit and Assembly (a named must-repeat, 8.3).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `frame_shift_px` | int pair | on the 24-px grid | Layout's `can_attach` / `LY D.6` for the candidate seam, passed as an argument (only Layout can compute it) | the shift the weld will apply (three arguments, CP-7) |
| `world(a)`, `world(b)` | int pair | D.7 ranges | `body.state` | exterior poses of the two frames' origins |
| `vel(a)`, `vel(b)` | int pair | D.7 ranges | `body.state` | exterior velocities |
| `delta_abs` | int pair | difference of a 24-grid value and a world difference | calculated | zero iff the position half of the invariant holds |

**Output Range:** boolean for `docked`; `delta_abs = (0, 0)` when the position half holds, so the visual jump at weld is zero. When it does not hold the weld still succeeds in the simulation and the absorbed hull jumps by `delta_abs` in the exterior layer only; a player-origin weld is only offered once the invariant holds (a named must-repeat, answered by MSA-12 and WM-21, 8.3), and cross-body light is OFF in the MVP (EM-30). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier "or the Pipeline hides the jump with its C5 cut" is struck, because C5 resets the camera and hides camera motion, not the absorbed hull's jump in the exterior layer; the offer gate is the only guard, BDY-27.)*

**Example (`LY D.6` airlock, `frame_shift_px = (576, 96)`):** `world(a) = (1000, 500)`, `world(b) = (1576, 596)`, equal `vel`: `world(b) - world(a) = (576, 96)`, `docked` true, `delta_abs = (0, 0)`. With `world(b) = (1600, 596)`: the difference is `(600, 96)`, `docked` false, `delta_abs = (576, 96) - (600, 96) = (-24, 0)`: the absorbed hull jumps 24 px west in the exterior layer when the weld lands, and no interior position changes.

### D.9 Interior invariance

The interior invariance formula is defined as:

`for all ticks t:   H_actors( t | vel = v, world = w )  ==  H_actors( t | vel = 0, world = 0 )   for every admissible (v, w)`

Equivalently, the interior view is a function with no argument in the `bodies` table: `interior_view(actors, layout.state)`. This is the registered rule `interior_invariance`.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `H_actors` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | bytes | `canonical_encode(payload["actors"])` = tag 0x07, u64 count `n_actors * 10`, then 9 bytes per int (3 actors: 9 + 30 * 9 = 279 bytes) | the kernel's `canonical_encode` helper (D.12) applied to the `actors` key of `body.state`'s `snapshot()` payload; compared per KEY, not per table: the kernel computes no per-table hash | the actor table's contribution |
| `v`, `w` | int pairs | D.7 ranges | the acceptance fixture | any admissible exterior velocity and pose |

**Output Range:** equality at every tick. The whole-block `block_hash` of `body.state` differs when `world_*` or `vel_*` differ, BY DESIGN (the `bodies` rows are part of the block); the criterion compares `canonical_encode(payload["actors"])` and the `layout.state` `block_hash` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*.

**Example (acceptance fixture):** give the avatar's body `vel = (3, 0)`, run 600 ticks of a scripted command stream, and run the identical stream at `vel = (0, 0)`: the `actors` bytes match at every tick, while the `bodies` row differs by `world.x = 1,800` at tick 600. A lint asserts that nothing reachable from `solid_in`, the actor loop of `body.integrate`, the Emitter, Suit or the interior camera pose names `world_` or `vel_`.

### D.10 Section lookup

The section lookup formula is defined as:

`actor_section(a) = s  iff  pos_curr(a) in cells(s) + section_offset(s),  s in sections_of(body_id(a));   NONE otherwise;     actor_local_px(a) = pos_curr(a) - section_offset(actor_section(a))`

**Containment, not precedence (CP-5).** `cells(s)` is each section's own half-open cell set (its rooms and the rectangles it owns, LY-7); LY-14's `GEOM_OVERLAP` test on every rect pair of a mate and the LY:207 `RECT_OVERLAP` lint make those sets disjoint across sections, so a point lies in at most one section and no tie rule exists or is needed.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `pos_curr(a)` | int pair | body frame | `body.state` | the foot ORIGIN (box top-centre) decides, not the box |
| `cells(s)` | half-open cell set, section-local | rooms plus owned rectangles, incl. the 24-px hull footprint and hatch rectangles | Layout template (*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Layout's `section_at(body_id, px)` (LY-28) publishes exactly this containment and is what Body calls; Body keeps no scan fallback, BDY-29) | the section's floor area |
| `section_offset(s)` | int pair | on the 24-px grid, may be negative after a detach | Layout | body-frame section origin |

**Output Range:** a section id or NONE; derived on demand at call time, never stored, any cache invalidated by every mutator and every restore (BDY-29). `actor_local_px` is SECTION-LOCAL (negative only if the geometry says so); Layout's `room_at(section_id, px)` takes it (LY-3 (c)) and floors (LY-3 (f)).

**Example (`LY D.6` airlock, sections 1 at `(0, 0)` and 3 at `(576, 96)`; A's east wall plate `[672, 696) x [240, 336)`, B's west wall plate `[696, 720) x [240, 336)` in body cells):** an origin at `(695, 280)` lies in `[672, 696)`, so section 1, `actor_local_px = (695, 280)`; an origin at `(696, 280)` lies in `[696, 720)`, so section 3, `actor_local_px = (696 - 576, 280 - 96) = (120, 184)`. That `x = 696` belongs to B is a consequence of half-open rectangles, not a rule. An origin in an exterior gap of the body answers NONE, a defect-only state whose consequence is exterior exposure (BDY-29, 8.3), never the last room's air.

### D.11 Overlap exemption and `actors_overlapping`

The overlap exemption formula is defined as:

`ov(box, r) = max(0, min(box.x1, r.x1) - max(box.x0, r.x0)) * max(0, min(box.y1, r.y1) - max(box.y0, r.y0));     E = { r SOLID : ov(foot_box(pos_start), r) > 0 }  (fixed for the tick);     a 1-px sub-step to box_after is admitted  iff  for every r in E:  ov(box_after, r) <= ov(box_before, r)`

Every rectangle not in `E` stays fully solid, so an exempt actor cannot walk into a wall it was not already inside. The test is **per rectangle, not total** (CP-1): on the `fixture_airlock` hull-line geometry a box astride two abutting exempt rectangles keeps its TOTAL overlap while stepping across their shared edge, which a total-area test would admit and the per-rectangle test refuses. Nothing shoves: `pos` is unchanged on every tick the actor does not step.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `box`, `box_before`, `box_after` | half-open rect | D.2 | `foot_box` at the sub-step | the foot box before and after one 1-px sub-step on either axis |
| `r` | half-open rect | body frame | Layout's `solid_in` result (SOLID filter) | a candidate rectangle |
| `ov(box, r)` | int | 0 to `FOOT_BOX_W * FOOT_BOX_H` = 128 | calculated | overlapped cells |
| `E` | set of rectangles | 0 to `K` (D.12) | calculated at the start of the actor's tick | rectangles the start box already intersects; empty on every ordinary tick |

**Output Range:** `E` empty means ordinary sweeping. For a PARTIAL overlap any sub-step with a component away from a rectangle shrinks that rectangle's `ov`, so the box leaves in a bounded number of ticks and can never deepen. A sub-step is also refused when its destination foot origin lies in no section of the actor's body (D.10), so no exempt step leaves the hull. Residuals (defect-only, BDY-16): a box wholly inside one rectangle has constant `ov` until an edge crosses out (it may exit either side provided the destination origin is in a section of the body); a box astride two abutting exempt rectangles can only slide along the shared edge or back out; a hatch-cap actor whose only shrinking direction leaves the body cannot leave (the cap trap). Each actor-tick on which `E` is evaluated non-empty (plus each detection event that finds `E` non-empty) increments `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` (BDY-16 counting rule) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Exposure of a footprint-resident exempt actor (stated once in BDY-16, repeated in BDY-29):** an exempt actor whose origin is inside a wall or hull-plate footprint of its own body has a section (`actor_section != NONE`) but `room_at(section, local)` answers NONE (Layout, LY-5), and Suit maps that to EXPOSED (SE:342); the guarantee is visibility, not safety, and a door-leaf exemption answers `sill_room` and is not exposed.

**Example (BDY-16 hatch cap, after a restore of a save recorded in that pose against the Layout state holding plate A SOLID)** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)***:** `pos = (690, 282)`, box `[682, 698) x [282, 290)`, plate `[672, 696) x [240, 336)`: `ov = (696 - 682) * 8 = 112`. West (box `[681, 697)`): `ov = 15 * 8 = 120 > 112`, refused. East (box `[683, 699)`): `13 * 8 = 104`, admitted. North or south: `14 * 8 = 112`, admitted. Walking east at `V_WALK` 2 the actor reaches `pos.x = 695` (692, 694, 695 over three ticks) and stops: the sub-step to 696 passes the overlap test (`ov` 64) but its destination origin `(696, 282)` lies in plate B's footprint, which left with its section and is in no section of this body, so exit containment refuses it. The actor rests in the cap at `pos.x = 695` (`ov = 72`), `actor_section` still answers section 1, and `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` counts every tick on which it presses a key (the cap trap; the exit it would have taken is the exterior face). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **At that resting pose `room_at(1, (695, 282))` answers NONE (inside plate A's footprint), so Suit reads EXPOSED: vacuum, anoxic, exterior temperature, on a pressurised ship.** This is the accepted residual, not a safe state (BDY-16). **The trap region is a strip, not the single point 695:** with box `[x - 8, x + 8) x [y, y + 8)` and plate `[672, 696) x [240, 336)`, the box straddles the plate's east edge for `x` 689 to 695 (at `x = 689`, box `[681, 697)`, `ov = 15 * 8 = 120`; west gives `[680, 696)`, `ov = 128`, refused; east gives `ov = 112`, admitted); for `x` 680 to 688 the box lies wholly inside the plate (`ov` constant at 128), west is admitted and the actor can walk back onto the floor, so those starts are residual (i), not the trap. In `y` the strip is `y` 240 to 328 (box rows wholly inside the plate's rows); at `y` 233 to 239 and 329 to 335 the neighbouring hull-wall rectangle joins `E` and the outcome depends on Layout's neighbour geometry, which the fixture does not fix. **Admitted-move set for each direction at `pos = (690, 282)`** (fixture assumption: the hull-wall footprint rectangles continue north and south of the plate and are not in `E` at the start): **east** is admitted for sub-steps to `x` 691 to 695 and refused at 696 (exit containment); **west** is refused at once (`ov` 120 > 112); **north** and **south** are admitted by the overlap test (`ov` stays 112) while the box rows stay inside `[240, 336)`, that is down to `y = 240` (north) and `y = 328` (south), and are refused at 239 and 329 by the non-`E` neighbour rectangles; an **east** input from anywhere in the strip converges to `x = 695`, a **north-east** input converges to `(695, 240)`, a **south-east** input to `(695, 328)`. The criterion asserts this full set (AC-BDY-29). **Abutting case:** `pos = (696, 282)` with both plates `[672, 696)` and `[696, 720)` in `E`: `ov_A = 8 * 8 = 64`, `ov_B = 64`, total 128. East (box `[689, 705)`): `ov_A = 56`, `ov_B = 72 > 64`, refused although the total is 128 again; west is refused symmetrically; north and south (both stay 64) are admitted.

The actors-overlapping formula is defined as:

`actors_overlapping(body_id, rects[]) = { a on body_id :  there exists r in rects with ov(foot_box(pos_curr(a)), r) > 0 },  in ascending actor_id`

The pure read Assembly's `validate` of a PLAYER-origin (`cause CHANNEL`) cut calls with the seam's two hatch rectangles (BDY-24); non-empty means reject (the reason code is Assembly's, `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED`). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)* BM4 evaluates the same read at apply time against `cap_rects[]` and returns `-BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP` when it is non-empty (BDY-24 (iii)).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `rects[]` | list of half-open rects, body frame | the seam's two hatch rectangles, each `width_tiles * 48` by 24 (LY-15) | Assembly, from Layout | the rectangles the cut would cap |
| `pos_curr(a)` | int pair | body frame | `body.state` at the tick the command validates | tick-start position |

**Output Range:** an ascending list of `actor_id`; empty means the cut may proceed. **Example:** hatch plates `[672, 696) x [240, 336)` and `[696, 720) x [240, 336)`; an actor at `pos = (690, 282)` has box `[682, 698) x [282, 290)`: `ov` with the first 112 and with the second `(698 - 696) * 8 = 16`, both positive, so the list holds that actor and the player cut is rejected; an actor at `pos = (640, 250)` has box `[632, 648)`, no overlap, and the list is empty. A multi-tick cut channel re-validates on its completion tick (a single-tick command cannot be raced because Assembly's `apply` is atomic in one P2, KRN:197).

### D.12 Sweep cost bound

The sweep cost bound formula is defined as:

`swept(pos, step) = [pos.x - 8 + min(0, step_x), pos.x + 8 + max(0, step_x)) x [pos.y + min(0, step_y), pos.y + 8 + max(0, step_y));     cells <= 4;     tests <= 7 * K  (1-px loop: K for the exemption scan plus (abs(step_x) + abs(step_y)) <= 6 sub-steps times K);     T_actor = c_q + tests * c_r + c_commit`

The swept box is the Minkowski cover of the foot box over the x-then-y path and is the ONE `solid_in` query of an actor-tick (BDY-15). A stationary actor issues no query and no sweep (BDY-9). An analytic clamp against each rectangle's near edge is permitted if and only if it is result-identical (an equivalence property test carries the proof; ADR-BODY-01) and reduces `tests` to about `3 * K`.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `step_x`, `step_y` | int | -3 to 3 | D.1 | whole px requested this tick |
| swept box width and height | int | `16 + abs(step_x)` (16 to 19) by `8 + abs(step_y)` (8 to 11) | calculated | at most 19 by 11 cells |
| `cells` | int | 1 to 4 | calculated; `BROADPHASE_CELL_PX` (Layout, LY:228) | a 19-px span crosses at most 2 broadphase cells per axis *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: this holds only while `BROADPHASE_CELL_PX >= FOOT_BOX_W + 3`, since the swept width is at most `FOOT_BOX_W + 3`; with `FOOT_BOX_W` 24 and a 24-px cell a swept box could touch 3 cells per axis and the 4-cell bound would break; Tuning Knobs)* |
| `K` | int | 0 to the densest 4 cells, far below `MAX_FOOTPRINT_RECTS_PER_BODY` 2,000 | Layout's `solid_in` result | rectangles returned for this actor-tick |
| `c_q`, `c_r`, `c_commit` | microseconds | **[UNV]**, spike S1 metrics | measured | one broadphase query, one box-versus-rectangle test, one commit with dirty marking |
| `B` | microseconds | 50 | cited: PCI:1078 (0.05 ms per actor at P99, 4 actors 0.2 ms, against a body of `MAX_FOOTPRINT_RECTS_PER_BODY` rectangles) | the per-actor budget; Body adds no figure |

**Output Range:** at most 7 tests per returned rectangle for the literal 1-px loop, one `solid_in` call, at most 4 broadphase cells; the cost is bounded independently of the 2,000-rectangle worst case because the query returns only the swept cells' rectangles. Whether PCI:1078's 0.05 ms includes the `solid_in` call is not stated there; this GDD states that the budget covers query, sweep and commit (Open Question for the Controller owner to confirm).

**Example:** `pos = (200, 140)`, `step = (0, -3)`: swept box `[192, 208) x [137, 148)`, 16 by 11 cells. With `K = 16`: the literal loop costs at most `7 * 16 = 112` tests, the analytic clamp about 48. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **No arithmetic is offered as evidence.** Every constant is **[UNV]** until spike S1, and the earlier illustration (`c_q + c_commit = 15 us`, `c_r = 0.25 us`, `T_actor = 43 us <= B`) used a native-code-scale `c_r`; interpreted GDScript is unlikely to be that fast, so `T_actor <= B` is spike S1's to settle and is not assumed here. The cited budgets, restated against the right denominators: eight actors (`MAX_ACTORS`) at the cited 0.05 ms are 0.4 ms, 20 % of the kernel's 2 ms P3 target; four actors at the cited 0.2 ms are 10 %. `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 is only a logging threshold and is not the denominator.

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **What the model above leaves out, and the whole-hook budget.** The figures above price only the moving-actor path, with the literal stepper, and the `c_r` 0.25 us of the earlier example was an **unmeasured illustration** (struck in the example above) (every constant is **[UNV]** until spike S1; it is not a design figure and no criterion gates on it). The full model, with the analytic clamp as the baseline (BDY-15) and the stepper only as the test oracle:

`T_move = c_q + K * c_r * 3 + c_commit`   (ordinary moving actor, clamp against each returned rectangle's near edge, about `3 * K` tests)
`T_exempt = c_q + K * c_r + n_sub * (|E| * c_ov + c_sec) + c_commit`   (exempt actor, `n_sub <= abs(step_x) + abs(step_y) <= 6` candidate sub-steps, `c_ov` one per-rectangle overlap area, `c_sec` one `section_at` call for the exit-containment test, BDY-29)
`T_idle = c_commit`   (stationary: residue update and commit, no query)
`T_hook = n_bodies_moving * c_pose + sum over actors of (T_move | T_exempt | T_idle) + c_diag`

Worst-case fixtures, committed with the criterion (AC-BDY-68): (W1) 8 actors all moving against the K = 16 cluster; (W2) 8 actors all EXEMPT at the abutting-pair cap with `|E| = 2`, all pressing east so every tick evaluates `E` and calls `section_at` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: W2 is NOT the most expensive exempt case; east is refused at the first candidate sub-step there, so `n_sub` = 1 and each actor pays one `|E| * c_ov + c_sec`)*; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (W2b) 8 actors all EXEMPT inside F7's single 24 x 96 rectangle (a box wholly inside one rectangle, `|E| = 1`, the section-set stub answering a section for every candidate origin), octant 1 at `v_eff` 3, so every candidate sub-step is admitted and `n_sub` reaches its bound of 6: `T_exempt = c_q + K * c_r + 6 * (c_ov + c_sec) + c_commit` per actor, the most expensive exempt tick; (W2c) the mixed fixture, 4 moving actors (W1's K = 16 cluster), 2 exempt actors (W2b) and 2 idle actors, the realistic bound on a busy tick; (W3) 8 bodies all with `vel != 0` and 8 idle actors (pose step plus 8 commits); (W4) Suit's reads, 8 seats each calling `actor_section` and `actor_local_px` once per tick for `suit.vitals` (BDY-29: 8 `section_at` calls per tick from the consumer and none from the hook itself), added to the hook total of W1 and of W2b; (W5) a weld or cut tick, the detection event of BDY-16 for every actor on the touched bodies with the trapped-actor probe for each actor whose start box has `E` non-empty: `T_event = n_touched * c_q + n_flagged * 192 * (|E| * c_ov + c_sec)` (192 = 4 directions x `TILE_PX` 48), at most 8 x 192 = 1,536 overlap tests and 1,536 `section_at` calls, once per event and never per tick. **Separate bounds for moving and exempt actors *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*:** a moving actor costs at most `3 * K` box-versus-rectangle tests; an exempt actor costs at most `K` rectangle tests plus `n_sub * |E|` overlap evaluations plus `n_sub` `section_at` calls (`n_sub <= 6`), a different shape that `3 * K` does not describe (AC-BDY-117 gates each on its own count). **Catch-up worst frame:** a late frame runs `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` = 4 ticks (BDY-32), so the worst frame cost is `4 * T_hook` of the more expensive of W2b and W2c, plus W4; on the cited per-actor figure the arithmetic for 8 actors is `4 * 0.4 ms = 1.6 ms`, 27 % of `FRAME_SIM_BUDGET_MS` 6.0 (the budget of a frame that runs `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` ticks; a one-tick frame is 4.0), an upper bound on the cited figure only, to be replaced by the S1 measurement *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was stated as 40 % of `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4, the wrong denominator)*. **Spike S1 is a hard prerequisite** of closing the Body hook story *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*: every wall-clock figure in this section is gated on the two reference machines of delegated ruling R12 and is *pending spike measurement on RM-DESKTOP / RM-DECK*. **RM-DESKTOP** is the minimum-spec desktop: Windows 10/11 64-bit; a 6-core/6-thread CPU of the Intel Core i5-9400F / AMD Ryzen 5 2600 class; 8 GB RAM; a GPU of the NVIDIA GeForce GTX 1050 Ti 4 GB / AMD Radeon RX 570 4 GB class (Vulkan 1.2+); SATA SSD; 1920x1080 at 60 Hz; P99 CPU frame-time ceiling 16.6 ms. **RM-DECK** is the handheld: Steam Deck LCD (2022), SteamOS stable, 1280x800 at 60 Hz panel, 30 fps target, P99 CPU frame-time ceiling 33.3 ms. **Build type for every wall-clock figure:** a release export (Godot 4.7.2 release export template, not the editor and not a debug template), renderer `forward_plus`, vsync on, the pipeline's 120-frame warm-up, with the startup read-back (renderer, driver) recorded with each result. **P99 headroom:** pass needs P99 <= 90 % of the ceiling (at least 1.7 ms of headroom on RM-DESKTOP, at least 3.3 ms on RM-DECK). The developer's machine is not a reference machine and its figures are indicative only (`project.yaml` `qa.min_spec`). Until S1 has recorded a number on both machines no figure in this section is a gate; the S1 baseline then becomes the +25 % reference (AC-BDY-117 (b)). The `STEP_BUDGET_MS` check is the logged share of the whole hook, not of one actor.

### D.13 Dirty-key encoding

The dirty-key encoding formula is defined as:

`key = (KIND << 32) | id;     KIND: BODY = 2 (the shared family), ACT = 4`

Layout's encoding (registry `layout_dirty_key_encoding`; Layout's KINDs are SEC 1, BODY 2, SEAM 3), keys being namespaced by block, so reusing `BODY` 2 inside `body.state` is legal and `ACT` 4 is unused by Layout. Every mutator and the hook mark explicitly (4.7 packed-array element writes no longer call the setter, **verified-from-docs**, `breaking-changes.md` 4.6 to 4.7).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `KIND` | int | 2 or 4 | Layout's encoding; Body adds `ACT` | key family |
| `id` | int | `body_id` or `actor_id`, below 2^32 | Layout / the block counter `next_actor_id` | the entity |
| `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` | int | 256 | registry (kernel) | per block per tick, above which the kernel reports `ALL` |

**Output Range:** a unique 64-bit key per `(KIND, id)`. Counts: the hook marks one `ACT` per actor that moved or whose `sub` changed (at most `MAX_ACTORS`) plus one `BODY` per body with `vel != (0, 0)` (at most `MAX_BODIES`); BM3 marks `n_moved` `ACT` plus 1 `BODY`; BM4 the same; BM1, BM2, BM5, BM6, BM7 mark 1 to 2. The worst-tick union is at most 16 distinct keys at the defaults (8 actors, `MAX_BODIES` 8) and *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier "32 at the tops of both ranges (16 actors, 16 bodies)" counted 16 bodies, but `MAX_BODIES` is Layout's allocation cap of 8, and the key count cannot exceed the number of bodies that can exist)* **24 at the top of the `MAX_ACTORS` range (16 actors + `MAX_BODIES` 8 bodies)**, 10 to 16 times under `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, so overflow cannot happen. A body with non-zero `vel` marks its `BODY` key every tick because its `world` changes, which keeps the delta stream non-empty for the whole pod leg; VS-B replication should send `world` only on a `vel` change (ADR-BODY-02).

**Example:** `ACT/3 = (4 << 32) | 3 = 17,179,869,187`; `BODY/1 = (2 << 32) | 1 = 8,589,934,593`. A weld with 8 absorbed actors marks 8 `ACT` + 1 `BODY` = 9 keys.

### D.14 Move-octant reset

The move-octant reset formula is defined as:

`move_octant = NONE if v_eff == 0;  else octant if octant in 0..7;  else last_octant if (STOP_DECEL_TICKS > 0 and last_octant != NONE)  (decel-ladder sub-case, dormant at STOP_DECEL_TICKS 0);  else NONE, counting BDY_OCTANT_INVALID;     reset  iff  move_octant != last_octant;     on reset  sub := (0, 0)  BEFORE the step;     after the step  last_octant := move_octant`

The predicate compares the move octant with Body's OWN persisted `last_octant` (0 to 7, 8 = NONE), never with the Controller's restore-normalised `octant := NONE` (PC-17), which is what makes a restore with the key still held continue the residue (BDY-31). The decel-ladder sub-case (octant NONE with `v_eff > 0`, PCI D.5) is **provisional**: it exists so the ladder is not silently broken if `STOP_DECEL_TICKS` is ever raised above its MVP default 0, where it is never taken (CP-8). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **The branch is gated** on `STOP_DECEL_TICKS > 0 and last_octant != NONE`: at the shipping default 0 an octant NONE or invalid with `v_eff > 0` (garbage input, a corrupt restore) yields NONE, so the actor is IDLE, `moved (0, 0)`, `sub` reset, `last_octant := NONE`, `BDY_OCTANT_INVALID` +1, and it never keeps walking its stale heading; and `last_octant` is read as an octant only when it is 0..7, so `UNIT[8]` is never indexed.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `octant` | int | 0 to 7 or NONE (8) | `controller.state` (written by `controller.step`) | the committed movement direction; any value outside 0 to 8 is treated as NONE (`BDY_OCTANT_INVALID`) |
| `v_eff` | int | 0 to 3 | `controller.state` `gait_effective` (PCI D.4) | zero for a stop, STAGGERED, DOWNED, DEAD, `IN_POD`, CHANNELLING and the end of a ladder |
| `last_octant` | int | 0 to 8 | `body.state` `actors[].last_octant` (persisted) | the octant the last step integrated; a different field from `controller.state`'s ladder memory of the same name (BDY-7) |
| `sub` | int pair | -255 to 255 | `body.state` | the residue of D.1 |

**Output Range:** boolean `reset`. `sub` resets on a stop, on any `v_eff == 0` tick and on any octant change; it does NOT reset on a gait change in one octant (walk to run keeps the residue), on a refused step, or in `post_restore`. A reset at `last_octant == NONE` is a no-op (the persisted invariant is `sub == (0, 0)` whenever `last_octant == NONE`, BDY-31).

**Example (re-fixtured PC-18 test 10):** NE (octant 1) at `v_eff` 2; after tick 2 `sub.x = 212`, `last_octant = 1`. Snapshot, restore, hold the same key (the Adapter's resync lands in P2 of the first post-restore tick, BDY-31): tick 3 reads `move_octant = 1 == 1`, no reset, `sub.x = 212 + 362 = 574`, `step = 2`, `sub.x = 62`, the uninterrupted cadence (1, 1, 2). Had the residue been zeroed, tick 3 would read `step = 1`, `sub.x = 106`, a different pixel path from tick 3 on. Key released across the save/load window: `v_eff = 0`, `move_octant = NONE != 1`, reset, identical to a stop. Octant change NE (1) to E (0) with `sub = (106, -106)`: `move_octant 0 != last_octant 1`, so `sub` resets to `(0, 0)` before the first E step, which moves exactly 2 px (the PCI D.4 fixture). Walk to run in octant 1: `move_octant == last_octant`, no reset. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Garbage input, ladder off (`STOP_DECEL_TICKS` 0):** `octant` 9, `v_eff` 2, `last_octant` 1: octant 9 is invalid so the move octant is NONE (the gated branch is not taken), `NONE != 1` resets `sub` to `(0, 0)`, `last_octant := 8`, the actor is IDLE and does not move, `BDY_OCTANT_INVALID` +1. `octant` NONE, `v_eff` 2, `last_octant` 8: the move octant is NONE, equal to `last_octant`, so the reset is a no-op, the actor is IDLE, no `UNIT` lookup is made and `BDY_OCTANT_INVALID` +1.

## Edge Cases

Each entry reads "If [condition]: [exact outcome]. [rationale]" and names the diagnostic or reason code where one exists. The defect counters are in the hook's diagnostics channel (3.7) and never in the block or the hashed set. *Disk state when this section was written (Glob of `design/gdd/`, 2026-10-26):* Derelict Authoring (`derelict-authoring-handmade-kits.md`) and Attention (`attention-system.md`) are on disk with status In Design (siblings in flight); Modular Ship Assembly, Web Map & Transit, Horror Entities, Boarding & Salvage, Sanity, Perception Filter and Debug Overlay have no file; every edge case that touches an undesigned or In Design system is **provisional** and says what they must repeat. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: all of those systems are now on disk with `Status: Designed` (Cross-References 4, 8.3); "provisional" in the entries below means the neighbour's text had not yet repeated the contract when this section was written, and 8.3 records which have.)*

**Sweep and collision**

- **If an actor's request is `(0, 0)` after `trunc` (a cardinal stop, or a carry diagonal on one of its roughly 29 % zero-motion ticks) or its move octant is NONE**: the actor issues no `solid_in` query and no sweep, `moved` is `(0, 0)`, `blocked` is false, and only the residue update and the commit run. The idle and carry-diagonal ticks are the most common ticks in the game and cost nothing beyond the commit (BDY-9; PCI D.3 carry cadence).
- **If x is refused and y is free (a diagonal against a wall, the PCI:908 slide)**: `moved = (0, moved_y)`, both residues advance exactly as in a free run (D.1: the x residue follows the free-run sequence and is reduced by the refused step's whole pixels, so a refused step neither stores energy nor loses any), `blocked` is false, and `odometer` advances by the free axis times `ODO_W[move octant]` (181 for one diagonal pixel). A slide keeps the free diagonal's residue sequence, so a wall never speeds an actor up or slows it beyond the lost axis (BDY-12, BDY-19).
- **If both axes are refused (a corner, a flush cardinal, a gap narrower than the foot box)**: `moved = (0, 0)`, `blocked` is true for PRESENTATION and DIAGNOSTIC subscribers only, the Controller's `PC_BLOCKED_TICKS` counts the tick (cited, not re-registered), `odometer` adds 0, and `sub` stays within 255 in magnitude. A flush cardinal actor keeps `sub = (0, 0)` because cardinal increments are multiples of 256 (BDY-13, BDY-19).
- **If a gap between two rectangles is narrower than `FOOT_BOX_W` on an east-west passage or `FOOT_BOX_H` on a north-south one**: the axis of passage is refused every tick and the actor cannot pass; this is Layout's passage test (`LY D.5`, 16 and 8) restated at the sweep, so a doorway the art shows as closed is closed to the foot (P1, BDY-15).
- **If `v_eff` 3 meets a 1-px-thick rectangle (a door leaf at a small `area`, `LY D.5`)**: the sweep tests every 1-px sub-step, so the sliver is never skipped and the actor stops at its near edge. This is why the production sweep is proved result-identical to a 1-px stepper by an enumerated property test (BDY-15, ADR-BODY-01).
- **If `v_eff` is outside 0 to 3 or `octant` is outside 0 to 8**: `v_eff` is clamped into `[0, 3]` for this tick only (-1 gives 0, 5 gives 3) and `BDY_VEFF_INVALID` counts; the octant is treated as NONE and `BDY_OCTANT_INVALID` counts, and with `v_eff > 0` and the ladder off (`STOP_DECEL_TICKS` 0, the default) that actor is IDLE and does not move rather than continuing a stale heading, and no `UNIT` lookup is made for `last_octant` NONE *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the hook never faults. A crash on a defective value would be the world stopping, which is a P1 violation of its own kind (BDY-9).
- **If a body-frame coordinate is negative (after a detach re-base)**: the sweep uses signed comparisons in body-frame coordinates, `sub / 256` uses `trunc`, and any px-to-tile or local-coordinate step uses `floor_div`; the two helpers are never swapped. A run at `pos = (-40, -16)` equals the translated positive-coordinate run (BDY-35, LY-3 (f)).
- **If two actors occupy the same pixel**: legal and silent, with no diagnostic and no order dependence; neither moves the other (BDY-17, PCI:105). Doors still count both boxes.
- **If `solid_in` returns an empty set**: the full request is granted. **If it returns a rectangle twice or out of key order (a Layout defect)**: the sweep takes a minimum over candidates and is idempotent and order-independent, so the result is unchanged; the order and uniqueness are asserted in debug and raise the debug signal `diag.solid_in_unordered` (BDY-32).

**Doors and leaves**

- **If `layout.doors` closes a leaf in P3 of tick T while an actor presses into the doorway**: `body.integrate` is ordered after `layout.doors` (BDY-8), so the leaf is solid when the sweep runs and the actor is stopped at it in tick T itself, with no one-tick lag. **If the leaf opens in the same tick**, the actor passes in tick T. If the registration were reordered by accident the actor would pass a tick late; the guard is the boot topological-order test over the real registrations (BDY-8, PC-18 test 7).
- **If a PLAYER-origin close would overlap any actor's foot box**: Layout's `leaf_would_overlap` rejects it (LY-11) using `foot_boxes(body_id)` for EVERY actor on the body, the closer and the crewmates alike; a `SEAT` row whose Controller row is unbound still publishes its box. "A door will not close on any crewmate" holds even when a crewmate's input is gone (BDY-18).
- **If a SYSTEM-origin close (a cascade) finds a foot box under the leaf**: the door owner holds the leaf at `CLOSING_BLOCKED` and retries (PCI:650); Body never sees the overlap because the rectangle is not yet solid. If a defect makes it solid anyway, BDY-16 applies: the actor is never displaced and `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` counts each exempt tick.

**Weld**

- **If the avatar is on the ABSORBED body when the weld applies**: its `pos_curr` gains `frame_shift_px`, its `body_id` becomes the surviving id, `pos_prev := pos_curr`, `sub` and `last_octant` are untouched, and `BODY_TRANSFER{weld}` is raised for it. `actor_section` and `actor_local_px` are identical before and after (P-W, D.4); the camera REBASE is the Pipeline's (PIPE C5: it shifts the solver by `frame_delta_px = +frame_shift_px` and keeps the lag, so the avatar's screen position does not move; a hard cut only where the camera bounds clamp), not Body's *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The mutator runs in P2 and `body.integrate` in P3 of the same tick, so the actor steps this tick from the shifted position with a zero interpolation delta.
- **If an actor is on the HOST body (including one standing in the host's hatch passage) when the weld applies**: it is untouched and no `BODY_TRANSFER` is raised for it; "a weld moves nothing already built" (LY:250).
- **If the absorbed body has no actors**: BM3 deletes its pose row only; no `BODY_TRANSFER` is raised; `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT` is raised once per BM3 regardless of the actor count (BDY-22, BDY-28).
- **If the host and absorbed bodies have different `vel` at the weld**: the host's pose governs from that tick; the absorbed pose row is deleted and no interior position changes; the exterior layer jumps by `delta_abs` (D.8), which is zero iff `docked` was true. The weld succeeds either way because Body's legality takes no pose input (BDY-27); a PLAYER-origin weld is not reachable in this state in a shipped build, because Web Map & Transit and Assembly offer it only once `docked` holds (MSA-12, WM-21; a named must-repeat, answered, 8.3); only a test build forces it (AC-BDY-74c, non-gating). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the alternative "or the Pipeline must hide any non-zero jump behind its C5 cut" is struck; C5 hides camera motion, not the absorbed hull's exterior jump, so the offer gate is the only guard, BDY-27. The interior half is proved by AC-BDY-118 and the gate by AC-BDY-74a.)*
- **If `can_rebase_attach` is called with the same body twice, or with a body Layout does not hold**: `BDY_REBASE_SAME_BODY` or `BDY_REBASE_UNKNOWN_BODY` is the answer to the caller's `validate`; inside `apply` the same checks return `-BDY_REBASE_SAME_BODY` or `-BDY_REBASE_UNKNOWN_BODY` with nothing written, and the sink counts `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH` (BDY-21) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
- **If a translated actor's foot box overlaps a host rectangle after a weld (impossible if `can_attach` rejected `GEOM_OVERLAP`)**: it is a Layout defect; BDY-16 applies, `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` counts and `diag.actor_in_footprint` fires in debug; the actor is not moved.

**Detach and the hatch cap**

- **If a foot box straddles the seam at the detach tick**: the foot ORIGIN picks the side and the box is not split (PCI:651, D.5). The origin is the box's top row, 4 px north of where a mid-box origin would sit, so an actor straddling an east-west seam line is assigned to the section holding its top row; this is stated once here and the Controller's wording rows are routed (8.3). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)* A box that straddles the seam overlaps the seam's hatch rectangles, so in play BM4's cap guard refuses before the side rule is reached (BDY-24 (iii)); the side rule is the specification for the unit-test surface (`cap_rects[]` empty) and for every origin that passes the guard.
- **If the foot origin lies in no section (an exterior gap, a defect)**: the actor stays on `C_keep`, `BDY_TRANSFER_NO_SECTION` counts, and `actor_section` answers NONE, a defect-only state that CI asserts never occurs in a clean build (AC-BDY-29a); its consequence is exterior exposure (Suit and the Graph, a routed must-repeat, 8.3), never the last room's air. From that origin the actor may step only toward an origin that lies in a section (BDY-16 exit containment). Rationale: the keep side retains the old `body_id`, so the stored record changes least.
- **If a PLAYER-origin (`cause CHANNEL`) cut is validated while an actor's foot box overlaps either hatch rectangle**: `actors_overlapping` (D.11) is non-empty and Assembly's `validate` rejects the cut (reason code Assembly's, `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED`, MSA-21). The ship will not close a seam on a crewmate. A SYSTEM-origin (`cause CASCADE`) cut defers while it is non-empty and on expiry is abandoned, never forced (MSA-22).
- **If a cut is applied anyway (a forced test hook, or a caller that skipped V5) while an actor's foot box overlaps the seam's hatch rectangles** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)*: BM4 evaluates `actors_overlapping(old_body_id, cap_rects)` before its first write, finds the actor, writes nothing and returns `-BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP`; the sink counts `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH`, the caller's `apply` faults the kernel (MSA-31) and the actor is not moved (REFUSE, not EJECT). The next autosave never holds the overlap (AC-BDY-141).
- **If a restore of an older save or a template defect leaves a foot box inside a SOLID hatch cap** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)* (a cut can no longer create this state: BM4 refuses it, BDY-24 (iii)): the actor is never displaced, may only take sub-steps that do not deepen its overlap with any rectangle in `E` (D.11), and `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` counts each exempt tick. From `pos = (690, 282)` the east step that would shrink the overlap is admitted to `pos.x = 695` and refused beyond it, because the destination origin would lie in no section of the body (exit containment, BDY-16, D.11): the actor rests inside the cap, still in section 1, `actor_section` never answers NONE, and `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` counts every tick on which it presses a key. This stuck state (the cap trap) is the accepted residual, preferred to a hull exit that would leave the actor outside the hull in no section at all (P1); Body offers no rescue and recovery is Assembly's, Save/Load's or the content fix (Open Question 27). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **The trapped actor is EXPOSED, not "stuck but safe":** it stands inside plate A's footprint, Layout answers `room_at` NONE there, and Suit reads EXPOSED (vacuum, anoxic, exterior temperature) on a pressurised ship (BDY-16, BDY-29, D.11); the earlier claim that its readings are "those of a section it is still in" is struck. The guarantee is visibility (the actor is visibly inside the plate and Suit's instruments show the band), not safety. The trap is a strip (`x` 689 to 695), not a point; D.11 lists the full admitted-move set. **If an autosave was written while an actor was trapped and the game restarts from it (the MVP death policy)**, the trap is restored exactly (BDY-31), `post_restore`'s probe counts `BDY_TRAPPED_ACTOR`, and Body still does not rescue: the remedy is content and authoring validation (Open Question 27) and Save/Load's autosave guard (a must-repeat, Open Question 33) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **If an exempt actor is stationary**: it issues no query, `E` is not evaluated, `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` adds nothing on its idle ticks (it counted once at the detection event that created the overlap and counts again on each tick the actor presses a key), and the probe and the debug tree-walk are what make the idle trap visible (BDY-16). It is why Assembly's refusal, its deferral and the P2 tell window are the primary guard, a named must-repeat (BDY-24, 8.3).
- **If a box is wholly inside one cap rectangle, or astride two abutting exempt rectangles with neither side reaching open floor**: the first has constant overlap until an edge crosses out and may exit either side, provided the destination origin stays in a section of the body (BDY-16 exit containment); the second can only slide along the shared edge or back out and, if neither reaches open floor, stays exempt in place, with `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` counting every tick and visible in the debug overlay. Both are defect-only paths and no guarantee is claimed for them (BDY-16 residuals).
- **If a cut leaves `C_new` with no actor, or moves every actor to `C_new`**: the new pose row is still created (`world_old + frame_shift_px`, `vel_old`) and the old body keeps its row; `actors_on` is empty for the empty side; an orphaned body persists, and discarding it is Layout's, Assembly's and Web Map's decision (BDY-4).
- **If the cut would exceed `MAX_BODIES`**: Layout rejects the detach (`CAPACITY`) before any Body mutator runs, because Body's mutators run after Layout's in the same `WriteScope` (Assembly's call order; LY-16, LY-17). If BM4 were ever called with an id Layout never allocated, `can_rebase_detach` answers `BDY_REBASE_UNKNOWN_BODY`.
- **If BM4 is invoked BEFORE Layout's mutator (a call-order defect in Assembly)**: the pre-cut reconstruction of D.5 would double-shift the moved sections; BM4 finds `new_body_id` not live (`-BDY_REBASE_UNKNOWN_BODY`) or a `moved_sections[]` member not owned by it (`-BDY_REBASE_SECTION_NOT_OWNED`), writes nothing, the sink counts `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH` and Assembly faults the kernel. The order Graph, Layout, Body is Assembly's contract (BDY-21, BDY-23) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
- **If a weld is followed by a cut of the same seam**: `abs` and the section-local position of every actor are conserved, but raw `pos_curr` is generally not restored (detach re-bases to the section with the minimum id in `C_new`, while a weld shifts by the absorbed offset); any round-trip test asserts `abs` and `actor_local_px`, never raw `pos_curr` (BDY-23, D.5).
- **If a transferring actor carries a module (`carry_ref`) or is DEAD on the transfer tick**: Body changes only `body_id`, `pos_curr`, `pos_prev` and `sub`; the carried record is the Controller's and Interaction's and is unchanged (PCI:651, IA-28); a death restart is Save/Load's and Body completes the transfer arithmetic first.

**Pod hop (Web Map & Transit is Designed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was "provisional — undesigned")*)**

- **If `transfer_actor` targets a body Layout has not built, or an arrival cell inside a footprint**: `can_transfer` answers `BDY_PLACE_UNKNOWN_BODY` or `BDY_PLACE_INSIDE_FOOTPRINT` and nothing changes. Body never searches for a free cell, because that would be a displacement; the alternative cell is Transit's (BDY-25).
- **If a hop is applied while the actor is mid-step**: the transfer happens in P2, and the P3 step sees `v_eff = 0` (the seat is `IN_POD`, PCI:694) and resets `sub`; BM5 keeps `last_octant`, and the P3 step then sets it to NONE (8) because the move octant is NONE (BDY-12, AC-BDY-103).
- **If the pod body has a non-zero `vel` while the actor is inside**: the actor's `pos_curr` is unchanged by `vel`; the exterior layer shows the motion and the interior does not (BDY-2, D.9).
- **If two crewmates arrive at one cell**: legal and silent (BDY-17); the arrival cells are Transit's to vary.

**Restore and idempotence**

- **If a snapshot is taken mid-move with `sub != (0, 0)` and restored with the same key held**: `pos_prev == pos_curr`, `sub` is preserved, and the next tick continues the cadence from the persisted residue (D.14). The claim is block-level only; whatever PC-17's normalisation does to Controller-owned state is the Controller's (BDY-31).
- **If the key is released during the save and load window**: the next tick reads `v_eff = 0`, NONE versus the persisted `last_octant`, and `sub` resets exactly as a stop does. **If the Adapter's resync lands one tick late**: the first post-restore tick reads NONE and resets `sub` (at most 255/256 px), after which the cadence restarts; this is not a correctness defect, but it changes the re-fixtured test's GIVEN (BDY-31).
- **If a saved `body_id` is not live in Layout after Layout restored**: `restore()` reports `BDY_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_BODY` as a failure against Layout's already-restored state, with the live state untouched *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was `post_restore`, where no intact state remains)*; nothing is dropped or clamped, because a clamp would make `snapshot(restore(x)) != x`.
- **If a save carries an invalid field**: the WHOLE block is rejected on the first failure with the named reason, reported through `ctx.fault(BDY_RESTORE_*)` and never by a return value *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0006 sync: section 2.2)*: `BDY_RESTORE_VERSION`, `BDY_RESTORE_TYPE` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: any non-integer field, for example a float `pos_x`)*, `BDY_RESTORE_ACTOR_ID` (duplicate, below 1, or not below `next_actor_id`), `BDY_RESTORE_KIND` (including an `AGENT` row with a seat or a negative seat), `BDY_RESTORE_OCTANT`, `BDY_RESTORE_SUB_RANGE`, `BDY_RESTORE_SUB_INVARIANT` (for example `last_octant` NONE with `sub = (3, 0)`, a sign opposing `UNIT[last_octant]`, or an off-axis residue on a cardinal octant), `BDY_RESTORE_ODOMETER` (negative), `BDY_RESTORE_HEADING_UNSUPPORTED` (`heading_q != 0`), `BDY_RESTORE_POSE_RANGE`, `BDY_RESTORE_CAPACITY` (above `MAX_ACTORS`), and *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* `BDY_RESTORE_COUNT`, `BDY_RESTORE_ORDER` and `BDY_RESTORE_NONCANONICAL`, with the diagonal clause (`|sub.x| != |sub.y|`) added to `BDY_RESTORE_SUB_INVARIANT`. A reported failure is the honest outcome; a partial restore would put people in a world that never existed (P1).
- **If a saved foot box intersects a rectangle that a changed template made SOLID**: the save loads; this is deliberately not a failure, so a build change cannot make a save unloadable. BDY-16 applies and `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` counts (BDY-31).
- **If `odometer` exceeds 2^53**: it travels in the binary payload at full width and round-trips exactly; it is never a JSON number and never placed in a `Vector2i` (SL-2, BDY-32).
- **If the crew dies and the game restarts from the last autosave (the MVP death handling, owned by Save/Load)**: every actor returns to the pixel and residue the autosave recorded; a position is part of what the world remembers honestly, and nothing else of the lost minutes is Body's to restore (P5). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **The restart is only playable once the kernel's seat binding is rebuilt**: Body restores the actors table with each `seat`, but the binding `PlayerId -> actor_id` is kernel identity data that is not saved (KRN:100); if the kernel's `LOADING` rebuild (CR-7) had not re-bound each `seat_bindings()` pair, the first seat-1 command would fail the authority stage with `ACTOR_NOT_OWNED` and the avatar would stand still. Body's half is the registered binding source `seat_bindings()`; the rebuild is the kernel's (BDY-7, KRN-BIND-01, ANSWERED), and AC-BDY-6 and AC-BDY-138 are the closure-gate integration tests through the real load path *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)*.

**Capacity, binding and defects**

- **If `place_actor` would exceed `MAX_ACTORS`, names an unknown body, a bad kind, a duplicate seat or a footprint cell**: `can_place` answers `BDY_PLACE_CAPACITY`, `BDY_PLACE_UNKNOWN_BODY`, `BDY_PLACE_BAD_KIND`, `BDY_PLACE_DUPLICATE_SEAT` or `BDY_PLACE_INSIDE_FOOTPRINT` to the caller's `validate`. If a caller ignores it, BM1 returns `-BDY_PLACE_CAPACITY` (or the matching negative code), writes no row and leaves `next_actor_id` unchanged, the sink counts `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH` and the caller's `apply` faults the kernel; the tables stay consistent and no over-cap row can exist, so the next autosave still restores; a restore above the cap is rejected as `BDY_RESTORE_CAPACITY` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
