- **If a `SEAT` row has no Controller row (a Vertical Slice disconnect, a corrupt save)**: the actor is not stepped, `sub` is reset and `last_octant := NONE`, and `BDY_SEAT_UNBOUND` counts; its box is still published to doors. **If an actor's `body_id` is not live in Layout during a tick**: it is skipped with its row untouched and `BDY_ORPHAN_ACTOR` counts (BDY-9, 3.2).
- **If a body's `world + vel` would leave +/-`WORLD_COORD_LIMIT`**: that body's `vel` is set to `(0, 0)` in the same tick, `world` is unchanged and `BDY_WORLD_LIMIT` counts once; **if `can_set_velocity` is asked for a speed above `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` or for an unknown body**: `BDY_VEL_OUT_OF_RANGE` or `BDY_VEL_UNKNOWN_BODY`. A zero velocity on a zero-world row deletes the row (BDY-26, D.7). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **If a cut would produce a new pose row beyond `±WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` or a `vel` beyond `±VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK`** (a body at `world.x = 4,194,304` that is then cut): `can_rebase_detach` answers `BDY_REBASE_POSE_OUT_OF_RANGE` or `BDY_VEL_OUT_OF_RANGE` and the cut is not offered; an unguarded `apply` saturates the new row and returns `OK` (BDY-23) so `restore(snapshot())` still accepts the block and the death-restart can load it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
- **If a late frame runs `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` ticks in one rendered frame**: each tick is a full independent step with no merging, an actor moves at most 12 px in the frame, under `JUMP_CUT` (PIPE:501), and the result is identical to the same ticks run one per frame (BDY-32).

**Degenerate strategies (the floor gives no lever)**

- **If a player tries to body-block a crewmate in a corridor or a doorway**: impossible; actors do not block one another (BDY-17), so nothing a player can do with a body pins another actor. Breach pacing comes from the world, not from the controls (P2, anti-PvP).
- **If a player tries to use a weld, a cut or a pod hop as a teleport past an obstacle**: no gain; each transfer preserves `actor_local_px` and `abs` (D.4, D.5), and the pod arrival cell is Transit's, validated clear and never searched. An actor moves only with its section.
- **If a player taps and releases a key to shed the sub-pixel residue**: the reset costs at most 255/256 px per change and gives nothing; displacement over any run in one octant is exact (D.1), so there is no speed exploit in either direction (D.14).
- **If a player cuts a crewmate off to hurt them**: a PLAYER-origin cut is refused while the crewmate stands in the hatch passage, a cut never displaces or damages anyone, and Body offers no harm channel of any kind; lethal friendly harm exists only where its own system gates it (BDY-24, BDY-28).

**Hallucination interplay (P4: Body is the human channel of truth)**

- **If a class (a) phantom entity appears**: it has no Body row (filter-only phantoms have none), so there is nothing to collide with and no position to read; walking "through" a phantom is walking through nothing. Nothing about the player's own footing is part of the lie: no actor is ever moved without a cause the simulation can show.
- **If a class (b) false state shows a wall as open or a door as shut**: Layout tags wall faces, door leaves and seam geometry truth-channel (LY:41, LY:47; routed to the Perception Filter), and Body's sweep always tests the true rectangles; the player who walks into a hallucinated opening bumps (`blocked`), which makes the lie falsifiable by the body.
- **If a class (c) crewmate-as-horror is rendered**: the rig is drawn at the crewmate's true `pos_curr` (PCI:216); Body has no perceived, per-player or `PERCEPTION_OUT` variant of any read, so where the "monster" stands is where the crewmate stands, for every player on every frame (BDY-30). Both the Perception Filter and Sanity (Designed; both repeat BDY-30) must repeat this *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If sanity is low**: it never slows, shifts, drifts, displaces or re-times any actor; sanity never touches `body.state` (inherited guard rail of the Controller's secondary test).

**Co-op later (Vertical Slice, VS-B; hooks only)**

- **If a `MIRROR` kernel ticks**: `body.integrate` does not apply on a `MIRROR` (CR-7); remote positions arrive through `SHARED_SUMMARY` (`pos_curr`, `body_id`, `seat`, `kind`, `odometer`, `last_octant`, `sub`); `blocked`, `moved`, `actor_section` and every `diag` value are not replicated and are re-derived by the same code from replicated state; there is no client-side prediction in the MVP (KRN:745).
- **If a seat rejoins after a disconnect**: the persisted `seat` field re-binds the surviving `SEAT` row to its Controller row without any other saved datum (KRN:100); a joiner who would exceed `MAX_ACTORS` meets `BDY_PLACE_CAPACITY`, and what the session then does is the session's.
- **If cross-body light is requested (an unlit detached section next to a lit one)**: it stays OFF in the MVP (EM-30); the open question belongs to Ship Body and the Emitter jointly (Open Questions).

**Simultaneous rules**

- **If a weld and the avatar's own step land on the same tick**: the weld's mutator runs in P2 and the step in P3, so the order is fixed by the phase, not by arrival: the actor is re-homed, then stepped once from the shifted position. **If two mutators target the same body in one `WriteScope`**: they apply in the call order Graph, Layout, Body (Assembly's call order; LY-16, LY-17); the caller's `validate` must compose its plan (Layout's own `can_*` reads show which body the earlier mutator retires); Body's `can_*` reads see the pre-state, so a plan that still names the retired body reaches `apply`, where the mutator returns its `FAULT_CODE` (for BM6, `-BDY_VEL_UNKNOWN_BODY`), writes nothing, the sink counts `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH` and the caller faults the kernel *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
- **If a door leaf and a cut both rewrite rectangles in one tick**: `layout.doors` (P3) runs before `body.integrate` and the cut's mutators (P2) ran before both, so the sweep reads one consistent rectangle set; Body never reads a half-applied weld, because the mutator is atomic in one `apply` (CR-9, BDY-21).

## Dependencies

*Status as of 2026-10-26 (Glob of `design/gdd/` re-run immediately before this section was written):* **designed** — Command & Simulation Layer, Life-Support Graph, Item & Module Database, Player Controller & Input, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera, Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query, 2D Lighting & Occlusion, Failure & Trail, Save/Load & Versioned Format, Suit & Equipment, Interaction System, Ship Section & Layout Data Model. Derelict Authoring (`derelict-authoring-handmade-kits.md`) and Attention (`attention-system.md`) are on disk with status In Design (siblings in flight); Modular Ship Assembly, Web Map & Transit, Horror Entities, Boarding & Salvage, Sanity, Perception Filter and Debug Overlay have no file (status *undesigned-provisional* for those without a file; Modular Ship Assembly and Web Map & Transit are being designed concurrently in this run). The systems index lists this system's dependencies as Command & Simulation Layer, Ship Section & Layout Data Model, Player Controller & Input and Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera (IDX:84; Dependency Map IDX:175), and its one listed dependent as Modular Ship Assembly (IDX:85); the tables below find more dependents than the index names and route the missing edges to the producer (never editing the index). *Refreshed at the consistency pass 2026-10-27: Modular Ship Assembly, Web Map & Transit, Horror Entities, Debug Overlay, Adaptive Audio & Tells and HUD & Suit Layer are on disk with status Designed; the rows that their routed asks name are updated below, and the remaining status words above are as of 2026-10-26.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **every system in the tables below has a GDD on disk with `Status: Designed`** (Glob of `design/gdd/`, 2026-10-27), including Boarding & Salvage, Sanity, Perception Filter, Attention, Derelict Authoring and Debug Overlay; the words "no file", "undesigned" and "In Design" in the status column and in the 2026-10-26 paragraph above are superseded, and the rows are corrected where they appear.)*

| Ship Section & Layout Data Model | Body depends on it | **Hard** | `solid_in(body_id, aabb)`, `rects_in`, `body_of`, `section_offset`, `sections_of`, template extents, the `attach_section` / `detach_section` return records; `restore_after: [layout.state]`; hook order `after: layout.doors` | designed | LY:989 lists "Ship Body & Interior Physics \| Body -> Layout \| Hard" — consistent; Layout reads nothing from `body.state` |
| Player Controller & Input | Body depends on it | **Hard** | `controller.state[seat]` `octant` and `v_eff`; hook order `after: [controller.step, layout.doors]`; the 12 tests of PC-18 | designed | PCI:728 lists the Ship Body row ("Yes — boundary provisional"), now closed by this GDD |
| Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera | Body depends on it | **Soft** (rules, not data) | the C2 constraints, the PR-4 canvas rule, the C5 rebase-or-cut rule, the `y_foot` definition (PIPE:535), `tick_alpha` | designed | PIPE:777 lists Ship Body as "Hard (camera) ... add edge" — the opposite direction, see below |

| Modular Ship Assembly | depends on Body | **Hard** | BM3, BM4, `can_rebase_attach`, `can_rebase_detach`, `actors_overlapping`, `docked`, `body_count`, `actors_on`, `foot_boxes` | designed (`modular-ship-assembly.md`) | IDX:85 lists Body; Assembly repeats the rows of 4.3 (MSA-12, MSA-17, MSA-21, MSA-22) |

| Web Map & Transit | depends on Body | **Hard** | BM1, BM5, BM6, BM7 `set_body_pose`, `world_pose`, `docked`, `can_transfer`, `body_of_actor` | designed (`web-map-and-transit.md`) | Web Map's Dependencies row lists Ship Body Hard (BM7 marked provisional on its side until it sees this GDD's change) — consistent |

| Perception Filter / Sanity | reads Body | **Soft** (read only) | the SIM read view | designed (`perception-filter.md`, `sanity-and-hallucination-rules.md`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was "undesigned-provisional (no files)")* | BDY-30 repeated: Perception Filter cites BDY-30 (true position, true chest point); Sanity cites BDY-29 and BDY-30 (no write to `body.state` or positions) — consistent |
| Debug Overlay | reads Body | **Soft** (read only) | the diagnostics of 3.7 | designed (`debug-overlay-true-values.md`) | the Overlay consumes the 3.7 diagnostics as one of its 15 expected MVP channels (AC-DO-69) — consistent |
| Adaptive Audio & Tells | reads Body events | **Soft** | `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT`, `BODY_HULL_SEPARATION`, `BODY_TRANSFER{weld \| detach \| pod}` (3.7) | designed (`adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`) | Audio's Dependencies row lists Ship Body Soft — consistent (consistency pass 2026-10-27) |
| Derelict Authoring | supplies spawn points | **Soft** | `can_place`, BM1 through `init_world`; BDY-25 pod template | designed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was "on disk, In Design")* | DA-3 repeats BDY-20 and BDY-25 (spawn conversion; the `pod` template); seat numbering **answered** (DA renumbers to seat 1; OQ 26 closed) |
| Attention | reads Body | **Soft** (read only) | `actor_section`, `body_of_actor` | designed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was "on disk, In Design")* | AT-14, AT-22, AT-25 name Body; index edge Attention ← Ship Body (soft) is Attention's routed correction |

#### Explicitly no edge

| System | Why |
|---|---|
| Life-Support Graph | **No edge; indirect via Layout `room_at`.** The Graph names Body nowhere; the only chain is Body → `actor_section` → Suit → Layout `room_at` → Graph `compartment_at`. |
| Failure & Trail; Item & Module Database | Zero hits for Body in either; a horror's footprint is a Graph trail and a carried module is the Controller's `carry_ref`. |

**Hard-dependency reading.** Of the four upstream edges, only the Pipeline's is Soft because Body consumes its rules, not its data; the three Hard ones are each the source of an input Body cannot function without (a clock and a block registry, the rectangles, the intent). No dependency cycle is created: Layout reads nothing from Body (LY-18), the Pipeline reads Body's transforms but Body reads no Pipeline data, and no Controller SIM hook reads Body in schema v1 except `moved`, read by the Controller's `controller.noise` hook ordered after `body.integrate` (CP-3 (b), adopted; PCI PC-15) (a difference of two tick-boundary values, never fed back into `octant` or `v_eff`, so no data cycle; the noise-ordering conflict of 4.1 was the one place a cycle could be created, and the adopted ordering creates none).

## Tuning Knobs

Body is a nearly knobless system by design: it is a kinematic frame, and the things that make it feel right (the speeds, the cadence, the wall depth, the zoom) belong to its neighbours. The table below therefore has two parts. **Part A** lists the constants Body owns. **Part B** lists every constant Body consumes, marked **not this system's**, with its owner and registry name and *no value column of Body's own* (R23; the pattern of PIPE:827): the owner GDD sets it, and a change there is the owner's decision, never a Body tweak. No knob is invented to reach a row count, and a Body knob that changes a sibling's locked value would be a **CONFLICT** routed to the producer, never a row. **★ = registry candidate** (a cross-system fact registered after this GDD is final, 8.2); values tagged `[PLACEHOLDER]` are not confirmed until the named spike. Two quantities with similar names have two owners and are named once here: `MAX_BODIES` (Layout's allocation cap, a registry entry) is how many `body_id`s may exist; `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` (Lighting's registry entry, 4, range 2-8, `[PLACEHOLDER]` until the Lighting spike: the count of live bodies, i.e. built, id allocated and not discarded, with at least one non-dormant section or hosting the avatar) is how many bodies may be live at once; Body does not confirm the latter and its only live-body count is the pure read `body_count()`.

### Part A. Constants Body owns

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ★ `FOOT_BOX_W` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: range corrected to the coupled constants)* | 16 (confirms PCI:103) | base px | 8 to 16, even; **coupled:** the upper bound is Layout's 16-px east-west passage extent (`LY D.5`) and may rise only if `LY D.5` changes in the same edit; and `BROADPHASE_CELL_PX >= FOOT_BOX_W + 3` (Layout's 48 allows up to 45; a 24-px cell with `FOOT_BOX_W` 24 lets the swept box touch 3 cells per axis and breaks D.12's 4-cell bound) | wider than the 16-px east-west passage extent of `LY D.5`: a doorway the art shows open stops admitting the foot (`area_pass_min` rises) and shoulders catch on props and corners | narrower than the sprite's shoulders; the foot passes gaps the eye reads as wall, a P1 lie; slips through 1-px slivers | `LY D.5` passage extents, the PCI:908 fixture, D.2, the swept-box width of D.12; changing it is a registry edit plus a re-run of the Layout and Controller fixtures, not a local tweak |
| ★ `FOOT_BOX_H` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: range corrected to the coupled constant)* | 8 (confirms PCI:103) | base px | 4 to 8; **coupled:** the upper bound is Layout's 8-px north-south passage extent (`LY D.5`) and may rise only if `LY D.5` changes in the same edit; `BROADPHASE_CELL_PX >= FOOT_BOX_H + 3` | the box bottom reaches deep into the wall to the south while `y_foot` sorts at the box top, so the stand-off north of a wall (`y_1 - FOOT_BOX_H`) grows and the actor looks to stand inside the face | feet look unsupported at north walls; the 24-versus-8 stand-off asymmetry of D.3 shrinks but the 24-px rule is unchanged | `LY D.5` north-south passage extent (8), D.3, the swept-box height of D.12, `ov` maximum in D.11 |
| ★ `MAX_ACTORS` `[PLACEHOLDER]` | 8 | actors | 4 to 16 | sweep and dirty-key cost scale linearly: 8 actors at the cited 0.05 ms are 0.4 ms against the cited 0.2 ms line for 4 (D.12); restore accepts a bigger table | below 4 the Vertical Slice's four seats plus a transient `AGENT` cannot place (`BDY_PLACE_CAPACITY`) | D.12, D.13, `BDY_RESTORE_CAPACITY`, PCI:1078 (cited); *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* lowering it after saves exist is a schema bump with a migration, never a silent tuning change (Open Question 13, closed) |
| ★ `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` `[PLACEHOLDER]` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **lowering = schema bump** (a saved row beyond the new limit is rejected by `BDY_RESTORE_POSE_RANGE`; BDY-26); the 2^21 default proposed at review is declined because Web Map's `WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX` 2^17 `<= WORLD_COORD_LIMIT / 32` would fail)* | 2^22 (4,194,304) | base px | 2^22 only *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: lowering fails Web Map's lint `WEB_COORD_BUDGET_PX` 2^17 `<= WORLD_COORD_LIMIT / 32` and is a schema bump; raising is outside the float32 bound until spike S2 settles it)* | a canvas offset (a difference of two world coordinates, up to 2^23) loses float32 exactness above 2^24 **[UNV: build precision]** | pod legs cannot be long; Transit must re-origin between legs often | D.7, `can_set_velocity`, `can_set_pose` (BM7), ADR-BODY-03, Transit's leg scripts |
| ★ `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` `[PLACEHOLDER]` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **lowering = schema bump**, as `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT`: a saved `vel` beyond the new bound is rejected by `BDY_RESTORE_POSE_RANGE`)* | 16 | base px per tick, per axis | 1 to 16 | per-tick canvas deltas exceed `JUMP_CUT` visibility on an exterior sprite and the exterior layer tears; 16 px per tick is 960 px/s against a 640-px field | pod legs cannot be scripted fast; any value of 1 or more is already 60 px/s (whole-pixel velocity, BDY-26) | D.7, BM6, the pod-leg design (Transit, provisional) |
| ★ `DETACH_DRIFT_PX_PER_TICK` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **LOCKED at 0**; the axis and sign rule that gives a non-zero value meaning does not exist yet, so a non-zero setting is a boot lint failure, BDY-28)* | 0 (Vertical Slice VS-A may raise it only when that rule exists) | base px per tick, exterior only | 0 only now; 0 to 2 once the Vertical Slice defines the axis and sign | the cut half leaves the camera's frame quickly and reads as a launch; at 1 a hulk crosses a 640-px field in about 10.7 s; the interior never sees it (BDY-2) | none at 0: detached hulls sit still ("adrift" is membership, not motion) | D.5 `vel_new`, the `JOLT_PX` trigger list (events only), a schema bump if a slower drift is wanted |
| ★ `SWEEP_COST_LOG_MS` `[PLACEHOLDER]` | 0.05 (the per-actor figure of PCI:1078, cited) | milliseconds | 0.01 to 0.5 | a regression slips past the log | log spam | D.12, the perf gate, `STEP_BUDGET_MS`; logging only, changes no behaviour |
*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **the `heading_q` row is moved out of this table**, because a locked value is not a knob; it is listed under "Locked invariants" below. The `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` "alarm threshold" row (0 to 10 per minute in a playtest) is **replaced by a QA gate, not a knob**: BDY-24 and MSA-22 say no system ever produces the overlap (Assembly refuses or defers, and BM4 refuses as the apply-time net, so no cut ever lands a box in a cap *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)*), so a non-zero playtest rate is a defect, never an allowance.)*

**QA gate (not a knob).** `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` = 0, `BDY_TRAPPED_ACTOR` = 0 and `BDY_REBASE_ACTOR_IN_CAP` = 0 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6)* at the end of every clean-build scenario in CI (the scenario manifest of AC-BDY-62); any non-zero reading fails the build. Interacts with BDY-16, BDY-24 and the debug overlay.

**Locked invariants (not tunable; changing one is a schema bump or a registry edit, never a local tweak).** `heading_q` = 0 — quantised heading; restore fails with `BDY_RESTORE_HEADING_UNSUPPORTED` otherwise; PIPE C2, Layout `rot_q` (`LAYOUT_ROT_UNSUPPORTED`); a rotating human-built body is never rendered inside (BDY-26). `DETACH_DRIFT_PX_PER_TICK` = 0 until the Vertical Slice defines its axis and sign (the knob row above). The foot origin is the box's top-centre (BDY-14). `sub` is preserved by a restore (BDY-31).

*Implied-by-the-text check:* D.2 uses `FOOT_BOX_W`, `FOOT_BOX_H`; D.7 uses `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT`, `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK`; D.5 and BDY-28 use `DETACH_DRIFT_PX_PER_TICK`; D.12 uses `MAX_ACTORS`, `SWEEP_COST_LOG_MS`; BDY-26 and BDY-31 use `heading_q` (a locked invariant, not a knob); BDY-16 uses the QA gate (not a knob). No other Body constant appears in the text. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: BDY-16's probe length is the registry's `TILE_PX`, not a new constant.)* The player-facing feel knobs (walk and run speed, the run ramp, carry speed, deceleration, the camera's follow and jolt) are rows in Part B, not Body's. **There is deliberately no knob for ship inertia, impulse, knockback, stagger-from-motion, speed buff or traversal unlock** (BDY-28); adding one would breach P2 and the anti-combat anti-pillar.

### Part B. Constants Body consumes (not this system's: owner and registry name only)

| Constant | Owner and source | What Body does with it | What breaks in Body if the owner moves it | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V_WALK`, `V_RUN`, `V_CARRY` | Player Controller; registry entries of the same names | integrates the whole-px speed the Controller resolves as `v_eff`; never restated as a Body value | `v_eff` above the Controller's ceiling of 3 is clamped (`BDY_VEFF_INVALID`); a faster ceiling changes the swept-box maximum (D.12) and the 12-px catch-up bound (BDY-32) | D.1, D.12, PCI D.4, PIPE camera predicates |
| the `UNIT` table | Player Controller, PCI D.3 (not a separate registry entry) | per-axis scale in 1/256 px (256 cardinal, 181 diagonal) | a change moves every cadence fixture and the 256-tick exactness of D.1 | D.1, D.14, `ODO_W` |
| `ODO_W` | Player Controller, PCI:491 (unregistered) | odometer weight per move octant | footstep cadence in the Presenter (PCI D.11), not Body's behaviour | D.1 odometer |
| `RUN_RAMP_TICKS` | Player Controller, PCI:105 (unregistered) | none directly: reaches Body as `v_eff` | none in Body; ramp timing is the Controller's | D.1, PCI D.4 |
| `STOP_DECEL_TICKS` | Player Controller, PCI:105 and PCI D.5 (unregistered; MVP default 0) | the provisional ladder sub-case of D.14 only | at a non-zero value D.14's sub-case becomes live (BDY-12, CP-8) | D.14 |
| `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` | art bible; registry entry | the 24-px collision strip every wall contributes through Layout's rectangles; read in D.3 | the first-legal-row corollary (`y_0 + 24`) and every wall fixture move; an art-bible change is a re-run of D.3 | D.3, PIPE D.12, `LY D.5` |
| `TILE_PX` | Pipeline; registry entry | px-to-tile conversions (FLOOR division) in the helper `floor_div` | broadphase cell alignment and the 24-grid fixtures | BDY-35 |
| `TICK_RATE` | kernel; registry entry | the tick Body runs on; speeds are per tick | px-per-second readings of `V_WALK` and `vel` change; no Body rule | D.7 |
| `MAX_BODIES` | Layout; registry entry (allocation cap) | none: Body has no section or body cap; a pose row exists per live body | a lower cap bounds `BODY` dirty keys (D.13) | D.13, `body_count()` |
| `MAX_FOOTPRINT_RECTS_PER_BODY` | Layout; registry entry | the worst-case rectangle count the cited sweep budget is stated against | the PCI:1078 performance claim is re-measured | D.12 |
| `BROADPHASE_CELL_PX` | Layout, LY:228 (unregistered; derived, never persisted) | bounds the broadphase cells one swept box touches (at most 4) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: requires `BROADPHASE_CELL_PX >= FOOT_BOX_W + 3`)* | the 4-cell bound of D.12 changes | D.12 |
| `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` | kernel; registry entry | the per-block per-tick key ceiling above which the kernel reports `ALL` | the 8-to-16-times headroom of D.13 | D.13 |
| `STEP_BUDGET_MS` | kernel; registry entry (logging threshold) | the logging threshold only; the shares of D.12 are stated against the kernel's 2 ms P3 target and `FRAME_SIM_BUDGET_MS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | none directly | D.12 |
| `FRAME_SIM_BUDGET_MS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | kernel; registry entry (4.0 for a one-tick frame, 6.0 for a frame that runs `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` ticks; advisory) | the denominator of the catch-up worst-frame share in D.12 | the quoted catch-up share (27 % at the cited figure) | D.12, `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` |
| `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` | kernel; registry entry | the catch-up bound of BDY-32 | the 12-px catch-up figure | BDY-32 |
| per-actor sweep budget | Player Controller, PCI:1078 (a performance criterion, not a registry entry) | cited as the budget D.12 is checked against | the spike S1 threshold | D.12, `SWEEP_COST_LOG_MS` |
| `JOLT_PX` | Pipeline; registry entry (default 0, max 2; reduced motion forces 0) | Body's events feed its trigger list; Body never reads it back | none in Body; the felt result of a weld or cut is the Pipeline's | `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT`, `BODY_HULL_SEPARATION` |
| `LAG_CUT` | Pipeline; registry entry | none directly: a camera predicate that a transfer's displacement may trigger | none for a weld or detach (a rebase keeps the lag, PIPE C5); a lower cut makes a larger share of pod hops and clamped transfers camera cuts *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | PIPE C5 |
| `JUMP_CUT` | Pipeline, PIPE:501 (unregistered) | none directly: the catch-up and transfer displacement bound is checked against it | the 12-px catch-up bound versus the cut | BDY-32 |
| `REBASE_BLOCKS` | Save/Load; registry entry | Body leaves the list once BDY-31 passes (block-level idempotence) | Body's entry would re-open the restore-idempotence debt | BDY-31, SL-15 item 10 |
| `BODIES_LIVE_MAX` `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Lighting; registry entry awaiting its owner | none: Body does not confirm it | none in Body | the lit-in-view mask bits (LO) |
| `MAX_SECTIONS_PER_BODY` | Layout and Assembly (Layout Open Question 8; no value yet) | none: Body sets no section cap | none in Body | `can_detach` `CAPACITY` (Layout) |
| `layout_dirty_key_encoding` KIND values (SEC 1, BODY 2, SEAM 3) | Layout; registry entry | Body reuses `BODY` 2 and adds `ACT` 4 in its own block namespace | a Layout renumbering is a no-op for Body (keys are namespaced by block) | D.13 |

**Dependency check:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Part A has 7 knob rows (the `heading_q` row became a locked invariant and the alarm row a QA gate, above) and Part B 23 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: `FRAME_SIM_BUDGET_MS` added)*, 30 in all; every Part A value traces to a formula (D.2, D.5, D.7, D.12) or a rule (BDY-26, BDY-28), and every Part B row names the owner that decides it. The coupled ranges are stated where the constants meet: `FOOT_BOX_W` 8 to 16 and `FOOT_BOX_H` 4 to 8 follow Layout's 16-by-8 passage extents (`LY D.5`) unless `LY D.5` changes in the same edit; `BROADPHASE_CELL_PX >= FOOT_BOX_W + 3` (a Part B row, Layout's, 48); `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT` and `VEL_MAX_PX_PER_TICK` lower only with a schema bump.

## Visual/Audio Requirements

*Category note: Visual/Audio is optional for a Core system. It is written as a real note because three things are owed here: the contact-row risk that BDY-14 and D.3 both defer to this section, the invariance Body promises the Pipeline, and the felt result Body asks of the exterior events. Body has no sprite, no VFX, no sound asset and no colour of its own; every cue below is rendered by another GDD.*

**Body's visual result is absence of motion.** Inside the loaded body nothing tilts, scrolls, shakes, slides or rotates because the ship moves, because a wreck is welded, because a section is cut or because a pod leg runs (BDY-2, BDY-26, BDY-28; PIPE C2). The interior is drawn in the avatar's body frame at the canvas origin and the Pipeline owns that composition; Body's contribution is that the integers it publishes are invariant under pose (D.9), so there is nothing for the Presenter to counter-animate.

| Event | Visual feedback (what is seen, and whose GDD draws it) | Audio feedback (what is heard, and whose GDD owns it) | Priority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Walking into a wall footprint | The sprite stops on the exact pixel; the contact row is the one set out in the stand-off note below. Drawn by the Controller's Presenter (frame index from `odometer`, PCI D.11) and the Pipeline's Y-sort (PIPE D.12). No Body-drawn cue | The Controller's footstep cadence stops (cadence is the Presenter's); no Body sound | MVP |
| Sliding along a wall (x refused, y free) | Continuous motion along the free axis at the free-run diagonal cadence; no stutter, no snag. Presenter-drawn | none of Body's | MVP |
| Weld (BM3, `BODY_TRANSFER{weld}`, `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT`) | **Nothing happens to the floor.** The Pipeline REBASES the camera by `frame_delta_px` (PIPE C5), so the avatar's screen position is unchanged; only where the camera bounds clamp is it a clean cut *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The seam bead is Layout's and the art bible's (3.5 hero #3, 6.1); the hatch state is Interaction's | The hull, not the floor: a structural contact heard through the ship. The event `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT` is an input to Adaptive Audio & Tells (Designed, `adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`) and to the camera's `JOLT_PX` list (default 0, max 2, reduced motion forces 0) | MVP (event), Vertical Slice (audio asset) |
| Cut (BM4, `BODY_TRANSFER{detach}`, `BODY_HULL_SEPARATION`) | Nothing under the crew moves. The other half is a separate exterior-layer sprite; the cap plate and the gap are Layout's and the Pipeline's. A crewmate on the other half simply stands on the other body | Structure-borne: groan, release, then the quiet of a smaller ship (Adaptive Audio & Tells, Designed). `BODY_HULL_SEPARATION` is its trigger | MVP (event), Vertical Slice (audio asset) |
| Pod hop (BM5, `BODY_TRANSFER{pod}`; Web Map & Transit is Designed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*) | The camera is pod-local, the interior is still, the exterior layer slides at constant velocity (art bible 2.5); the arrival is a cut (PIPE C5) | Transit's and Adaptive Audio's | Provisional |
| Restore | Each actor is on the same pixel it was saved on; `pos_prev == pos_curr`, so the first frame has no interpolation artefact | none | MVP |
| Overlap defect (BDY-16) | Debug overlay only: the `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP` count, and a NONE section for the gap defect (no exempt step produces one). The debug tint must not use a flesh tone or violet (art bible 4.4: the sanity palette is reserved for sanity leaks); use a Hull Grey or text-only cue (art bible 4.1) | none | Debug |

**The contact-row risk, stated for the art owner (the sentence BDY-14 and D.3 cite).** Because the foot origin is the box's top-centre, a body walking north into a wall stops with its box top on the footprint's south edge (`pos.y = y_0 + 24`, `y_foot` equal to the footprint's south edge row, so the Pipeline's Y-sort draws the sprite over the wall face, PIPE:543), and a body walking south into a wall stops with its box bottom flush against the footprint's north edge (`pos.y = y_1 - 8`). The north-south stand-off therefore differs: 24 rows south of the footprint's north edge on one side and 8 rows north of the next footprint's north edge on the other (D.3). The sprite's contact row (the bottom of the boots) must sit on the box's NORTH edge for the first case to read as standing on the floor and not inside the wall; this is a constraint on the character sprite sheet (art bible 5.7), not a collision parameter. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Definition (the term AC-BDY-73 tests): the *contact row* is the lowest opaque pixel row of a character frame, measured separately for the idle frame and for every walk-cycle frame, in sprite-local rows.** The asset spec (`/asset-spec`) must carry the requirement that the contact row of every idle and walk frame sits on the sprite's origin row, so that with the Presenter placing the origin row at the integer foot row `p.y` (BDY-33 (e)), the contact row's screen row equals `y_foot` at rest; the requirement is added to the character asset spec as a hard constraint (not a style note). AC-BDY-73 is first run on a **greybox** (a placeholder character whose lowest opaque row is the origin row, built from the asset-spec dimensions) **before the Presenter story opens**, so a wrong anchor convention is found with no final art in the loop; the final art re-runs the same screenshots. If the playtest shows the boots reading as buried, the fix is a sprite offset in the Presenter, never a change to the box (BDY-14, a registry edit with a re-run of the Layout and Controller fixtures). Evidence: the screenshot protocol of AC-BDY-73 captures both poses at ladder step 1 and at `k_auto + 2` with a 1-px grid overlay, retained in `production/qa/evidence/` and signed off by the art owner.

**Art bible sections that apply (by number):** 3.3 environment geometry (the orthogonal, grid-bound human hull Body's rectangles describe; the web is never a Body fact); 3.5 hero shape 3, the weld seam (Body adds no visual to the seam; it only guarantees nobody is moved when the seam appears); 4.1 Hull Grey (the one colour honest enough to stand for a debug cue); 4.4 the sanity palette (flesh and violet are forbidden for any Body-originated cue); 5.2 stiff-weighted pose (no overshoot, no squash: a pixel-exact stop is consistent with it); 5.6 facings and the per-facing chest point (Body's foot origin is the shadow and occluder origin, the lamp's beam origin is the chest point the Controller publishes); 2.5 pod transit (the interior is still while the exterior moves). The `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` (24) strip itself belongs to the art bible's wall construction and the Layout GDD.

**Frozen at Body, owned elsewhere.** Animation (walk, run, carry) and the beam are the Controller's; zoom and the camera are the Pipeline's; light, shadow and occlusion are Lighting's; the seam, hatch and cap geometry are Layout's; sound is Adaptive Audio & Tells' (Designed). Body specifies no sound value, no colour value and no animation timing.

📌 **Asset Spec** — Visual/Audio requirements are defined. After the art bible is approved, run `/asset-spec system:ship-body-and-interior-physics` to produce per-asset visual descriptions, dimensions and generation prompts from this section.

## Game Feel

*Category note: Game Feel is optional for a Core system and this note is real, not a placeholder. Body is felt only through what it refuses to do. The feel of movement (gait, ramp, weight, cadence) is owned by the Player Controller & Input GDD; the feel of the cut and of zoom by the Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera GDD; Body owns the integration those feels stand on and the guarantee that no ship event reaches the floor.*

**What the player feels of this system.** Footing. A deck that stops them on the exact pixel the wall footprint says, lets them slide along a wall without snagging, never lurches, tilts, settles or pops, and does the same thing in the same place after a restart. The two moments that are most violent on paper, a weld and a cut, are felt as nothing at all underfoot (Player Fantasy, "The Deck Is the Ground"). If anything is felt it is the camera's `JOLT_PX` (default 0) or the hull's sound, both owned elsewhere (BDY-28).

**Feel reference.** Project Zomboid's physics-free, pixel-stepped avatar: position is a plain exact fact, the world is hard, and the floor is never the thing that hurts. Pandorum's ship-as-world: the interior is the whole world and the outside is unknowable because the deck is not. Rejected: any visibly moving deck, crew thrown by impacts, zero-g gimmicks (Player Fantasy, reference techniques).

| Feel target | What Body guarantees | Owner of the felt result | Fails if |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pixel-exact stop, no overshoot | `STOP_DECEL_TICKS` 0 at the MVP default; the sweep stops on the first refusing pixel (BDY-15, D.1) | Controller (stop and deceleration choice) | the actor slides past a wall edge or stops short of it by a pixel |
| Wall slide, no snag | x refused with y free keeps the free axis's cadence; residues follow the free run (D.1, Edge Cases) | Controller (diagonal cadence 1,1,2,1,2,1,1,2 read as weight) | an actor sticks to a wall it should slide along, or speeds up against it |
| No tile snap, no settle | positions are whole pixels in a 1-px sweep; nothing re-centres an actor after a stop or a transfer (BDY-16: no push-out) | Body | any visible pop when a door closes near a foot, after a weld, a cut or a restore |
| Weld and cut are non-events underfoot | a transfer changes `body_id` and `pos_curr` by exact arithmetic, keeps `actor_local_px`, and applies in P2 so the same tick's step starts from the shifted pixel (BDY-22, BDY-23) | Pipeline (the camera rebase, PIPE C5) | a playtester reports the ship jerked, the floor moved, or "I got knocked" |
| Pod leg is still inside | the interior never reads `vel` (BDY-2) | Pipeline and Transit (exterior layer) | any visible drift of the interior during the leg |
| Same pixel after a restore | `sub` and `last_octant` are persisted; `pos_prev := pos_curr` (BDY-31) | Save/Load (death handling), Body (position) | the actor loads a pixel away, or the first step's cadence differs from uninterrupted play (other than the stated late-resync case) |

**Input responsiveness.** Body adds no latency: the actor is stepped in the same P3 as the Controller's intent (`body.integrate` after `controller.step`), so an intent that `controller.step` resolves in a tick is integrated in that same tick with no extra tick of lag; the interpolated pose adds at most one tick of presentation delay, which is the Pipeline's one-interpolator rule (`tick_alpha`). A door that closes in P3 stops the actor in the same tick (BDY-8). Tapping gives no speed and costs at most 255/256 px per release (D.14).

**Impact moments.** Body has none of its own. Contact with a wall is silent and exact (the Controller's footstep stops); a cut, a weld and a hop are events published for the camera and Adaptive Audio (3.7). There is no knockback, stagger-from-motion, speed buff or traversal unlock to tune (BDY-28), and no knob exists for one.

**Feel acceptance criteria** are AC-BDY-73 (contact rows, retained screenshots, art sign-off) and AC-BDY-74 (the weld-and-cut feel gate, in two parts and one diagnostic). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **AC-BDY-74a (automated, BLOCKING):** no weld offer is exposed while `docked()` is false (the Assembly/Transit gate, MSA-12, twin of AC-MSA-16), paired with AC-BDY-118's forced-docked-false test build in which the avatar's screen delta is 0 px. **AC-BDY-74b (playtest, BLOCKING)** runs on the **shipped, gated build only**; its protocol file is `production/qa/evidence/body-weld-cut-playtest.md`: at least 4 playtesters, each performing 3 welds with the avatar on the absorbed body and 3 cuts; immediately afterwards ONE neutral open prompt ("What did you notice, if anything, while the weld and the cut happened?") with `JOLT_PX` at its default 0, coded independently by the creative-director and the qa-lead; PASS iff 0 of N sessions carry a floor-moved, ship-jerked or knocked code from either scorer, with both scorers' sign-off recorded in the protocol file *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the three yes-or-no questions were leading, named no scorer and confounded the floor with the camera jolt and the hull sound)*. The earlier requirement that each playtester also perform a weld staged with the docking invariant violated is **removed from the gate**: a violated-invariant weld is not reachable in the shipped build once the offer is gated, so "the build as shipped" and "expected to FAIL" described two incompatible pass states; the staged run is **AC-BDY-74c, a non-gating diagnostic** (group N) that records what a skipped gate would look like. Feel failures that would be Body's: any visible slide or pop on weld, cut or pod hop, any one-frame overlap with a wall face, any drift during a pod leg seen inside. Both are BLOCKING visual/feel evidence and are not waived at `qa.level: minimal`.

## UI Requirements

**Ship Body & Interior Physics has no UI of its own.** No HUD element, instrument, menu, prompt, tooltip or overlay reads Body, and none is added: there is no velocity readout, heading, g-force, docking-alignment or position gauge for the player (Pillar 3 and the "no meter" guard rail; BDY-28). What the player can see of the system is the world: the ship does not move under them. Surfaces that touch Body are owned by their own GDDs:

| Surface | Owner | What it shows of Body | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Interior view, camera and zoom | Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera | the avatar and crew at `pos_curr` interpolated by `tick_alpha`; the camera limit rectangle from `body.projector` | Body supplies positions and the limit rectangle; the Pipeline composes |
| Room and compartment readout | no HUD surface (HUD & Suit Layer, designed: HS-10's closed surface inventory has none) | the room is known by the world and, at Vertical Slice B, by the crew-strip location glyph (HUD & Suit Layer HS-10, designed); the room is derived from `actor_section` then Layout `room_at` | the player never sees a section id or a coordinate |
| Door and hatch prompts, "will not close on a crewmate" refusal | Interaction System | the refusal reason, derived from Body's `foot_boxes(body_id)` | wording is Interaction's |
| Cut refusal while someone stands in the hatch passage | Modular Ship Assembly (designed, `modular-ship-assembly.md`) | Assembly's reason code `MSA.CUT_BLOCKED` (MSA-21), derived from `actors_overlapping` | Body supplies the read only |
| Debug overlay (developer builds only) | Debug Overlay (designed, `debug-overlay-true-values.md`; Body is one of its 15 expected MVP channels, AC-DO-69) | the diagnostics of 3.7, rendered in text with no flesh tone and no violet: `PC_FOOTPRINT_OVERLAP`, `diag.actor_in_footprint`, `BODY_TRANSFER{weld \| detach \| pod}` counts, `BDY_ORPHAN_ACTOR`, `BDY_OCTANT_INVALID`, `BDY_VEFF_INVALID`, `BDY_SEAT_UNBOUND`, `BDY_WORLD_LIMIT`, `BDY_TRANSFER_NO_SECTION`, `BDY_PLAN_MISMATCH`, `BDY_TRAPPED_ACTOR` and the list returned by `trapped_actors()` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, per-actor sweep cost, `blocked` per actor | read-only: a write attempt never alters `body.state` (AC-BDY-122); never shipped to players |

There is no accessibility surface of Body's own: it has no audio-only or colour-only cue, no timed input and no flashing element. Reduced motion is already satisfied by construction (the interior never moves from ship motion) and `JOLT_PX` is forced to 0 by the Pipeline's setting. No UX Flag is raised for this system and no `/ux-design` spec is required; if the Debug Overlay GDD adds a Body panel it must use the exact diagnostic names above.

Citation form: `KRN` = command-simulation-layer.md, `LY` = ship-section-and-layout-data-model.md, `PCI` = player-controller-input.md, `PIPE` = pixel-perfect-render-pipeline-camera.md, `EM` = emitter-state-and-light-exposure-query.md, `LO` = 2d-lighting-and-occlusion.md, `SL` = save-load-and-versioned-format.md, `SE` = suit-and-equipment.md, `IA` = interaction-system.md, `LSG` = life-support-graph.md, `IDX` = systems-index.md, `REG` = design/registry/entities.yaml (cited by registry name, because line numbers drift as sibling stages append). Where a constant is not a separate registry entry it is cited to the GDD that owns it.
