The allow-list is empty: a token that is needed is added to the file with a reason in review, never silently.
### J. Cross-system (one criterion per neighbour; provisional where the neighbour is undesigned)

- **AC-BDY-65a (auto, integration) Emitter reads [4.2, EM-21, EM-30].** GIVEN an Emitter stub, WHEN it reads Body, THEN it anchors the ACTOR emitter on `pos_curr` and partitions subjects by `body_of_actor`; `emitter.place` runs after `body.integrate` (the order is AC-BDY-2).
- **AC-BDY-65b (auto, integration) Suit reads [4.2, SE-3, BDY-29].** GIVEN a Suit stub and a Layout stub that REJECTS body-frame pixels, WHEN Suit asks for the occupant's room, THEN it calls `room_at(section_id, actor_local_px)` with SECTION-LOCAL pixels and the lookup succeeds; an origin in an exterior gap (a deliberate-defect fixture) yields NONE, which Body reports and Suit and the Graph must treat as exterior exposure, never as the last room (the routed must-repeat, 8.3; Suit's hold applies only to the IN_POD abstraction).
- **AC-BDY-65c (auto, integration) Interaction reads [4.2, IA-24].** GIVEN an Interaction stub, WHEN it validates a door close, THEN it gathers `foot_boxes(body_id)` unfiltered (every actor on the body, including a SEAT whose Controller row is unbound; 8.3), ascending by `actor_id`.
- **AC-BDY-65d (auto, integration) Lighting reads [4.2, LO-10].** GIVEN a Lighting stub, WHEN it reads Body, THEN it takes `pos_curr` floored as the shadow and occluder origin (the foot, not the chest) and the per-body canvas offset; the lamp beam origin is the Controller's chest point and Body publishes none.
- **AC-BDY-116 (auto, integration) Doors see every box [BDY-18].** GIVEN a Layout stub implementing `leaf_would_overlap`, three actors including a `SEAT` row whose Controller row is unbound, WHEN a player-origin close is validated with `foot_boxes(body_id)`, THEN the unbound seat's box is in the list (ascending ids) and a close over it is rejected.
- **AC-BDY-121 (auto, integration) One consistent rectangle set per tick [Edge Cases].** GIVEN a door leaf closing in P3 and a system-origin cut applied in P2 of the same tick, WHEN `body.integrate` sweeps, THEN the rectangle set it reads is the post-cut, post-leaf set (no half-applied state), and the result equals the run in which the two events happen on separate ticks in the same order.
- **AC-BDY-118 (auto, integration with the Pipeline) Weld invisible [BDY-22, PIPE C5].** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier criterion asserted that a camera cut fires on a weld, which a correct Pipeline does not do.)* GIVEN the Pipeline with camera bounds that do not clamp and the avatar on the absorbed body, WHEN the weld applies, THEN the `BODY_TRANSFER{weld}` payload carries `frame_delta_px == +frame_shift_px`, the Pipeline REBASES by it (C5: it shifts `f` and its history and keeps the lag and `L`; registry `camera_cut_predicates`: a weld or detach transfer that carries `frame_delta_px` is a rebase, not a cut), the avatar's screen-space position on the weld tick equals the previous tick's (delta 0 px) and no camera cut fires (`diag.cut_reason` stays empty); WHERE the new body's camera bounds clamp the rebased target the result is a cut, never a pan. The cut variant (`BODY_TRANSFER{detach}` with `frame_delta_px == -frame_shift_px`) asserts the same. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Forced docked-false variant (test build only; the pairing of AC-BDY-74a):** GIVEN a test-build override that applies the weld with `docked` false (`delta_abs` non-zero, e.g. `(-24, 0)`), the avatar on the absorbed body and the camera bounds not clamping, WHEN the weld applies, THEN the avatar's screen-space position on the weld tick equals the previous tick's (delta **0 px**), the interior actor bytes equal the run with `docked` true (BDY-2), and the exterior jump of the absorbed hull is `delta_abs` and is NOT asserted away — it is recorded, because the only guard of that jump is the offer gate (AC-BDY-74a), not the camera rebase.
- **AC-BDY-92 (auto, integration with a scene harness) Projector behaviour [BDY-33].** GIVEN two live bodies (the avatar on A, B at `world (600, 240)`), WHEN the projector runs, THEN there is exactly one root `Node2D` per live `body_id`, A's root position is `(0, 0)` and B's equals `canvas_offset(B)`, every root rotation is 0, no `Camera2D` is a descendant of any body root (the primary variant has no `Camera2D` in the world tree at all, ADR-0008 section 6.2), the camera-limits rectangle the projector publishes to the Pipeline solver equals the avatar body's bounding rectangle and is republished on weld, on detach and on load, and no `Camera2D.limit_*` property is written by `body.projector` *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0009 sync: OQ-C)*, physics interpolation is disabled on every presentation node, and after a weld the absorbed body's root no longer exists (node count equals the pre-weld count minus one root, no orphan).
- **AC-BDY-92b (auto, integration with a scene harness) Y-sort layer and Presenter parenting [BDY-33 (d) to (f)].** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* GIVEN the AC-BDY-92 scene and a Presenter registered for the avatar with `attach_presenter`, WHEN a weld (avatar on the absorbed body), a cut (avatar on the new body) and a pod hop each apply and the P5 batch of that tick carries the `BODY_TRANSFER`, THEN in the same tick and before the next frame renders the Presenter's parent is the destination body's actor/props layer, that layer has `y_sort_enabled` true and is a sibling of (not inside) the section-node layer under the root, the Presenter's local position equals the floored `(p.x, p.y)` of the destination frame (not the engine's global-preserving reparent), and each body root has exactly one such layer; AND GIVEN an actor one pixel south and one pixel north of a wall footprint edge before, on and after each of the three transfers, THEN the draw order is unchanged and equals PIPE D.12's (actor over the face when south; behind it when north; equal keys resolved by layer order).
- **AC-BDY-84 (auto, integration with the real Perception Filter and Sanity registrations; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was "provisional — Perception Filter undesigned")* the Filter and Sanity GDDs are Designed and cite BDY-30) A false opening is falsifiable by the body [BDY-30].** GIVEN a Layout stub with a closed leaf rectangle and a presentation stub that draws it open, WHEN the actor walks into it, THEN `pos_curr` stops at the leaf's near edge, `blocked` is true for the Presenter, and the `solid_in` stub's call log shows the leaf in the returned set; GIVEN a stub class (c) rig, THEN it is positioned from `pos_curr` with no per-player offset.
- **AC-BDY-123 (auto, integration with the real Sanity and Perception Filter registrations) Sanity never moves anyone [BDY-30].** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was "provisional — Sanity and Perception Filter undesigned"; both GDDs are Designed and repeat BDY-30.)* GIVEN the Sanity and Perception Filter registrations (a stub only for any hook whose code is not yet in the test build, and then named) run at sanity 100 and sanity 0 with identical commands, THEN `body.state` bytes are identical at every tick, and (lint) no Sanity or Filter code path writes `body.state` or calls a Body mutator.
- **AC-BDY-122 (auto) Diagnostics are read-only [BDY-34].** GIVEN a Debug Overlay stub reading the diagnostics channel, THEN reads succeed and a write attempt does not alter `body.state` bytes.
- **AC-BDY-74a (auto, integration: Modular Ship Assembly / Web Map & Transit + Body; BLOCKING) No weld offer while `docked()` is false [BDY-27, Game Feel].** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: moved here from group L, because it is an automated integration criterion and not visual evidence; neighbour-owned, see the classification after harness rule (6))* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the first half of the split of AC-BDY-74; the earlier single criterion asked for "0 of N yes" on a weld staged with the docking invariant violated and called that "the build as shipped", then said the same weld "is expected to FAIL" if the offer was not yet gated — two incompatible pass states that depended on another GDD's gating and on a Pipeline cut that does not hide the exterior hull jump, so it could not be closed.)* GIVEN the real Assembly `weld_offer` and Body's `docked(a, b, frame_shift_px)`, WHEN the pair has `world(b) - world(a) != frame_shift_px` (for example `(720, 384)` against `(696, 384)`, or equal poses with `vel(b) != vel(a)`) and the seat holds the welder, is lit and in reach, THEN `weld_offer` is false, no prompt exists and `begin_weld` is not accepted; WHEN `docked` is true, THEN the offer exists. This is the Body-side twin of AC-MSA-16 and uses the same fixtures; it passes iff both do. It is paired with the forced-docked-false weld of AC-BDY-118 (avatar screen delta 0 px) and gates the build: no weld offer is exposed while `docked()` is false. **BLOCKING; not waived at `qa.level: minimal`.**

### K. Performance and spikes (one-off spikes record a number; the recurring test fails when exceeded)

- **AC-BDY-68 (spike S1) Sweep cost [D.12, PCI:1078].** GIVEN a synthetic body of `MAX_FOOTPRINT_RECTS_PER_BODY` (2,000) rectangles with a 16-rectangle worst-case cluster (K = 16) around the actor's swept box, committed as `tests/fixtures/body/sweep_worst_case.tres` (or an equivalent data file), WHEN 1,000 ticks run for one actor, THEN P99 per-actor step cost is at most the cited 0.05 ms at `K = 16`; with 4 actors the P99 `body.integrate` hook total is at most the cited 0.2 ms, and with all 8 actors moving (`MAX_ACTORS`) at most 0.4 ms (20 % of the kernel's 2 ms P3 target), all cited from PCI:1078 and not new figures; `c_q`, `c_r` and `c_commit` are recorded, and the spike records whether the cited 0.05 ms includes the `solid_in` call. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The spike measures the **whole `body.integrate` hook** on the worst-case fixtures of D.12 (W1 eight actors all moving against the K = 16 cluster; W2 eight actors all EXEMPT at the abutting-pair cap, all pressing east, refused at the first sub-step; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* W2b eight actors admitting six sub-steps each, the most expensive exempt tick; W2c the mixed fixture; W3 eight bodies in motion plus eight idle actors; W4 Suit's per-seat `actor_section` reads; W5 the weld-tick probe), with the analytic clamp as the measured path and the 1-px stepper only as the oracle, and the **4x catch-up worst frame** (four hooks back to back, W2b and W2c); it records `c_q`, `c_r`, `c_ov`, `c_sec`, `c_commit` and the per-fixture P99 as numbers (a spike records, it does not gate on the unmeasured `c_r` of D.12). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **S1 is a hard prerequisite of closing the Body hook story**: the numbers it records replace the cited figures, and every wall-clock threshold in D.12 and AC-BDY-117 is *pending spike measurement on RM-DESKTOP / RM-DECK* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)* (S1 is run on both machines; the machines, the release-export profile and the headroom rule are stated once in D.12; Open Question 37 is closed).
- **AC-BDY-117 (perf; hard gate in CI = deterministic counts; wall-clock = separate nightly) Recurring sweep-cost test [D.12].** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the project's gdUnit4 CI runs a headless debug build on shared runners, which cannot host a release-export wall-clock P99 gate; the criterion is split.)* **(a) CI hard gate, BLOCKING, count-based and deterministic** (`tests/performance/body/`): GIVEN the worst-case fixtures W1, W2 and W3 of D.12 (the files of AC-BDY-68), WHEN 1,000 ticks run on the headless debug build, THEN per tick (i) the number of box-versus-rectangle tests is at most `3 * K` per moving actor for the analytic clamp (and at most `7 * K` for the oracle in the equivalence test only), and, for an EXEMPT actor, at most `K` rectangle tests plus `n_sub * |E|` overlap evaluations with `n_sub <= 6` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: `3 * K` does not describe the exempt path)*, (ii) the number of `solid_in_into` / `solid_in` queries is at most 1 per moving actor and exactly 0 for a stationary actor, (iii) the number of `section_at` calls is 0 from the hook on every tick with no exempt actor and at most 6 per exempt actor-tick, plus exactly one per seat per tick from Suit (W4) and at most `n_flagged * 192` on a detection-event tick (W5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and (iv) the allocation proxy of AC-BDY-125 holds once the technical-director adopts it (Open Question 15; until then it is recorded, group N); the counters are incremented by instrumented stubs (`collide` and `section_at` wrappers), never by timing, so the result is identical on any runner. **(b) Nightly benchmark, not a CI gate** (`tests/performance/body/nightly/`): the same fixtures, 1,000 ticks, on each of the two reference machines, RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK, with the RELEASE export, warm-up and headroom rule of R12 (D.12) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*, P99 per hook for W1, W2, W3 and for `4 * hook(W2)` (the catch-up worst frame); it FAILS when any P99 exceeds the spike-S1 baseline on that machine by more than **+25 %** and it records the numbers; *pending spike measurement on RM-DESKTOP / RM-DECK*, (b) records only. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Calibration-ratio form (the performance-analyst's proposal):** to give a shared runner a time gate that does not depend on its absolute speed, the merge-blocking form of (b) would be a RATIO gate: each run first times a fixed pure-integer calibration loop of committed length in the same process, and the gate compares `P99 hook time / calibration time` with the S1 baseline ratio, failing above baseline + 25 %. It is adopted only together with the threshold the spike S1 baseline supplies and is RECORD-ONLY until then, like (b); the machines are RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK (R12, D.12). A (b) failure opens a bug and, until the ratio form is adopted, never blocks a merge on a shared runner.
- **AC-BDY-69 (spike S3) Weld projection [BDY-33, ADR-BODY-03].** GIVEN three Layout bodies (home 5 compartments, derelict A 4, derelict B 5, the MVP slice) with the avatar on derelict A, A absorbed into home while A has `vel (3, 0)` and home has `vel (0, 0)`, WHEN the weld runs, THEN the P99 projector frame time over 600 frames spanning the weld, the allocations per weld and the node count before and after (no orphaned roots) are recorded, and the Y-sort check passes: for an actor one pixel south and one pixel north of a wall footprint edge, the draw order on the tick before, of and after the weld is unchanged (PIPE:543); the reparent-versus-rebuild choice is made from the decision table in ADR-BODY-03. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Pass threshold (the earlier text recorded numbers and had none):** the **weld frame** (the frame in which the P2 weld applies and the projector reparents or rebuilds) must complete its projector work in at most **8 ms** (about half of a 16.7 ms frame at 60 Hz) at P99 over 100 repeated welds on each of RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK (release export, R12, D.12) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*, no frame of the 600-frame window may exceed that machine's P99 frame-time ceiling (16.6 ms RM-DESKTOP, 33.3 ms RM-DECK), the node count after the weld equals the pre-weld count minus one root, and the Y-sort check holds (AC-BDY-92b); the 8 ms figure is a working threshold, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R21; Open Question 37, closed), *pending spike measurement on RM-DESKTOP / RM-DECK* with the release export of R12 and not on a shared CI runner (AC-BDY-117 (b)).
- **AC-BDY-70 (spike S4) Snapshot byte identity [ADR-BODY-02].** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* GIVEN the kernel's `canonical_encode` (D.12) over `snapshot()`, WHEN snapshot, restore and snapshot repeat, THEN the bytes are identical, including a synthetic `odometer` of 2^53 + 7 through Save/Load's binary payload (SL-2), and equal the committed golden file of AC-BDY-110 on the pinned build and on the build before it (`var_to_bytes` is not used for any Body byte, and the spike records whether it would have differed, as a finding only).
- **AC-BDY-71 (spike S5) Tick alignment [BDY-32, ADR-BODY-04].** GIVEN displays at 30, 60 and 144 Hz and a 10-minute run walking east at `V_WALK`, WHEN ticks and frames are counted, THEN (a) ticks executed equals `floor(elapsed_s * TICK_RATE)` to within 1 at the end (none lost, none doubled); (b) for every rendered frame the Presenter's integer row and column equal `floor(prev + (curr - prev) * tick_alpha)` of the last two committed positions; (c) the per-frame integer displacement is `floor(d)` or `ceil(d)` of the ideal `d = V_WALK * TICK_RATE / refresh_hz` on every frame, that is, it deviates from `d` by strictly less than 1 px *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the bound is derived, not proposed. The presented row is `p = floor(prev + (curr - prev) * tick_alpha)` (PIPE D.9); for a steady walk the un-floored interpolated position advances by exactly `d` per frame, and the difference of two floors of values `d` apart is `floor(d)` or `ceil(d)`, so the deviation is below 1 px. Hence at 30 Hz `d` = 4 and every frame is exactly 4; at 60 Hz `d` = 2 and every frame is exactly 2; at 144 Hz `d` = 0.8333 and every frame is 0 or 1. This holds when the frame intervals are exact, so (c) is asserted on a DETERMINISTIC clock harness with fake frame deltas at 30, 60 and 144 Hz; the real-display run records the observed deviation including frame-time jitter as a finding and does not gate on it. The Pipeline owner still confirms the harness, not the bound.)*
- **AC-BDY-72 (spike S2) Engine semantics [BDY-35].** GIVEN the integer-semantics fixtures of AC-BDY-19 and the float32 canvas-offset exactness at `canvas_offset` up to 2^23, WHEN run on the pinned build (`4.7.2.stable.mono`, `VERSION.md`), THEN they hold, or the failure is a recorded finding that replaces the expression with an explicit sign-split form or lowers `WORLD_COORD_LIMIT`.

Files `production/qa/evidence/body-contact-north-k1.png`, `body-contact-north-kmax.png`, `body-contact-south-k1.png`, `body-contact-south-kmax.png`; the art owner's sign-off is recorded in `production/qa/evidence/body-contact-signoff.md`.

One pass state, no dependence on an ungated offer. The objective companions are AC-BDY-118 and AC-BDY-74a. **BLOCKING; not waived at `qa.level: minimal`.**

- **AC-BDY-131 (manual, retained screenshots; BLOCKING) Run-and-observe evidence for the player-observable stories [coding standards, Visual/Audio Requirements].** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the project standard requires a launched build and a retained screenshot, which a parse check or a headless test does not give; AC-BDY-73 covers the contact rows only.)* GIVEN a launched build of the MVP-slice home ship, WHEN each of the following is played and captured, THEN the files exist in `production/qa/evidence/` when the story closes, each with a one-line observation in `production/qa/evidence/body-run-and-observe.md` (the pixel read off a 1-px grid overlay where one applies): the wall stop (`body-wall-stop.png`: the avatar flush against a wall footprint, with the frame before and the frame after the stop); the door leaf closing in the same tick (`body-door-leaf-stop.png`: the avatar stopped at the leaf); the restore (`body-restore-before.png` and `body-restore-after.png`: the same pixel and the same facing); the pod arrival (`body-pod-arrival.png`: the first frame after the hop, standing on the arrival cell). **BLOCKING; not waived at `qa.level: minimal`.**

### M. Documentation gates (review; they check this document, not product behaviour, and do not count toward the Logic or Integration story gate)

- **AC-BDY-66 (review) Wording amendments are tracked [8.3, 8.4].** GIVEN Cross-References 8.3, WHEN each row is read, THEN it carries a status in {OPEN, ANSWERED, WITHDRAWN} with an owner and a date, and the confirming run of 8.4 reports zero stale tokens in Body's own text. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: a status may carry a qualifier ("partly") and every ANSWERED row cites its evidence (file and line of the neighbour's sentence); the 8.4 confirming run of 2026-10-27 is recorded in 8.4 with its patterns, so this criterion is checked by running those patterns, not by reading.)*

### N. Deferred (Vertical Slice or awaiting an owner's decision; not MVP gates)

- **AC-BDY-64 (auto, Vertical Slice) Replicated fields [BDY-30].** GIVEN a `MIRROR` kernel, WHEN a host snapshot is delivered, THEN `pos_curr`, `body_id`, `seat`, `kind`, `odometer`, `last_octant` and `sub` are present, `blocked`, `moved`, `actor_section` and `diag` values are not, and `body.integrate` does not apply on the `MIRROR`.
- **AC-BDY-124 (auto, deferred with `DETACH_DRIFT_PX_PER_TICK` > 0) Detach drift [BDY-28].** Deferred until the Vertical Slice stage that raises the knob defines the separation axis and sign. Then: GIVEN drift 1, WHEN BM4 applies, THEN the new body's `vel` equals `vel_old` plus drift along the defined axis and the interior actor bytes are unchanged (AC-BDY-46). The default 0 is covered by AC-BDY-38.
- **AC-BDY-X1 (auto; live — CP-3 option (b) adopted, `controller.noise`, PCI PC-15) Noise reader sees this tick's `moved`.** With the divisor-1 Controller hook `controller.noise` ordered `after: [body.integrate]`, GIVEN the real hook order, WHEN an actor presses into a wall, THEN that hook reads `moved == (0, 0)` on the SAME tick. AC-BDY-53 is the Body half and stands now.
- **AC-BDY-74c (auto, test build only; NON-GATING diagnostic) Violated-invariant weld, recorded, not scored.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the violated-invariant run moved out of the gate.)* GIVEN a test-build override that applies a weld with `docked` false (`delta_abs` non-zero, e.g. `(-24, 0)` as in D.8) and, separately, a scripted playtest session on that override, THEN the run records the interior result (the avatar's screen delta is 0 px, AC-BDY-118), the exterior jump of the absorbed hull (`delta_abs`) and the playtesters' coded responses to the neutral prompt of AC-BDY-74b, as numbers in `production/qa/evidence/body-weld-cut-playtest.md`'s diagnostic appendix; there is no pass or fail, and the shipped build never reaches this state (AC-BDY-74a). Purpose: to show what a skipped offer gate would look like, not to gate on it.
- **AC-BDY-125 (perf; count-based proxy, CI-BLOCKED-ON `solid_in_into`; adoption of the Open Question 15 rule is the technical-director's) No per-tick allocation in the hook.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "zero allocations" cannot be measured from GDScript, so the criterion measures what GDScript can observe.)* GIVEN 8 bodies all with `vel != 0` and 8 actors idle, and again the W1 and W2 fixtures of D.12, THEN between ticks 100 and 1,100 (i) the delta of the engine's object-count monitor (`Performance.OBJECT_COUNT`, **[UNV]** on the pinned build; spike S2 confirms the monitor name and that `RefCounted` construction moves it) is 0, (ii) is promoted to the BLOCKING lint AC-BDY-139 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and (iii) the packed-array buffers (`scratch`, the `solid_in_into` out-buffer, the SoA tables) keep their sizes (no `resize` between ticks 100 and 1,100). A pass on (i) and (iii), together with AC-BDY-139, is the CI gate; a literal "zero allocations" claim is not made, because `PackedArray` growth is invisible to (i), and (iii) covers it.

### Gaps / untestable as written

- **G-A (closed 2026-10-27).** The Controller's noise-ordering choice (CP-3) is answered: option (b), hook `controller.noise` (PCI PC-15, D.13); its same-tick criterion AC-BDY-X1 is now live. Owner: Controller (Open Questions 5).
- **G-B.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: rewritten.)* `AC-BDY-71` (c)'s bound is now derived (floor interpolation error below 1 px) and asserted on a deterministic clock harness; the Pipeline owner confirms the harness (Open Question 17). `AC-BDY-117` (a) is a count-based CI gate that needs no threshold from hardware; (b) is a nightly benchmark that fails at +25 % over the S1 baseline on each of RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK (R12, Open Question 37, closed). `AC-BDY-69`'s weld-frame threshold (8 ms) is a working threshold (R21). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)* Both are *pending spike measurement on RM-DESKTOP / RM-DECK*, and (b) records only until S1 has run on both.
- **G-C.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: rewritten.)* Modular Ship Assembly, Web Map & Transit, the Perception Filter and Sanity are now Designed, so AC-BDY-42 to 44, 84 and 123 are integration criteria against their real registrations; what remains a stub is only code that does not exist in the test build yet, and each such criterion names the neighbour's twin criterion (AC-MSA-16 for AC-BDY-74a, AC-WM-89 for AC-BDY-127). The Boarding & Salvage extraction path stays under AC-BDY-90.
- **G-D.** Residual (ii) of BDY-16 (a box astride two exempt rectangles with neither side reaching open floor) is a defect-only path and has no pass/fail outcome by design; AC-BDY-30 asserts that it is named and labelled, not that it is safe.
- **G-E.** Float32 exactness of `canvas_offset` up to 2^23, `Dictionary` insertion order, 2D interpolation and Y-sort under reparent are **[UNV]** (not in the engine reference); they are covered only by spikes S2 to S5 (AC-BDY-69 to 72), which produce findings, not guarantees. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: plus the claims of Open Question 39 — the `Performance.OBJECT_COUNT` monitor, directory-scoped warning levels (settled: `directory_rules`, ADR-0010 section 5), `Node.reparent` position semantics; `posmod` and integer-division semantics are now probed, ADR-0010 P11.)*
- **G-F.** Frame-by-frame feel of the stop, the slide and the weld is a human judgement; AC-BDY-73 and AC-BDY-74b *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was AC-BDY-74)* turn it into retained evidence with a named sign-off but cannot be automated.

**Count.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* 138 criteria across groups A to N (131 at authoring, plus 7: AC-BDY-29b and 29c; AC-BDY-74 split into 74a, 74b and 74c, a net +2; AC-BDY-92b; AC-BDY-128 and AC-BDY-129; ids are non-contiguous by design; letter suffixes 29b, 29c, 30a, 65a to 65d, 74a to 74c, 92b and X1 are additions to the numbered set). Tag mentions as of the review (110 at authoring; +7 new automated criteria, 29b, 29c, 74a, 74c, 92b, 128 and 129, less the one auto tag of the former AC-BDY-74 that they replace, a net +6), with a few criteria carrying two tags (for example auto + lint): 116 automated, 8 lint, 8 review (AC-BDY-30, 67, 115 and 126 now backed by the grep scripts G4 to G7 of 8.4), 5 spike, 2 recurring performance (AC-BDY-117, now (a) a CI count gate and (b) a nightly benchmark) and 2 manual (AC-BDY-73 and 74b); 5 are in the deferred or non-gating group N (AC-BDY-64, 74c, 124, 125 and X1). The two BLOCKING Visual/Feel items are AC-BDY-73 and 74b; AC-BDY-74a is a BLOCKING automated gate. Every criterion carries at least one tag. (AC-BDY-65 of the draft is split into 65a to 65d.) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Re-review additions:** 10 new criteria, AC-BDY-130 to 139 (ids non-contiguous with the rest), bring the criterion count taken by grep over this section from 140 to 150 (the design-review figure of 138 above was two short of the grep count of its own time, which is corrected here) across groups A to N: 130 and 136 in group D, 132 to 135 in group E, 137 and 138 in group H, 139 (lint, BLOCKING) in group I, and 131 (manual, BLOCKING evidence) in group L. The carry intake (about 7,000 mAU per tick while a HEAVY item is carried) is Attention's and Boarding's to price against this duration. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
